Naiṣkramyabhūmi: The Stage of Renunciation

Śrāvakabhūmi · Sanskrit (Shukla 1973) ↔ English (1 rendering)
← All topicsView English only →

The third and longest sub-bhūmi of Yogasthāna I. Naiṣkramyabhūmi covers renunciation (naiṣkramya) — the practitioner's actual work after going forth: gathering the requisites (saṁbhāra) for dispassion (vairāgya), traversing the worldly and supramundane paths, and embodying the concrete shape of the renunciant's life.

id
Sanskrit
English
sbh1.3:0.1
Śrāvakabhūmi
Śrāvakabhūmi
sbh1.3:0.2
Naiṣkramyabhūmi: The Stage of Renunciation
Naiṣkramyabhūmi: The Stage of Renunciation
sbh1.3:1.0
[III.] naiṣkramyabhūmiḥ
[III.] The Stage of Renunciation
The source has naiṣkramyabhūmiḥ naiṣkramyabhūmiḥ katamā — the bhūmi name repeated, with no Roman-numeral marker. Treated here as 'name + question': the first occurrence (now :1.0) functions as the section header (as Roman-numeral I. and II. did in [sbh1.1](sbh1.1.html) and [sbh1.2](sbh1.2.html)), the second (now :1.1) as the standard X katamā opening question. The Roman numeral III. has been editorially restored in brackets — the dittographic doubling of naiṣkramyabhūmiḥ in the source likely covers a lost III. marker, since the section is the third sub-bhūmi of Yogasthāna I in clear continuity with the I./II. pattern of the preceding two.
sbh1.3:1.1
naiṣkramyabhūmiḥ katamā |
What is the stage of renunciation?
sbh1.3:1.2
āha |
Answer:
sbh1.3:1.3
yacca laukikena mārgeṇa vairāgyagamanaṁ |
the going to dispassion by the worldly path,
sbh1.3:1.4
yacca lokottareṇa mārgeṇa vairāgyagamanaṁ |
the going to dispassion by the supramundane path,
sbh1.3:1.5
yacca yaśca tayossambhāraḥ tadekatyamabhisaṁkṣipya naiṣkramyabhūmirityucyate |
and the requisites for these two — gathered into one, this is called the stage of renunciation.
sbh1.3:1.6
laukikena mārgeṇa vairāgyagamanaṁ katamat |
What is going to dispassion by the worldly path?
sbh1.3:1.7
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ kāmadhātāvaudārikadarśī bhavati |
When someone here sees grossness in the realm of desire,
sbh1.3:1.8
prathama eva sa samāpattyupapattike dhyāne vivekaje prītisukhe śāntadarśī bhavati |
and sees peacefulness in the first dhyāna — both as a meditative attainment and as a [corresponding] mode of rebirth — born of seclusion, accompanied by joy and pleasure:
samāpatti-upapattika dhyāna — the dhyāna treated under both its aspects: as a meditative attainment (samāpatti) reached in this life, and as a corresponding mode of rebirth (upapatti) into the dhyāna-heaven of the same name. This dual reference is canonical: the same term names both the practice and the cosmological-rebirth realm it leads to.
sbh1.3:1.9
sa tathādarśī tadbahulavihārī satkāyavairāgyamanuprāpnoti |
so seeing, abiding much in that, he attains dispassion toward the [desire-realm] personality,
satkāya-vairāgyam — 'dispassion toward the personality.' In context, the practitioner attains dispassion specifically toward the kāmadhātu-bound personality — the embodied-self of the desire realm — which is the precondition for transitioning into the first dhyāna. Satkāya (literally 'existing body / aggregates') is the same term that grounds satkāya-dṛṣṭi (identity-view).
sbh1.3:1.10
prathamañca dhyānaṁ samāpadyate |
and attains the first dhyāna.
sbh1.3:1.11
evaṁ sarvadhyānādūrdhvaṁ sarvāsvadharimāsu bhūmiṣvaudārikadarśī bhavati |
So, above each dhyāna, he becomes a seer of grossness in all the lower stages
sbh1.3:1.12
sarvāsu coparimāyu bhūmiṣu śāntadarśī, sa tathādarśī tadbahulavihārī samāno yāvadākiñcanyāyatanādvairāgyamanuprāpnoti |
and a seer of peacefulness in all the upper stages; so seeing, abiding much in that, he attains dispassion all the way up to the sphere of nothingness,
sbh1.3:1.13
naivasaṁjñānāsaṁjñāyatanaṁ ca samāpadyate |
And he attains the sphere of neither-perception-nor-non-perception.
sbh1.3:1.14
laukikena mārgeṇa vairāgyagamanaṁ nāstyata uttari nāto bhūyaḥ |
The going to dispassion by the worldly path goes no higher, no further than this.
sbh1.3:1.15
lokottareṇa mārgeṇa vairāgya gamanaṁ katamat |
What is the going to dispassion by the supramundane path?
sbh1.3:1.16
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ satpuruṣāṇāṁ darśī āryadharmeṣu kovidaḥ duḥkhamvā duḥkhato yathābhūtaṁ prajānāti |
When someone here, having seen good persons, learned in the noble dharmas, truly knows suffering as suffering,
sbh1.3:1.17
samudayamvā samadayataḥ |
origin as origin,
sbh1.3:1.18
nirodhamvā nirodhataḥ |
cessation as cessation,
sbh1.3:1.19
mārgamvā mārgataḥ |
path as path,
sbh1.3:1.20
śaikṣeṇa jñānadarśanena samanvāgataḥ |
endowed with the trainee’s knowing-and-vision;
sbh1.3:1.21
tataścottari mārgaṁ bhāvayaṁstraidhātukebhyo darśanabhāvanāprahātavyebhyodharmebhya ātmānaṁ visaṁyojayati vimocayatyevaṁ cāsau traidhātukasamatikrānto bhavati |
thereafter, cultivating the higher path, he disjoins and releases himself from the dharmas of the three realms — those to be abandoned by [the path of] vision and [the path of] cultivation — and thus passes beyond the three realms.
darśana-bhāvanā-prahātavyebhyo dharmebhyaḥ — 'the dharmas to be abandoned by vision and by cultivation.' Refers to the canonical two-stage path of the darśana-mārga (path of seeing, which abandons the intellectually-grasped defilements at first true insight) and the bhāvanā-mārga (path of cultivation, which gradually wears down the residual affective defilements). Together these constitute the supramundane path proper.
sbh1.3:1.22
idamucyate lokottareṇa mārgeṇa vairāgyagamanam |
This is called going to dispassion by the supramundane path.
sbh1.3:2.1
tatra sambhāraḥ katamaḥ |
What, then, are the requisites?
sbh1.3:2.2
tadyathā ātmasvaparasampat ātmasampat |
Namely: endowment-from-self, endowment-from-others,
sbh1.3:2.3
parasampat, kuśalaḥ lo dharma c chandaḥ, śīlasamvaraḥ, bhojane mātrajñatā, pūrvarātrāpararātraṁ jāgarikānuyuktatā, saṁprajānadvihāritā, kalyāṇamitratā, saddharmaśravaṇacittaṁ cintanā a na ntarāyaḥ tyāgaḥ, śramaṇālaṁkāraśca itīme dharmā laukikalokottaravairāgya gama nāya sambhāra ityucyate |
wholesome dharma-desire, restraint of virtue, [restraint of] sense-faculties, knowing the right measure in food, application to wakefulness in the early and late watches of the night, dwelling with full awareness, association with good friends, hearing-and-reflection on the true dharma, [practice] without obstacle, generosity, the ornament of the recluse — these dharmas are called the requisites for going to worldly and supramundane dispassion.
The list enumerates the thirteen saṁbhāra (requisites) that the rest of this chapter unpacks. The extracted root here skips directly from śīla-saṁvara to bhojane mātrajñatā, but the closing uddāna at 80.1 has śīlendriya- and the chapter itself devotes 32.1–35.65 to indriya-saṁvara; English therefore supplies sense-faculty restraint in brackets. Kalyāṇamitratā (association with good friends) appears in this enumeration but is folded into [parasampat](sbh1.1.html#sbh1.1:1.26) in the gotrabhūmi treatment, hence its absence from the closing verse.
sbh1.3:2.4
tatra yā cātmasampat, parasampat kuśalaśca dharmacha ccha nda eṣāṁ pūrvavadvibhāgo veditavyaḥ |
Of these, endowment-from-self, endowment-from-others, and wholesome dharma-desire — their division is to be understood as before,
sbh1.3:2.5
yaduktaṁ nihīne bījasamudāgamapratyaye |
namely as stated [in the gotrabhūmi] regarding the lesser supporting condition for the perfecting of the seed.
Cross-reference back to [sbh1.1:10.20–21](sbh1.1.html#sbh1.1:10.20), the gotrabhūmi treatment of ātmasampat, parasampat, and kuśala-dharma-cchanda under the rubric of the lesser supporting condition for the perfecting of the lineage-seed. The avatārabhūmi does not re-define these three; the present chapter inherits them and proceeds directly to the fourth requisite (śīla-saṁvara) at 2.6.
sbh1.3:2.6
tatra śīlasamvaraḥ katamaḥ |
What is restraint of virtue?
sbh1.3:2.7
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ śīlavān viharati yāvat samādāya śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣu |
When someone here dwells endowed with virtue, [and so on through the standard formula] up through undertaking the precepts of training.
sbh1.3:2.8
kathaṁ ca śīlavān viharati |
How, again, does he dwell endowed with virtue?
sbh1.3:2.9
yāvat samādāya śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣu |
[As above,] up through undertaking the precepts of training.
sbh1.3:2.10
kathaṁ śīlavān viharati |
How [more concretely] does he dwell endowed with virtue?
sbh1.3:2.11
yathā samātteṣu śikṣāpadeṣu avipannakāyakarmāntaśca, bhavatyavipannakāyakarmāntaśca |
When, in the precepts undertaken, his bodily action is unimpaired,
sbh1.3:2.12
akhaṇḍacārī, achi cchi dracārī evaṁ śīlavān bhavati |
his conduct unbroken, his conduct unrent — thus is he endowed with virtue.
sbh1.3:3.1
kathaṁ prātimokṣasamvarasaṁvṛto bhavati |
How is he restrained by the restraint of the Prātimokṣa?
sbh1.3:3.2
saptanairyāṇikaṁ śīlaṁ prātimokṣasamvara ityucyate |
The seven categories of virtue conducive to liberation are called the restraint of the Prātimokṣa.
sapta-naiḥsaraṇika śīla-skandha — the seven categories of virtue conducive to liberation (naiḥsaraṇika, 'leading-out'). The Yogācāra schema enumerates the seven kinds of Prātimokṣa-restraint (bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, śikṣamāṇā, śrāmaṇera, śrāmaṇerī, upāsaka, upāsikā). The clause 'taking the monastic restraint specifically' (3.4) signals that this whole chapter operates within the bhikṣu's full ordination, even though the underlying śīla-skandha is structurally broader.
sbh1.3:3.3
ta ete nikāyabhedena bahavaḥ samvarā bhavanti |
These same restraints, divided by category, are many.
sbh1.3:3.4
asmiṁstvarthe bhikṣusamvaramadhiṣṭhāyāha pratimokṣasamvarasaṁvṛtaḥ |
Here, however, taking the monastic [bhikṣu] restraint specifically, [the text] says ‘restrained by the restraint of the Prātimokṣa.’
sbh1.3:3.5
kathamācārasampanno bhavati |
How is he endowed with proper conduct?
sbh1.3:3.6
yathāpi tadīryāpathamitikaraṇīya yaṁ vā, kuśalapakṣaprayogamvā adhiṣṭhāya lokānuvarttinā, lokānutkrāntena, vinayānuvarttinā, vinayānutkrāntena cācāreṇa samanvāgato bhavati |
When, with respect to deportment, duties-to-be-performed, or application to the wholesome side, he is endowed with conduct that conforms to the world and does not transgress the world, conforms to the Vinaya and does not transgress the Vinaya.
sbh1.3:3.7
tatra īryāpathādhiṣṭhāna ācāraḥ |
[First,] conduct grounded in deportment.
sbh1.3:3.8
kathaṁ na lokānu ko tkrānto na vina yotkrāntaḥ |
How does he avoid transgressing the world and avoid transgressing the Vinaya [in this respect]?
sbh1.3:3.9
yathāpi tadyatra caṁkramitavyaṁ |
He walks where one should walk,
sbh1.3:3.10
yathā caṁkramitavyaṁ |
and walks as one should walk —
sbh1.3:3.11
tatra yathā caṁkramyate caṁkramate yena na lokagarhito bhavati, na satāṁ, samyagratānāṁ, satpuruṣāṇāṁ, sahadhārmikāṇāṁ, vinayadharāṇāṁ, vinayaśikṣitānāmabadhyo bhavati |
[walking] in such a way that he is not blamed by the world, by the good, by the properly engaged, by the noble ones, by his co-religionists, by the Vinaya-holders, by those trained in Vinaya; he is not censurable,
Samyag-ratāḥ is rendered 'properly engaged' in the conduct sections. The earlier 'rightly delighted' was too literal for the social-review context, where the list names those competent to judge whether a monk's deportment and duties conform to the world and Vinaya.
sbh1.3:3.12
a garhyasthānīyaḥ |
not in a position to be reproached.
sbh1.3:3.13
yathā caṁkrama evaṁ sthānaṁ, niṣadyā, śayyā veditavyā |
As with walking, so with standing, sitting, and lying down — [the same applies].
sbh1.3:3.14
tatra itikaraṇīya ācāraḥ |
[Second,] conduct grounded in duties-to-be-performed.
sbh1.3:3.15
kathaṁ na lokotkrānto bhavati |
How does he avoid transgressing the world,
sbh1.3:3.16
na vinayotkrānta iti karaṇīyamucyate |
[how does he avoid] transgressing the Vinaya? — [These] are called the duties-to-be-performed:
sbh1.3:3.17
cīvarācchādanaṁ |
putting on the robe,
sbh1.3:3.18
uccāravastrāvaṁ |
disposing of excrement and urine,
sbh1.3:3.19
udakadantakāṣṭhaṁ |
[the use of] water and the tooth-stick,
sbh1.3:3.20
grāmapraveśaḥ |
entering the village,
sbh1.3:3.21
piṇḍapātanirhāraparibhogaḥ |
taking and bringing back alms-food, eating,
sbh1.3:3.22
pātranirmārda rja naṁ sthāpanaṁ ca |
the cleaning and putting away of the bowl,
sbh1.3:3.23
pādaprakṣālanaṁ ca |
the washing of the feet,
sbh1.3:3.24
śayanāsanaprajñaptiḥ |
the laying out of the lodging —
sbh1.3:3.25
tasyaiva cābhisaṁkṣepaḥ pātrakarma cīvarakarma iti |
and, in summary, bowl-work and robe-work.
sbh1.3:3.26
yadvā punarevaṁbhāgīyaṁ kiṁcittaditikaraṇīyamityucyate |
Whatever else is of this kind is called a duty-to-be-performed.
sbh1.3:3.27
tacca yathāyogaṁ yatra kalpayitavyaṁ, yathā ca kalpayitavyaṁ tatra tathā kalpayati |
These, as appropriate — wherever they are to be performed and however they are to be performed — he performs there and in that manner,
sbh1.3:3.28
yena laukikānāmanabha bhi yojyo bhavatyavigarhitaḥ |
such that he is not criticized by worldlings, not censured;
sbh1.3:3.29
vinayadharāṇāṁ vinayaśikṣitānā manapavādyo bhavatyavigarhitaḥ |
by the Vinaya-holders and those trained in Vinaya, he is not blameworthy, not censured;
sbh1.3:3.30
samyagratānāṁ sahadhārmikāṇāmevamitikaraṇīyādhiṣṭhāna ācāro lokānutkrānto bhavati |
by the properly engaged co-religionists [likewise]. Thus conduct grounded in duties-to-be-performed is conduct that does not transgress the world
sbh1.3:3.31
vinayānutkrāntaśca |
and does not transgress the Vinaya.
sbh1.3:4.1
tatra kuśalapakṣaprayogadhiṣṭhāna ācāraḥ |
[Third,] conduct grounded in application to the wholesome side.
sbh1.3:4.2
kathaṁ lokānutkrāntaśca bhavati, vinayānutkrāntaśca |
How is this non-transgressive of the world and non-transgressive of the Vinaya?
sbh1.3:4.3
kuśalapakṣaucyate |
The wholesome side is said to be:
sbh1.3:4.4
tadyathā svādhyāyagurūṇāṁ sāmīcīkarma, upasthā pa naṁ ca, tathā glānopasthānamanyo a nyamanukampācittamupasthāpya cchandadāna sa muprayogaḥ, paripṛcchā, dharmaśravaṇadakṣasyānalasasya vijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇāṁ kāyena caiyā ceryā kṛtyakriyā, pareṣāṁ ca kuśalapakṣasamudāpanā, dharmadeśanā |
attending and serving the recitation-teachers, looking after the sick, mutually arousing compassionate mind, the exchange of consents [chanda-dāna], inquiry, hearing of Dharma, adroitness in service, performing bodily and verbal duties toward co-religionists, rousing others to the wholesome side, teaching Dharma,
sbh1.3:4.5
pratisaṁlayanapraveśaparyaṅkanibandhāniṣadyā iti ya evaṁ bhāgīyā apyanye dharmā ayamucyate |
entering retreat, sitting cross-legged — these and other such dharmas are called
sbh1.3:4.6
kuśalapakṣaprayogaḥ |
application to the wholesome side.
sbh1.3:4.7
sa evaṁ kuśalapakṣaprayogo gaṁ yathāyogaṁ yathāparikīrtitaṁ |
He, accordingly — as has been proclaimed,
sbh1.3:4.8
yatra kalpayitavyaṁ tatra tathā kalpayati |
wherever it is to be performed — performs it in just that way,
sbh1.3:4.9
yena nānuyojyo bhavati |
such that he is not blameworthy,
sbh1.3:4.10
garhito laukikānāṁ vinayadharāṇāṁ, vinayaśikṣitānāṁ, samyagratānāṁ, satpuruṣāṇāṁ, sahadhārmikāṇāmayamucyate kuśalapakṣa prayogādhiṣṭhāna ācāra ro lokānutkrānto, vinayānutkrāntaśca |
not censured by worldlings, by Vinaya-holders, by those trained in Vinaya, by the properly engaged, by noble persons, or by his co-religionists. This is called conduct grounded in application to the wholesome side, [conduct] non-transgressive of world and Vinaya.
sbh1.3:4.11
ya ebhirākāraiḥ sampanna ācāra iyamucyate ācārasampat |
One endowed with such modes of conduct is called endowed with proper conduct.
sbh1.3:4.12
evaṁ cācārasampanno bhavati |
Thus he is endowed with proper conduct.
sbh1.3:4.13
kathañca gocarasampanno bhavati |
And how is he endowed with proper resort?
sbh1.3:4.14
pañca bhikṣoragocarāḥ |
Five are the non-resorts for a monk.
sbh1.3:4.15
katame pañca |
What five?
sbh1.3:4.16
tadyathā ghoṣo, veṣaṁ veśyā- pānāgāraḥ, rājakulaṁ, caṇḍālakaṭhinameva pañcamamiti |
A cowherd settlement, a prostitute’s house, a tavern, the royal household, and fifth a caṇḍāla quarter.
sbh1.3:4.17
ya etāṁstathāgatapratikṣiptānagocarān varjayitvā anyatra gocare caratyanavadye tatra kālenaivaṁ gocarasampanno bhavati |
He who avoids these non-resorts rejected by the Tathāgata, and who at the right time frequents elsewhere — the blameless resort — is endowed with proper resort.
sbh1.3:5.1
kathamaṇumātreṣvavadyeṣu bhayadarśī bhavati |
How does he see danger in the slightest fault?
sbh1.3:5.2
aṇumātramavadyamucyate |
‘Slightest fault’ is so called [for the following reasons].
sbh1.3:5.3
kṣudrāṇu nu kṣudrāṇi śikṣāpadāni yeṣvadhyāpattirvyutthānaṁ ca prajñāyate |
Very minor are those precepts of training in which transgression and rising-out [from transgression] are recognized [as small].
sbh1.3:5.4
teṣāṁ yādhyāpattiridamavadyamaṇumātraṁ |
Transgression of those [minor precepts] is the slightest fault.
sbh1.3:5.5
punastathā hi tasyā adhyāpatteralpakṛccheṇa vyuttiṣṭhate yena tadaṇumātramityucyate |
Again, since rising-out from such transgression is accomplished with little difficulty, it is therefore called slightest.
sbh1.3:5.6
tatra kathaṁ bhayadarśī bhavati |
How, then, does he see danger [in it]?
sbh1.3:5.7
sā hai vāha so ’ha meṣāmadhyāpattihetorabhavyo vā syāmaprāptasya prāptaye, anadhi gatasyādhi gamāya, asākṣātkṛtasya sākṣātkriyāyai, apāyago vā syāmapāyagāmī, ātmā vā me apavadet, śāstā vā, devatā vā, vijñā vā, sabrahmacāriṇo a dharmatayā vigarhayeyuḥ |
He reflects: ‘On account of this transgression I might become unfit for attaining what has not yet been attained, for realizing what has not yet been realized, for directly realizing what has not yet been directly realized; I might go to the lower realms, might become one bound for the lower realms; I would reproach myself, or the Teacher would reproach me, or the deities, or the wise, or my co-religionists would censure me as acting contrary to Dharma;
sbh1.3:5.8
digvidikṣu ca me pāpako varṇakīrttiśabdaśloko a bhyudgacchetsa ebhyo dṛṣṭadharmasāṁparāyikebhyastaddhetukebhyo vi śi ṣṭebhyo dharmebhyo bhayadarśī bhavati |
and in all directions and intermediate directions an evil reputation would arise about me’ — through [reflection on] the visible-here-and-now consequences and the after-state consequences, and through the distinguished dharmas obstructed by them, he becomes one who sees danger.
sbh1.3:5.9
yena tāni kṣudrāṇu nu kṣudrāṇi śikṣāpadāni jīvitahetorapi na saṁdhibhyo vyāpadyate |
By this [seeing of danger] he does not violate even those very minor precepts of training even for the sake of life — under any circumstance.
sbh1.3:5.10
kadācit karha rhi cit smṛtisaṁpramoṣādadhyāpannaḥ laghu laghveva yathādharmaṁ pratikaroti vyuttiṣṭhate |
If at some time, on some occasion, through forgetfulness he transgresses, he quickly, very quickly makes amends according to Dharma and rises out [of the transgression].
sbh1.3:5.11
evamaṇumātreṣvavadyeṣu bhayadarśī bhavati |
Thus he sees danger in the slightest fault.
sbh1.3:5.12
kathaṁ samādāya śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣu |
How does he train by undertaking the precepts of training?
sbh1.3:5.13
āha |
Answer:
sbh1.3:5.14
pūrvamanena prātimokṣasamvarasamādānajñapticaturthena karmaṇā upasampadyamānena katipayānāṁ śikṣāpadānāṁ śarīraṁ śrutaṁ, sātirekaṁ ca tadanyaṁ divasaṁ śikṣāpadaśataṁ prātimokṣasūtroddiṣṭaṁ pratijñayevopagataṁ sarvatra la bdhā ṣyāmīti lapsyāmīti |
Previously, when receiving the Prātimokṣa-restraint by the fourth formal act of motion at his full ordination, he heard the formulation of only a few precepts; the further hundred-odd precepts of the Prātimokṣa-sūtra he received only by way of pledge, [saying] ‘I shall acquire them in all respects.’
jñapti-caturtha karman — the 'fourth-formal-motion act,' the standard Vinaya procedure by which full ordination is conferred. The proposer states the motion (jñapti) and then three times asks the assembled saṅgha to confirm; silence on all three counts conveys consent. The phrasing here makes the point that at ordination the candidate hears only the framework of the precepts — the full hundred-odd training rules are received under pledge (samādāna-pratijñā) and gradually awakened to over the course of training, which is the work of section 5 as a whole.
sbh1.3:5.15
ācāryopādhyāyānāmantikācchrutvā ālaptakasaṁlaptakasaṁstutakamapriyakānāṁ ṇām adhbaramāsaṁgaprātimokṣasūtroddeśataḥ |
Having heard from his preceptor and instructor the Prātimokṣa-sūtra recited in the regular half-monthly recitation,
sbh1.3:5.16
tataśca tena sarvaśikṣāsamādānāt prātimokṣasamvaraḥ avyutpannabuddhiḥ |
by which [recitation] the Prātimokṣa-restraint [is understood] through the undertaking of all the trainings — though his understanding had not yet become developed [in detail]:
sbh1.3:5.17
tāni pūrvaṁ pratijñāsamādānena samādattānyetarhi vyutpattikauśalyatayā samādadāti |
those precepts previously undertaken by way of pledge he now undertakes through developed skill [in their actual content],
sbh1.3:5.18
tebhyaḥ pūrvaṁ yathāparikīrttitebhyaḥ sthānebhya ācāryasya vopādhyāyasya vā pūrvavat |
[doing so] on the basis previously proclaimed, [namely] from the preceptor or instructor, as before.
sbh1.3:5.19
vyutpattikauśalyatayā ca punaḥ samādāya, yathānuśiṣṭaḥ anyūnamadhikaṁ śikṣate |
And having thus undertaken [them] through skilled awakening, he trains as instructed, neither lessening nor adding.
sbh1.3:5.20
te ṣu guru sthānīyavyapadiṣṭeṣu śikṣāpadeṣu aviparītagrāhī ca bhavatyarthasya vyaṁjanasya ca |
In those precepts of training proclaimed in the Teacher’s place [i.e., authoritatively transmitted], he is a non-perverted grasper both as to meaning and as to letter.
sbh1.3:5.21
evaṁ samādāya śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣvayaṁ tāvadvibhaṁgaḥ śīlasamvarasya vistarakṛtaḥ |
Thus he trains by undertaking the precepts of training. — Such, in this much, is the detailed division of the restraint of virtue.
sbh1.3:6.1
tatra katamaḥ samāsārthaḥ |
What, then, is the summary meaning?
sbh1.3:6.2
tathāyaṁ samāsārthastrilakṣaṇa eva |
This summary meaning has just three marks.
sbh1.3:6.3
śīlaskandhaḥ paridīpito bhagavatā, tadyathā avipraṇāśalakṣaṇaḥ, svabhāvalakṣaṇaḥ, svabhāvaguṇalakṣaṇaśca |
[That is,] the aggregate of virtue is elucidated by the Blessed One under three marks: the mark of non-loss, the mark of own-nature, and the mark of own-nature-with-its-qualities.
sbh1.3:6.4
yathā kathamiti yattāvadāha śīlavān viharatītyanena tāvadavipraṇāśalakṣaṇaṁ śīlasamvarasyākhyātaṁ |
How so? — In that he says ‘he dwells endowed with virtue,’ by this the non-loss mark of the restraint of virtue is declared.
sbh1.3:7.1
yatpunarāha prāti mokṣasamvarasaṁvṛta iti |
In that he says ‘restrained by the restraint of the Prātimokṣa,’
sbh1.3:7.2
anena svabhāvalakṣaṇamākhyātaṁ |
by this the own-nature mark is declared.
sbh1.3:7.3
yatpunarāha |
Again, in that he says
sbh1.3:7.4
ācāragocarasampannaḥ |
‘endowed with proper conduct and proper resort,’
sbh1.3:7.5
anena paramupanidhāya, tathā samādattasya prātimokṣasamvarasya guṇalakṣaṇamākhyātaṁ |
by this — taking him in further [comparison] — the quality-mark of the Prātimokṣa-restraint so undertaken is declared.
sbh1.3:7.6
tathāpi pare tāmācāragocarasampadamupalabhyāprasannāśca prasīdanti, prasannānāṁ ca bhavati bhūyobhāvaḥ |
For when others observe his fullness of conduct and resort, those without confidence gain confidence, and those already confident become still more so;
sbh1.3:7.7
prasannāśca prasannādhikāraṁ kurvanti |
those with confidence perform acts grounded in confidence.
sbh1.3:7.8
na ca manānsi manāṁsi pradūṣayanti |
And they do not defile their minds,
sbh1.3:7.9
nāvvarṇaṁ niścārayantyanyathā śīlasampannasyācāragocarasampannasyāyaṁ parādhipateyo guṇa ānuśansā ānuśaṁsā ca bhavedetadviparyayeṇa vā cā sya doṣa eva bhavet |
they do not send forth blame. For one endowed with virtue and endowed with proper conduct and resort, this is the quality and benefit dependent on others; in the opposite case, it would instead be his fault.
sbh1.3:7.10
yatpunarāha |
Again, in that he says
sbh1.3:7.11
aṇumātreṣvavadyeṣu bhayadarśī samādāya śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣu |
‘seeing danger in the slightest faults, he undertakes the precepts of training,’
sbh1.3:7.12
anenādhyātmādhipateyaguṇānuśansa śaṁsa lakṣaṇamākhyātaṁ |
by this the mark of the quality-and-benefit under self-predominance is declared.
sbh1.3:7.13
tatkasya hetoryadasyeda ma mācāragocarasampannaḥ parādhipateyaṁ guṇānuśansaṁ śaṁsaṁ pratilabheta |
For what reason? Because this person, endowed with proper conduct and resort, [already] gains the quality-and-benefit dependent on others.
sbh1.3:7.14
api ca |
Moreover,
sbh1.3:7.15
śīlaṁ vi pātayitvā vipātya taddhetustatpratyayamapāyeṣūpapadyate |
[were he to] ruin virtue, by that cause, by that condition, he would be reborn in the lower realms,
sbh1.3:7.16
abhavyatāmvā tāṁvā aprāptasya prāptaye pūrvat |
or would become unfit for the attainment of the not-yet-attained, [as detailed] above.
sbh1.3:7.17
yatpunaraṇumātreṣvavadyeṣu bhayadarśī bhavati |
Now since he sees danger in the slightest fault —
sbh1.3:7.18
prāgevādhi mātreṣvasamādāya ca śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣu tasmāttaddhetustatpratyayaṁ kāyasya bhedāt sugatāvupapadyate |
still more in the gross — and trains by undertaking the precepts of training, by that cause, by that condition, at the breaking up of the body he is reborn in a happy destiny,
sbh1.3:7.19
bhavyo vā bhavatyaprāptasya prāptasya prāptaye pūrva va danena kāraṇenā dhyātmādhipateyo a yaṁ śīlasamvarasya guṇānuśaṁsa ityucyate |
or becomes fit for the attainment of the not-yet-attained, [as] above. For this reason [the foregoing] is called the quality-and-benefit under self-predominance of the restraint of virtue.
sbh1.3:8.1
aparaḥ punaḥ paryāyaḥ |
Another mode [of summary]:
sbh1.3:8.2
samāsato bhagavatā samādattaśīlatā paridīpitā, nairyāṇikaśīlatā ca, śīlabhāvanā ca |
in brief, the Blessed One has elucidated [under three rubrics] undertaken-virtue, leading-out virtue, and the cultivation of virtue.
sbh1.3:8.3
tatra yattāvadāha |
In that he says,
sbh1.3:8.4
śīlavānviharatītyanena samādattaśīlatākhyātā |
‘he dwells endowed with virtue,’ by this undertaken-virtue is declared.
sbh1.3:8.5
yat punarāha |
Again, in that he says,
sbh1.3:8.6
prātimokṣasamvarasaṁvṛta ityanena nairyāṇikaśīlatā ākhyātā |
‘restrained by the restraint of the Prātimokṣa,’ by this leading-out virtue [nairyāṇika] is declared.
Nairyāṇika-śīla — 'leading-out virtue,' i.e., virtue that leads out of saṁsāra. The text triangulates the three trainings: Prātimokṣa-restraint is the higher-virtue training (adhiśīla-śikṣā), which serves as forerunner for the higher-mind (adhicitta) and higher-wisdom (adhiprajñā) trainings that complete the path. The term nairyāṇika recurs as the standard Yogācāra marker of soteriological efficacy.
sbh1.3:8.7
tathā hi prātimokṣasamvarasaṁgṛhītaṁ śīlamadhiśīlaṁ śikṣetyucyate |
For the virtue subsumed under the Prātimokṣa-restraint is called the training in higher virtue [adhiśīla].
sbh1.3:8.8
adhiśīlaṁ ca śikṣāṁ niśritya, adhicittaṁ ca, adhiprajñaṁ ca śikṣāṁ bhāvayatyevamasau sarvaduḥkhakṣayāya |
And on the basis of the training in higher virtue, he cultivates the training in higher mind [adhicitta] and the training in higher wisdom [adhiprajñā] — and thus is led out toward the destruction of all suffering.
sbh1.3:8.9
niryāto bhavati |
He is led out [from saṁsāra by this means].
sbh1.3:8.10
yadutādhiśīlaṁ pratiṣṭhāya pūvvaṁgamaṁ kṛtvā tasmāt prātimokṣasaṁvaro nairyāṇikaṁ śīlamityucyate |
That is, on the basis of higher virtue, taking it as forerunner — therefore the Prātimokṣa-restraint is called leading-out virtue.
sbh1.3:8.11
yatpunarāha |
Again, in that he says,
sbh1.3:8.12
ācāragocarasampannaḥ aṇumātreṣvavadyeṣu bhayadarśī samādāya śikṣate śikṣāpadeṣvanena śīlabhāvanā ākhyātā |
‘endowed with proper conduct and resort, seeing danger in the slightest fault, undertaking the precepts of training,’ by this the cultivation of virtue is declared.
sbh1.3:8.13
ebhirākāraistatprātimokṣasamvaraśīlambhāvayitavyaṁ |
By these modes that Prātimokṣa-restraint-virtue is to be cultivated,
sbh1.3:8.14
evaṁ ca bhāvitaṁ subhāvitaṁ bhavatīti sa eṣa ekaḥ śīlasamvaraḥ ṣaḍākāradeśanāpratyupasthāno veditavyaḥ |
and so cultivated, it becomes well cultivated. Thus this single restraint of virtue is to be understood as set forth by a six-mode teaching.
sbh1.3:9.1
sa caiṣa śīlasamvaro daśabhirākārairvipanno veditavyaḥ |
This restraint of virtue is to be understood as ruined by ten modes,
sbh1.3:9.2
viparyayāddaśabhiścaiva kāraṇaiḥ sampannaḥ |
and conversely, by ten causes, [as] perfected.
sbh1.3:9.3
katamairdaśabhiḥ kāraṇairvipanno bhavati |
By what ten causes is it ruined?
sbh1.3:9.4
ādita eva durgṛhīto bhavatyatilīno bhavatyanisṛto bhavati, pramādakausīdyaparigṛhīto bhavati |
It is ill-grasped from the very beginning; it is excessively slack; it is excessively strict; it is gripped by heedlessness and indolence;
sbh1.3:9.5
mithyā praṇihito bhavati |
it is wrongly resolved;
sbh1.3:9.6
ācāravipattyā parigṛhīto tatra kathamādito durgṛhītaṁ śīlaṁ bhavati |
it is gripped by deficiency of conduct. Here, how is virtue ill-grasped from the very beginning?
sbh1.3:9.7
yathāpī haikatyo rājābhinirṇīto vā, pravrajitaścaurābhinirṇīto vā, ṛṇārttovā, bhayārtto vā, vā, a ā jīvikābhayabhīto vā, na śrāmaṇyāya, na brāhmaṇyāya, nātmaśamāya, nātmadamāya, nātmaparinirvāṇāyaivamādita ḥ |
When someone here goes forth under pressure from a king, or under pressure from thieves, or distressed by debt, or distressed by fear, or in fear for his livelihood — not for the sake of asceticism, not for the sake of brahminhood [brāhmaṇya], not for the calming of self, not for the restraint of self, not for the final nirvāṇa of self — thus from the very beginning
Rājābhinirṇīta / caurābhinirṇīta repeat the bad-going-forth motivation list from sbh1.1:12.14. Rendered 'under pressure from a king/thieves' rather than 'resolved upon' because the issue is external coercion or fear-driven renunciation, not a mental resolution.
sbh1.3:9.8
duḥgṛhīto durgṛhītaṁ bhavati |
virtue is ill-grasped.
sbh1.3:9.9
kathamatilīno naṁ bhavati |
How is it excessively slack?
sbh1.3:9.10
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ alajjī bhavati mandakaukṛtyaḥ |
When someone here is shameless, with weak remorse,
sbh1.3:9.11
śaithilikaḥ śithilakārī śikṣopadiṣṭe a yamatilīno naṁ bhavati |
loose, doing loosely what is taught in the training — this is excessive slackness.
sbh1.3:10.1
kathamatisṛto taṁ bhavati |
How is it excessively strict?
sbh1.3:10.2
yathāpīhaikatyo durgṛhītagrāhī bhavatyasthānakaukṛtyaḥ |
When someone here is an ill-grasper, feeling regret in the wrong place —
sbh1.3:10.3
saukṛtyakaraṇīyeṣu sthāneṣu kaukṛtyāyamānaḥ |
feeling regret in cases where good action should be done;
sbh1.3:10.4
asthāne pareṣāmantike paribhavacittaṁ vā ākhyātaṁ votpādayati |
without proper occasion, he produces with respect to others
sbh1.3:10.5
pravedayatyevamatisṛtaṁ bhavati |
either a contemptuous attitude or a denouncing one — and reports [the supposed faults] to them. Thus it is excessively strict.
sbh1.3:10.6
kathaṁ pramādakausīdyaparigṛhītaṁ bhavati |
How is it gripped by heedlessness and indolence?
sbh1.3:10.7
yathāpīhaikatyo a tītamadhvānamupādāyāpattimāpannaḥ |
When someone here, with respect to the past, has fallen into transgression,
sbh1.3:10.8
sā cānena smṛtisaṁpramoṣādekatyā na yathādharmaṁ pratikṛtā bhavati |
and through forgetfulness has not atoned for a single [transgression] according to Dharma;
sbh1.3:10.9
yathā atītamadhvānamupādāya evamanāgataṁ vartamānamadhvānamupādāya yāmāpattimāpanno bhavati |
as with the past, so with the future and the present — whatever transgression he has fallen into,
sbh1.3:10.10
sā cānena smṛtisaṁpramoṣādekatyā na yathādharmapratikṛtā bhavati |
through forgetfulness none is atoned for according to Dharma;
sbh1.3:10.11
na ca pūrvamevāpatterāyatyāmanadhyāpattaye tīvramautsukyamāpadyate |
and he does not, in advance, undertake intense effort for the sake of non-transgression in the future, [resolving]
sbh1.3:10.12
yannvahaṁ tathā tathā careyaṁ, yathā yathā caran viharaṁścāpattiṁ nādhyāpadyeya, tathā ca, tathā carati, viharati |
‘Let me act in such a way that, acting and dwelling [so], I would not commit transgression’ — yet so he acts, so he dwells,
sbh1.3:10.13
yathāpattimadhyāpadyate |
such that he commits transgression.
sbh1.3:10.14
so a nena pūrvāntasahagatenāparāntasahagatena, madhyāntasahagatena, pūrvakālakaraṇīyena sahānucareṇa pramādena samanvāgato nidrāsukhaṁ, śayanasukhaṁ, pārśvasukhaṁ ca svīkaroti |
Endowed with this heedlessness connected with the past limit, the future limit, and the middle limit — along with heedlessness about what should have been done earlier — he holds dear the pleasure of sleep, lying down, and resting on the side;
sbh1.3:10.15
adakṣaśca bhavatyalasaḥ, anutthānasaṁpannaḥ, na kartā bhavati vijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇāṁ kāyena vaiyāpṛtyamevaṁ vyāpṛtyaivaṁ pramādakausīdyaparigṛhītaṁ bhavati |
he becomes unskilled, lazy, lacking energy [to rise up]; he does not perform bodily service for his wise co-religionists. Thus engaged, he is gripped by heedlessness and indolence.
sbh1.3:11.1
kathaṁ mithyāpraṇihitaṁ bhavati |
How is it wrongly resolved?
sbh1.3:11.2
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ praṇidhāya brahmacaryaṁ carati |
When someone here lives the holy life having resolved:
sbh1.3:11.3
anenāhaṁ śīlena vā, bratena vā, tapasā vā, brahmacaryavāsena vā, devo vā syāṁ, devānyatamo vā, lābha satkārakāmo bhavati |
‘By this virtue, observance, austerity, holy life, I shall become a god or some [other] kind of divine being,’ desirous of gain and honor.
sbh1.3:11.4
parataḥ lābhasatkāraṁ prārthayate |
He seeks gain and honor from others;
sbh1.3:11.5
lābhasatkārasya spṛhayati |
he yearns for gain and honor.
sbh1.3:11.6
evaṁ mithyāpraṇihitaṁ bhavati |
Thus it is wrongly resolved.
sbh1.3:11.7
kathamācāravipattyā parigṛhītaṁ bhavati |
How is it gripped by deficiency of conduct?
sbh1.3:11.8
yathāpīhaikatya īryāpathaṁ vādhiṣṭhāya itikaraṇīyambā kuśalapakṣaprayogaṁ vā lokotkrāntaśca bhavati |
When someone here, having undertaken either deportment, or duties-to-be-performed, or application to the wholesome side, transgresses the world
sbh1.3:11.9
vinayotkrāntaśca pūrvavadevamācāravipattyā parigṛhītaṁ bhavati |
and transgresses the Vinaya, [as detailed] above — thus it is gripped by deficiency of conduct.
sbh1.3:11.10
kathamājīvavipattyā parigṛhītaṁ bhavati |
How is it gripped by deficiency of livelihood?
sbh1.3:11.11
yathāpīhaikatyo maheccho bhavatyasaṁtuṣṭaḥ, durmoṣo, durgharajātīyaḥ |
[Damaged/Conjectural]: When someone here has great desires, is discontented, hard to satisfy, and hard to support,
The printed/root durmoṣa, durghara-jātīya is suspect in this wrong-livelihood profile. The expected pair is the stock requisites-related description 'hard to satisfy / hard to support' (durpoṣa/durbhara-jātīya). The earlier English 'dull memory, bad-house lineage [duṣkulīna]' did not match the local root or the livelihood context, so the line is marked conjectural/corrupt.
sbh1.3:11.12
sa cādharmeṇa cīvaraṁ paryeṣate |
and seeks robes by non-Dharma,
sbh1.3:11.13
na dharmeṇā a dharmeṇa piṇḍapātaṁ, śayanāsanaṁ, glānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkāramparyeṣate |
not by Dharma — and seeks alms-food, lodging, medicine, [and other] requisites by non-Dharma,
sbh1.3:11.14
na dharmeṇa |
not by Dharma;
sbh1.3:11.15
sa ca cīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārahetoḥ ātmano guṇasaṁbhāvanānimittamaprākṛtaṁ tiṭhapita mīryāpathaṁ kalpayatyanuddhatendriyatāmacapalendriyatāṁ, śāntendriyatāñca pareṣāmupadarśayati |
and for the sake of robes, alms-food, lodging, medicine, [and other] requisites — for the sake of generating regard [for himself in others] — he displays to others a contrived, deliberately composed deportment: non-restless faculties, non-fickle faculties, peaceful faculties,
sbh1.3:11.16
yenāsya pare guṇasaṁbhāvanā jātā dātavyaṁ kartavyaṁ manyante |
by which others, having generated regard for his qualities, think [that things] should be given, [things] should be done —
sbh1.3:11.17
yaduta cīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārān |
namely, robes, alms-food, lodging, medicine, [and other] requisites.
sbh1.3:11.18
dhvāṅkṣaśca bhavati, mukharaḥ |
He becomes clamorous, talkative,
The root has dhvāṅkṣa ('crow/raven-like,' hence clamorous or cawing) plus mukhara ('talkative'). The prior bracket bhāṇḍaka was not local to this line and wrongly suggested flattery rather than noisy wrong-livelihood speech.
sbh1.3:11.19
pragalbhaḥ, kelāyitā, nāmagotrodgṛhītā, bahuśruto bhavati |
brazen, frivolous, taking note of [people’s] names and clans; he is much-learned,
sbh1.3:11.20
dharmadharo, lābhakāraṇādeva ca pareṣāṁ dharmaṁ saṁlapati, śrāvakabhāṣitaṁ vā, ātmano vā guṇān, bhūtānvā ki ñcidvā punaḥ samāropya svayameva vaktā bhavati |
a Dharma-holder, and from a gain-motive alone he speaks Dharma to others — what was spoken by the disciples, or his own qualities, whether actual or falsely attributed — making himself the speaker.
In the wrong-livelihood speech section, bhūta is read as actual/real qualities, contrasted with qualities falsely attributed or inflated for gain. It is not 'past matters.' The passage is about manipulative self-presentation to induce donors, not historical narration.
sbh1.3:11.21
lāpayati vā parairanuttareṇa vā upadarśayitā, cīvarārthī vā, anyatamānyatamena vā śrāmaṇakena pariṣkāreṇārthī, prabhūtena vā, agratareṇa vā, avihanyamāno ’pi prākṛtasya cīvarasyopadarśayitā bhavati |
Or he induces others to speak [on his behalf]; or, being in need of a robe or some other recluse's requisite, whether much or excellent, he displays an inferior robe even when it has not been damaged,
sbh1.3:11.22
asyecchan śrāddhā brāhmaṇagṛhapatayaḥ cīvareṇa vighātaṁ saṁlakṣayitvā saṁlakṣya prabhūtaṁ praṇītaṁ cīvaraṁ dātavyaṁ kartavyaṁ maṁsyate nte |
[thinking]: ‘Faithful brāhmaṇas and householders, noticing trouble with his robe, will think that a plentiful and fine robe should be given.’
sbh1.3:11.23
yathā cīvaramevamanyatamānyatamaṁ śrāmaṇakaṁ jīvitapariṣkāraṁ śrāddhānāñca brāhmaṇagṛhapatīnāmantikādyathākāmaṁ vā alabhamānaḥ, asatsu vā a saṁvidyamāneṣu bhogeṣvalabhamāna evaṁcoparodhena yācate |
As with the robe, so with any other recluse’s requisite — when he does not obtain it at will from faithful brāhmaṇa-householders, or when the enjoyments are non-existent or not present — by importunity he begs,
sbh1.3:11.24
niṣpiṣya niṣpiyāmi pi caināṁ paruṣayatyapi hīnamvā punarlabdhvā tathā saṁvidyamāneṣu bhogeṣu taṁ lābhaṁ mansa maṁsa yatyavasādayati |
pressing them and speaking harshly. Or, having obtained an inferior thing when [better] enjoyments are present, he derogates that gain, depreciates it,
sbh1.3:11.25
saṁmukhaṁ ca dātāraṁ dānapatiṁ, evaṁ cāha |
and in front of the giver-patron speaks thus:
sbh1.3:11.26
haṁ bhoḥ, kulaputra, santyeke kulaputrāḥ kuladuhitaraśca ye tavāntikā nīcakulīnatarāśca daridratarāśca te punarevaṁ caivañca praṇītadāyino mana āpadāyinaśca |
‘Ho, son of good family, there are sons of good family and daughters [of good family] in your vicinity — of lower clan and poorer than you — who nevertheless give thus and so, finely, pleasingly.
sbh1.3:11.27
kasmāttvaṁ teṣāmantikāduccakulīnatarāścānyataraśca samāna eva samanāpa mana āpa dāyī, nāpraṇītaparīttadāyī ceti |
Why do you, though of higher clan and other [advantages], not give as pleasingly? Why are you not a pleasing giver, a giver of fine, non-meager gifts?’
sbh1.3:11.28
ya ebhirākauraḥ kuhanāmbā niśritya, lapanāmvā, naimittikatāmvā, naiṣpeṣikatāmvā, lābhena lābhaṁ niścikīrṣatāṁ, cīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārān parataḥ paryeṣate |
He who by such modes — relying on hypocrisy, on talkative chatter [lapanā], on insinuation [naimittikatā], on extortion [naiṣpeṣikatā], on gain-seeking-by-gain [lābhena lābha-niścikīrṣā] — seeks robes, alms-food, lodging, medicine, [and other] requisites from others,
The list — kuhanā (hypocrisy), lapanā (talkative chatter), naimittikatā (insinuating hints), naiṣpeṣikatā (extortion / pressing), and lābhena lābha-niścikīrṣā (seeking gain by means of gain) — is the canonical pañca-mithyā-jīva, the five wrong livelihoods of monastics. Standard list across both Theravāda Visuddhimagga (I.61) and Sarvāstivāda-Yogācāra abhidharma; preserved here in brackets so the reader can recognize the technical taxonomy underneath the long descriptive expansions at 11.15–27.
sbh1.3:11.29
so a dharmeṇa |
he [does so] by non-Dharma —
sbh1.3:11.30
yaḥ punaradharmeṇa so a sya bhavati mithyājīvaḥ |
and what is by non-Dharma is for him wrong livelihood.
sbh1.3:11.31
evaṁ tacchīlamājīvavipattyā parigṛhītaṁ bhavati |
Thus that virtue is gripped by deficiency of livelihood.
sbh1.3:11.32
kathamantadvayapatitaṁ bhavati |
How is it fallen into the two extremes?
sbh1.3:11.33
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ kāmasukhallikānuyukto bhavatyadhyavasitatāṁparaḥ |
When someone here is bent on the pleasure of sense-desires, supremely attached to them.
sbh1.3:11.34
pratilabdhāndharmeṇa vā adharmeṇa vā cīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārān paribhuṅkte |
He uses the robes, alms-food, lodging, medicine, [and other] requisites — obtained either by Dharma or by non-Dharma —
sbh1.3:11.35
ādīnavādarśī niḥsaraṇamaprajānannayamucyate eko a ntaḥ |
without seeing the danger, without knowing the escape. This is called one extreme.
sbh1.3:11.36
punaraparamihaikatya ātmaklamathānuyukto bhavatyanekaparyāyeṇātmānamātāpayati, santāpayati |
And again, here someone is bent on self-mortification; in manifold ways he scorches and torments himself.
sbh1.3:11.37
kaṣṭavratasamādāyī ca bhavati |
He undertakes severe vows:
sbh1.3:11.38
tadyathā kaṇṭakāpāśrayo vā bhavati |
one whose resort is thorns,
sbh1.3:11.39
bhasmādāyī musalāpāśrayaḥ |
an ash-taker, one whose resort is the pestle,
sbh1.3:11.40
phalakāpāśrayo bhavati |
one whose resort is the plank,
sbh1.3:11.41
utkuhakasthito bhavatyutkuhakaprahāṇayogamanuyuktaḥ |
a squatter applied to the squatting-renunciation practice,
sbh1.3:11.42
agniparicārako bhavati |
a fire-attendant —
sbh1.3:11.43
yāvat trirapyagniṁ parica rati udakamadhyāhāro bhavati |
up to three times [a day] he tends the fire; a water-immerser —
sbh1.3:11.44
yāvat trirapyudakamadhyāvai va hati |
up to three times [a day] he immerses in water;
sbh1.3:11.45
ekapādakaḥ sthitvā yataḥ sūryastataḥ parivartate |
standing on one foot, he turns with the sun.
sbh1.3:11.46
iti yo vā punarapyevaṁbhāgīya ātmakaklamathānuyogaḥ ayamucyate dvitīyo a ntaḥ |
Such, or any other such kind of application to self-mortification — this is called the second extreme.
sbh1.3:11.47
e vamanta dvayapatitaṁ bhavati |
Thus it is fallen into the two extremes.
sbh1.3:11.48
kathamanairyāṇikaṁ bhavati |
How is it not leading out [aniḥsaraṇika]?
sbh1.3:11.49
yathāpīhai katyaḥ śīlaṁ vā vratamvā dṛṣṭyā parāmṛśati |
When someone here clings to virtue or observance by [wrong] view:
sbh1.3:11.50
anenaiva śīlena vā vratena vā śuddhirbhaviṣyati |
‘By this very virtue or observance there will be purification,
sbh1.3:11.51
muktirniryāṇaṁ bhaviṣyatīti |
release, leading out.’
sbh1.3:11.52
sarvaṁ ca śīlamito bāhyānāṁ surakṣa kṣi tamapi suviśuddhamapi tadupamayā viśuddhyā anairyāṇikamityucyate |
All the virtue of those outside [the Buddha’s dispensation] — even well-guarded, even well-purified — by such [a wrong-view] purification is called not-leading-out.
sbh1.3:11.53
evamanairyāṇikaṁ bhavati |
Thus it is not-leading-out.
sbh1.3:11.54
kathaṁ samādānaparibhraṣṭaṁ bhavati |
How is it fallen from the undertaking?
sbh1.3:11.55
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ sarveṇa sarvamalajjī bha bhavati nirapekṣaḥ |
When someone here is wholly, entirely shameless, indifferent —
sbh1.3:11.56
śrāmaṇye sa ca bhavati |
[though] he [stands] within the ascetic life —
sbh1.3:11.57
duḥśīlaḥ pāpadharmā antaḥ pūtiravasrutaḥ kaśambodakajātaḥ |
of evil virtue, evil dharma, inwardly rotten, oozing, of impure-water-mixed-up nature,
sbh1.3:11.58
śaṁkhasvarasamācāraḥ |
of conch-shell-rattle conduct,
sbh1.3:11.59
aśramaṇa ḥ śramaṇapratijñaḥ abrahmacārī brahmacāripratijña evaṁ samādānaparibhraṣṭaṁ bhavati |
not a recluse but claiming to be a recluse, not a celibate but claiming to be celibate — thus it is fallen from the undertaking.
sbh1.3:11.60
ebhirdaśabhiḥ kāraṇairvipanna nnaṁ śīlaṁ śīlavipattirityuktā bhagavatā |
By these ten causes virtue is ruined — this is called by the Blessed One the ruin of virtue.
sbh1.3:11.61
api ca śīlābhyasanamapyuktaṁ bhagavatā |
Moreover, the practice [of virtue] too was stated by the Blessed One.
sbh1.3:11.62
da ta cca ebhyaḥ kāraṇebhyo dvābhyāṁ kāraṇābhyāṁ veditavyam |
Now among these [ten] causes, [the ruin] is to be understood [especially] from two:
sbh1.3:11.63
yā cānairyāṇikatā, yaśca samādānabhraṁśaḥ tadanyaiśca kāraṇaiḥ śīlavipattireva veditavyā |
namely, from not-leading-out and from falling-from-the-undertaking — and also from the other causes; [all] these are to be understood as the ruin of virtue.
sbh1.3:11.64
eṣāmeva ca kṛṣṇapakṣavyavasthitānāṁ kāraṇānāṁ viparyayeṇa śuklapakṣyaḥ kāraṇaiḥ śīlasampattirveditavyā |
From these very causes set on the dark side, by their reverse — by the bright-side causes — the fullness of virtue is to be understood,
sbh1.3:11.65
śīlaviśuddhiśca |
and [likewise] the purification of virtue.
sbh1.3:11.66
kvacit punarbhagavatāśīlaṁ mūlārthenoktaṁ |
In some places, again, the Blessed One speaks of virtue by the word ‘root.’
sbh1.3:11.67
yathoktaṁ- supratiṣṭhitamūla ḥ syāccittasyopaśame rataḥ |
As said: ‘He is one whose root is well-established, delighting in the calming of the mind,
sbh1.3:11.68
saṁyuktā ca visaṁyuktā dṛṣhyādṛṣhyāryapāpayeti gāthā |
connected and disconnected from the seen-and-unseen, noble and evil’ — so the verse.
sbh1.3:12.1
kvacidalaṁkāra śabdenoktaṁ |
In some places it is spoken of by the word ‘ornament.’
sbh1.3:12.2
yathoktaṁ śīlālaṁkārasampanno bhikṣurvā bhikṣuṇī vā akuśalaṁ prajahāti kuśalaṁ bhāvayati |
As stated: ‘A monk or nun endowed with the ornament of virtue abandons the unwholesome, cultivates the wholesome.’
sbh1.3:12.3
kvacidanulepanaśabdenoktaṁ |
In some places it is spoken of by the word ‘ointment.’
sbh1.3:12.4
yatrāha |
Where he says:
sbh1.3:12.5
śīlānulepanasampanno bhikṣurvā bhikṣuṇīveti pūrvavat |
‘A monk or nun endowed with the ointment of virtue,’ [the rest] as before.
sbh1.3:12.6
kvacidgandhaśabdenoktaṁ |
In some places it is spoken of by the word ‘scent.’
sbh1.3:12.7
asti taddānaṁ yadgandhajātaṁ yasyānuvātamapi gandho vāti prativāma ta mapyanuvātamapi gandho vāti |
‘There is that giving which is a kind of scent, whose scent reaches downwind, against the wind, [and] both with and against the wind.’
sbh1.3:12.8
kvacit sucaritaśabdenoktaṁ |
In some places it is spoken of by the word ‘good conduct’ [sucarita].
sbh1.3:12.9
yatrāha |
Where he says:
sbh1.3:12.10
kāyasucaritasyeṣṭo vipāko dṛṣṭe dharme abhisaṁparāye ca evaṁ vāksucaritasya |
‘The fruit of good bodily conduct is to be desired in the visible-here-and-now and in the after-state; likewise of good verbal conduct.’
sbh1.3:12.11
kvacitsamvaraśabdenoktam |
In some places it is spoken of by the word ‘restraint.’
sbh1.3:12.12
yatrāha |
Where he says:
sbh1.3:12.13
dātā dānapatiḥ śīlavān bhavati |
‘The giver, the donor, becomes endowed with virtue,
sbh1.3:12.14
samvarasthāyī āgamadṛṣṭiḥ phaladarśī |
established in restraint, grounded in scriptural learning and view, seeing the fruit.’
sbh1.3:12.15
apicoktaṁ |
And it is said:
sbh1.3:12.16
śīlavān viharati |
‘He dwells endowed with virtue,
sbh1.3:12.17
prātimokṣasamvarasaṁvṛta iti vistaraḥ |
restrained by the restraint of the Prātimokṣa,’ [as elaborated above] in detail.
sbh1.3:12.18
kena kāraṇena bhagavatā śīlaṁ mūlaśabdenoktam |
For what reason did the Blessed One speak of virtue by the word ‘root’?
sbh1.3:12.19
pratiṣṭhārthaṁ ādhārārtho mūlārthaḥ |
Because the meaning of ‘root’ is the meaning of establishment, the meaning of basis.
sbh1.3:12.20
taccaitacchīlaṁ sarveṣāmeva laukikalokottarāṇāṁ śuddhānā manavadyānāmagryāṇāṁ pravarāṇāṁ sukhāhārāṇāṁ, pratiṣṭhāsthānīyaṁ cotpattaye, pratilaṁbhāya tasmānmūlaśabdenocyate |
This very virtue is the establishment, the basis, for the arising and attainment of all worldly and supramundane pure, blameless, supreme, distinguished, pleasant-fruited dharmas; therefore it is spoken of by the word ‘root.’
sbh1.3:12.21
tadyathā pṛthivī pratiṣṭhā bhavatyādhāra stṛṇagu lmauṣadhivanaspatīnāmutpattaye evameva śīlambistareṇa pūrvavadvācyam |
Just as the earth is the establishment, the basis, for the arising of grasses, shrubs, herbs, and trees, just so [is] virtue, [as detailed] above.
sbh1.3:12.22
kena kāraṇena śīlamalaṁkāraśabdenākhyātaṁ |
For what reason is virtue declared by the word ‘ornament’?
sbh1.3:12.23
āha |
Answer:
sbh1.3:12.24
yāni tadanyāni bhūṣaṇāni tadyayā harṣamvā, kaṭāhā vā, keyūrā vā, mudrikā vā, jātarūparajatamālā vā tāni yāvadayaṁ dahro bhavati |
Those other adornments — necklaces, bracelets, armlets, rings, garlands of gold and silver — only as long as one is young,
sbh1.3:12.25
śiśu ḥ kṛṣṇakeśaḥ pratyagrayauvanasamanvāgataḥ tāvadasya vibhūṣaṇāni prāvṛtāni śobhāmātrāṁ janayanti |
youthful, with dark hair, possessing the freshness of youth, do those adornments, when put on him, [actually] adorn.
sbh1.3:12.26
na tvevaṁ punarjīrṇasya, vṛddhasya, mahallasyāśītikasya vā, nāvatikasya vā, śaṇḍadantasya, palitaśiraso, nānyatra tairvibhūṣaṇaiḥ prāvṛtaiḥ sa viḍamvita iva khyāti |
But not so for one aged, old, weak, octogenarian or nonagenarian, broken-toothed, gray-haired — on such a one those adornments, put on, appear on the contrary as if mocking [him].
sbh1.3:12.27
ārogyavyasane vā, bhogavyasane vā, jñātivyasane vā pratyupasthite na śobhate |
In sickness, in loss of wealth, in loss of kin — when these befall — he does not shine [in such adornments].
sbh1.3:12.28
śīlaṁ punaḥ sarveṣāṁ sarvakālañca śobhākāraṁ bhavati |
Virtue, however, is an adornment for all [people], at all times.
sbh1.3:12.29
tasmādalaṁkāraśabdenocyate |
Therefore it is spoken of by the word ‘ornament.’
sbh1.3:13.1
kena kāraṇenaśīlamanulepanaśabdenoktam |
For what reason is virtue spoken of by the word ‘ointment’?
sbh1.3:13.2
tatra bahukuśalamanavadyaṁ śīlasamādānaṁ sarvadauḥśīlyasamādānahetukaṁ kāyaparidāhaṁ cittaparidāhaṁ apanayati |
Here, the great wholesome, blameless undertaking of virtue removes the bodily and mental burning caused by every undertaking of evil [contrary to] virtue —
sbh1.3:13.3
gharmābhitaptasya uttamagrīṣmaparidāhe kāle pratyupasthite candanānulepanaṁ vā karpūrānulepanaṁ vā anena kāraṇena śīlamanulepanaśabdenocyate |
as for one scorched by heat in the great-summer-burning time, sandal ointment or camphor ointment [removes the burning]. For this reason virtue is spoken of by the word ‘ointment.’
sbh1.3:14.1
kena kāraṇena śīlaṁ gandhajātaśabdenocyate |
For what reason is virtue called ‘scent’?
sbh1.3:14.2
śīlavataḥ khalu puruṣapudgalasya digvidikṣu kalyāṇaḥ ṇaṁ kīrttiyaśaḥ śabdaśloko niścarati |
For a virtuous individual, in all directions and intermediate directions, fair fame, repute, voice, [and] eulogy go forth —
sbh1.3:14.3
vividhānāmvā mūlagandhajātānāṁ, sāragandhajātānāmvā, puṣpagandhānāmvāteritānāṁ digvidikṣu mana āpo gandho niścarati |
just as, of various kinds of root-scents, sap-scents, or flower-scents, when wafted in the directions, an agreeable scent goes forth.
sbh1.3:14.4
anena kāraṇena śīlaṁ gandhajātaśabdenocyate |
For this reason virtue is spoken of by the word ‘scent.’
sbh1.3:15.1
kena kāraṇena śīlaṁ sucaritaśabdenocyate |
For what reason is virtue called ‘good conduct’?
sbh1.3:15.2
sukhagāminī eṣā caryā svargagāminī sugatigāminī / eṣā caryā |
‘This conduct leads to ease, this conduct leads to heaven, leads to good destinies.’
sbh1.3:15.3
tasmāt sucariṁtamityucyate |
Therefore it is called good conduct.
sbh1.3:16.1
kena kāraṇena śīlaṁ samvaraśabdenocyate |
For what reason is virtue called ‘restraint’?
sbh1.3:16.2
nivṛttisvabhāva eṣa dharmo nivṛttilakṣaṇo viratisvabhāvaḥ |
This dharma has cessation [of unwholesome dharmas] as its own-nature, has the mark of cessation, has abstention as its own-nature.
sbh1.3:16.3
tasmāt samvaraśabdenocyate |
Therefore it is called by the word ‘restraint.’
sbh1.3:16.4
asya khalu śīlasamvarasya trividhā pratyavekṣā pariśuddhinimittaṁ |
Of this restraint of virtue, the threefold review is the sign of purification.
sbh1.3:16.5
katamā trividhā |
What threefold?
sbh1.3:16.6
yaduta kāyakarmapratyavekṣā, vākkarmapratyavekṣā, manaḥkarmapratyavekṣā |
Namely: review of bodily action, review of verbal action, review of mental action.
sbh1.3:16.7
tatra ca punaretāni karmāṇi pratyavekṣamāṇa ḥ śīlasamvaraṁ pariśodhayati |
Reviewing these [three kinds of] action, he purifies the restraint of virtue.
sbh1.3:16.8
yatkarma kāyena praṇihitaṁ bhavati karttuṁ tadeva pratyavekṣate |
Whatever action he intends to perform with the body, that he reviews [thus]:
sbh1.3:16.9
kinnu vyābādhikaṁ me etat kārya karma ātmanā antarāyaḥ pareṣā maku śalaṁ duḥkhodayaṁ duḥkhavipākamāhosvidavyābādhikaṁ me etat kāyakarmātmanaḥ pareṣāṁ kuśalaṁ sukhodayaṁ sukhavipākaṁ sa cetsa evaṁ pratyavekṣamāṇo jānāti vyābādhikaṁ me etatkāyakarmātmano vā, parasya vā akuśalaṁ rāgodayaṁ, rāgavipākaṁ, sa cetsa evaṁ pratyavekṣamāṇo na ca rati |
‘Is this bodily action of mine harmful to myself or others, unwholesome, giving rise to suffering, with painful result? Or is this bodily action of mine non-harmful to myself and others, wholesome, giving rise to ease, with pleasant result?’ If, reviewing thus, he knows: ‘this bodily action of mine is harmful to myself or another, unwholesome, giving rise to suffering, with painful result,’ then, having so reviewed, he does not engage in it;
sbh1.3:16.10
tatkarma na karoti |
that action he does not do,
sbh1.3:16.11
nānuprayacchati |
does not perform.
sbh1.3:16.12
sa cetpunarjānātyavyābādhikaṁ me etat kāyakarma kuśalaṁ pūrvavata sa karoti |
If, again, he knows: ‘this bodily action of mine is non-harmful, wholesome,’ [the rest] as before, that he does —
sbh1.3:16.13
tat kāyena karma na pratisaṁharati, anuprayacchati |
that bodily action he does not withhold, but performs.
sbh1.3:16.14
yadapyanenātītamadhvānamupādāya kāyena karma kṛtaṁ bhavati |
Even an action that, taking up the past, has already been done by him through the body —
sbh1.3:16.15
tadapyabhīkṣṇaṁ pratyavekṣate |
that too he repeatedly reviews:
sbh1.3:16.16
kintu vyābādhikaṁ me etat pūrvavat |
‘Was this [action] of mine harmful?’ [the rest] as before.
sbh1.3:16.17
sa vijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇāṁ sacet sa e vaṁ pratyavekṣamāṇo jānāti vyābādhikaṁ me etat karma pūrvavat |
[He reviews it] with [the help of] wise co-religionists. If, reviewing thus, he knows: ‘this action of mine is harmful,’ [the rest] as before,
sbh1.3:16.18
savijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇāmantike pratisaṁharati |
in the presence of wise co-religionists he withholds,
sbh1.3:16.19
anuprayacchati |
he performs [appropriately].
sbh1.3:16.20
yadapyanenātītamadhvānamupādāya kāyena karma kṛtaṁ bhavati |
Even an action that, taking up the past, has already been done by him through the body —
sbh1.3:16.21
tadapyabhīkṣṇaṁ pratyavekṣate |
that too he repeatedly reviews:
sbh1.3:16.22
kinnu vyābādhikaṁ me etat pūrvavat |
‘Was this [action] of mine harmful?’ [the rest] as before.
sbh1.3:16.23
savijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇā mantike sacet sa evaṁ pratyavekṣamāṇo jānāti vyābādhikaṁ me etat karma pūrvavat |
[He reviews it] with [the help of] wise co-religionists. If, reviewing thus, he knows: ‘this action of mine is harmful,’ [the rest] as before,
sbh1.3:16.24
sa vijñānāṁ sabrahmacāriṇāmantike pratideśayati, yathādharma pratikaroti |
in the presence of wise co-religionists he confesses, [and] makes amends according to Dharma.
sbh1.3:16.25
sa cet punarevaṁ pratyavekṣamāṇo jānātyavyābādhikaṁ me etat kāyakarma pūrvavat |
If, on the other hand, reviewing thus, he knows: ‘this bodily action of mine is non-harmful,’ [the rest] as before,
sbh1.3:16.26
sa tenaiva prītiprāmodyenāhorātrānuśikṣī bahulamviharatyevamasya tatkāyakarma supratyavekṣitaṁ ca bhavati |
by that very joy and gladness he dwells, day and night, much in training. Thus that bodily action of his is well reviewed
sbh1.3:16.27
suviśodhitaṁ ca |
and well purified,
sbh1.3:16.28
yadutātītānāgatapratyutpanneṣvadhvasu |
namely, in past, future, and present times.
sbh1.3:16.29
yathā kāyakarma evaṁ vākkarma veditavyaṁ |
As with bodily action, so verbal action is to be understood.
sbh1.3:16.30
atītān saṁskārān pratītyotpadyate manaḥ |
Mind arises dependent on past formations;
sbh1.3:16.31
anāgatān, pratyutpannān saṁskārān pratītyotpadyate manaḥ |
mind arises dependent on future and present formations.
sbh1.3:16.32
tanmano a bhīkṣṇaṁ pratyavekṣate |
That mind he repeatedly reviews:
sbh1.3:16.33
kinnu vyābādhikaṁ me etanmanaḥ pūrvavat |
‘Is this mind of mine harmful?’ [the rest] as before,
sbh1.3:16.34
yāvannotpādayati |
up through: he does not produce [it];
sbh1.3:16.35
pratisaṁharati, nānuprayacchati |
he withholds, does not perform,
sbh1.3:16.36
tanmanaskarma |
that mental action.
sbh1.3:16.37
śuklapakṣeṇa punarutpādayati, na pratisaṁharati, anuprayacchatitanmanaskarma |
Conversely, on the bright side, he produces [it], does not withhold, [but] performs that mental action.
sbh1.3:16.38
evamanena tanmanaḥ karma pratyavekṣitaṁ bhavati |
Thus that mental action is by him reviewed
sbh1.3:16.39
supariśodhitaṁ |
[and] well purified,
sbh1.3:16.40
yadutātītānāgatapratyupanneṣvadhvasu |
namely, in past, future, and present times.
sbh1.3:16.41
tatkasya hetoratīte a pyadhvani anāgate a pipratyutpanne a pi ye kecicchramaṇā vā, brāhmaṇā vā, kāyakarma, vākkakarma, manaskarma pratyavekṣya pariśodhya, pariśodhya, bahulaṁ vyāhārṣuḥ, sarve te evaṁ pratyavekṣya, pariśodhya ca |
For what reason? In past time, in future time, and in present time, whatever recluses or brahmins, having reviewed and purified bodily, verbal, and mental action, have lived extensively [in this way] — all of them have done so by such review and purification,
sbh1.3:16.42
yathoktaṁ bhagavatā āyuṣmantaṁ rāhulamārabhya |
as was said by the Blessed One concerning the Venerable Rāhula:
sbh1.3:16.43
kāyakarmātha vākkarma manaskarma ca rāhula |
‘Bodily action, then verbal action, and also mental action, Rāhula —
sbh1.3:16.44
abhīkṣṇaṁ pratyavekṣasva smaran buddhānuśāsanam |
repeatedly review [them], remembering the instruction of the Buddhas.
sbh1.3:17.1
etacchrāmaṇakaṁ karma atra śikṣasva rāhula |
This is the work of the recluse — train in this, Rāhula.
sbh1.3:17.2
atra te śikṣamāṇasya śreya eva na pāpakam |
For one training therein, there is only good, not evil.’
sbh1.3:18.1
tatra yadevaṁ vicinoti tat kāyakarma, vākkakarma, manaskarma kiṁ vyābādhikaṁ me iti vistareṇa pūrvavadiyaṁ pratyave kṣaṇā |
Here, that he investigates ‘bodily action, verbal action, mental action — is this harmful for me?’ [the rest] as before — this is the review.
sbh1.3:18.2
yatpunarekatyaṁ pratisaṁharati pratideśayatyekatyamanuprayacchati |
And that some [actions] he withholds and confesses, some he performs,
sbh1.3:18.3
tenaiva prītiprāmodyenāhorātrānuśikṣī bahulaṁ viharatīyamucyate pariśodhanā |
and by that very joy and gladness — training day and night — he dwells much [in training] — this is called purification.
sbh1.3:18.4
tatraivaṁ pariśuddhasya śīlasamvarasya daśānuśansā śaṁsā veditavyā ḥ |
Of the restraint of virtue thus purified, ten benefits are to be understood.
sbh1.3:18.5
katame daśa |
What ten?
sbh1.3:18.6
iha śīlavāṁ vān avipratisāriṇaḥ prāmodyaṁ pramuditacittasya prītirjāyate |
[1] Here a virtuous one, free of regret, gains gladness; in a gladdened [mind] joy arises;
sbh1.3:18.7
prītamanasaḥ kāyaḥpraśrabhyate |
with a joyful mind the body is tranquilized;
sbh1.3:18.8
praśrabdhakāyaḥ sukhaṁ vedayate |
with tranquilized body he feels pleasure;
sbh1.3:18.9
sukhitasya cittaṁ samādhīyate |
with pleased mind, the mind is concentrated;
sbh1.3:18.10
samāhitacitto yathābhūtaṁ prajānāti |
with concentrated mind he knows as it truly is,
sbh1.3:18.11
yathābhūtaṁ paśyati |
sees as it truly is;
sbh1.3:18.12
yathābhūtaṁ jānan paśyannirvidyate |
knowing-and-seeing as it truly is, he grows weary [of saṁsāra];
sbh1.3:18.13
nirviṇṇo virajyate, virakto vimucyate, vimuktasya vimukti ḥ smṛtirjñā vimuktijñā nadarśanaṁ bhavati |
wearied, he becomes dispassionate; dispassionate, he is liberated; for one liberated, the knowing-and-seeing of liberation arises;
sbh1.3:18.14
yāvannirupadhiśeṣe nirvāṇadhātau parinirvāti |
[and so on] up through: he attains final nirvāṇa in the nirvāṇa-element without remainder.
sbh1.3:18.15
yacchīlavān puruṣapudgalaḥ śīlaviśuddhaydhiyateyamavipratisāraṁ pratilabhate |
[Thus] a virtuous individual, by way of the purity of virtue, gains non-regret,
sbh1.3:18.16
anupūrveṇa yāvannirvāṇagamanāyāyaṁ prathamaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
and by gradual progression [proceeds] up to the going to nirvāṇa — this is the first benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:18.17
punaraparaṁ śīlavāṁ vān puruṣapudgalaḥ maraṇakālasamaye pratyupasthite, kṛtaṁ etanme sūktaṁ ta ...... sucaritaṁ kāyena vācā manasā na kṛtaṁ etanme duścaritaṁ kāyena pūrvavat |
[Damaged/Conjectural]: [2] Further, a virtuous individual, when the time of death is present, [reflects]: ‘good has been done by me, well [done]; good conduct of body, speech, [and] mind [has been done by me]; bad conduct of body [etc.] has not been done by me,’ [the rest] as before.
sbh1.3:18.18
iti yā gatiḥ |
‘Such is the destiny
sbh1.3:18.19
kṛtapuṇyānāṁ kṛtakuśalānāṁ kṛtabhayabhīrūprāṇānāṁ tāṁ gatiṁ pretya gamiṣyāmīti dvitīyamavipratisāraṁ pratilabhate |
of those who have done merit, done wholesome deeds, done deeds [out of] fearfulness about evil — to that destiny shall I, after death, go.’ He gains this second [kind of] non-regret,
sbh1.3:18.20
sugatigamanāya, avipratisāriṇo hi puruṣapudgalasya bhadrakaṁ maraṇaṁ bhavati |
[which leads] to a happy destiny. For a non-regretful individual, death is good,
sbh1.3:18.21
bhadrikā kālakriyā bhadrako a bhisamparāyaḥ |
the time of death is good, the after-state is good.
sbh1.3:18.22
ayaṁ dvitīyaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
This is the second benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:19.1
punaraparaṁ śīlavataḥ puruṣapudgalasya kalyāṇo varṇaḥ kīrtiryaśaḥ ḥ śabdaśloko niścarati |
[3] Further, of the virtuous individual, fair fame, repute, voice, [and] eulogy go forth.
sbh1.3:19.2
ayaṁ tṛtīyaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
This is the third benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:20.1
punaraparaṁ śīlavān puruṣapudgalaḥ sukhaṁ svapiti sukhaṁ pratiyujyate |
[4] Further, the virtuous individual sleeps comfortably and wakes comfortably,
sbh1.3:20.2
niṣparidāhena kāyena cittena cāyaṁ caturthaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
with body and mind free from burning. This is the fourth benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:20.3
punaraparaṁ śīlavāṁ vān puruṣapudgalaḥ supto a pi devānāṁ rakṣyo bhavati |
[5] Further, the virtuous individual, even when asleep, is guarded by the deities.
sbh1.3:20.4
ayaṁ pañcamaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
This is the fifth benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:21.1
punaraparaṁ śīlavān puruṣapudgalaḥ na śaṁkī bhavati |
[6] Further, the virtuous individual is not suspicious,
sbh1.3:21.2
parataḥ pāpasya, na bhīteśca saṁtrastamānasaḥ |
not afraid of [any] evil from outside, not of terrified mind.
sbh1.3:21.3
ayaṁ ṣaṣṭhaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
This is the sixth benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:22.1
punaraparaṁ śīlavāṁ vān puruṣapudgalaḥ badhakānāṁ pratyarthikānāmapi pra--trāṇāṁ chidraprāpto a pi rakṣyo bhavati |
[7] Further, the virtuous individual — even when openings [for attack] are sought by killers [and] enemies — is guarded;
sbh1.3:22.2
sarvadāyaṁ puruṣapudgala iti viditvā mitratāmvāpadyante te madhyamasthatāmvā |
knowing always ‘this individual is such [a virtuous one],’ they fall either into friendship with him or into a neutral disposition.
sbh1.3:22.3
ayaṁ saptamaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ |
This is the seventh benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:23.1
punaraparaṁ pūrvabahvāsthānānāṁ yakṣāṇāṁ nivāsikānāmamanuṣyāṇāṁ chidraprāpto a pi rakṣyo bhavati |
[8] Further, even when openings [for attack] are sought by [hostile] yakṣas of long standing or by amanuṣyas dwelling there, he is guarded —
sbh1.3:23.2
yaduta tadeva śīlamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā |
namely, by making that very virtue predominant.
sbh1.3:23.3
ayamaṣṭamaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
This is the eighth benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:24.1
punaraparaṁ śīlavān puruṣapudgalaḥ dharmeṇālpakṛcchreṇa parato lābhaṁ labhante labhate |
[9] Further, the virtuous individual, by Dharma [and] with little hardship, gains from others
sbh1.3:24.2
yaduta cīvarapiṇḍapātaśayanāsanaglānapratyayabhaiṣajyapariṣkārānyaduta śīlādhikā ka raṇahetoḥ satkṛtaśca bhavati |
robes, alms-food, lodging, medicine, [and other] requisites — that is, on account of virtue, he is honored
sbh1.3:24.3
gurukṛto rājñāṁ rājāmātrāṇāṁ naigamajānapadānāṁ dhanināṁ śreṣṭhināṁ sārthavāhānāṁ |
[and] esteemed by kings, royal ministers, townsmen, country-folk, the wealthy, the chiefs, [and] caravan leaders.
sbh1.3:24.4
ayaṁ navamaḥ śīlānuśansaḥ śaṁsaḥ |
This is the ninth benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:25.1
punaraparaṁ pūrvavatsarvapraṇidhānāni samṛdhyanti |
[10] Further, [as] above, all his vows succeed.
sbh1.3:25.2
sa cedākāṁkṣate kāmadhātau kṣatriyamahāsālakulānāṁ, brāhmaṇamahāsālakulānāmvā, gṛhapatimahāsālakulānāṁ vā cāturmahārājakāṇi kānāmvā cāturmahārājikāṇāṁ vā devānāṁ, trā tra yastriṁśānāmvā, yāmānāṁ, tuṣitānāṁ, nirmāṇaratīnāṁ, paranirmitavaśavarttināṁ devānāṁ sabhāgatoyopapaho sabhāgatāyāmupapanno yathāpi tadviśuddhatvācchīlānāṁ samadānāṁ atyarthajātaṁ dhyānāni ca samāpadya dṛṣṭe dharme sukhaṁ vihareyaṁ |
If he wishes, [saying]: ‘In the realm of desire, may I be born in the company of great kṣatriya-mahāsāla families, or great brāhmaṇa-mahāsāla families, or great householder-mahāsāla families, or among the deities of the Four Great Kings, the Trāyastriṁśa, Yāma, Tuṣita, Nirmāṇarati, or Paranirmitavaśavartin; or, because of the purity of well-undertaken virtue and through entering the dhyānas, may I dwell at ease in the visible-here-and-now;
sbh1.3:25.3
rūpopamānāṁ ca devānāṁ sabhāgatāyopapadyeya yāmupasampadyeya vihare ya eta dyaśa etacca śīlavato vītarāgasya praṇidhānaṁ samṛdhyati |
and may I be born in the company of the form-realm deities and dwell [there]’ — this aspiration and vow of the virtuous, lust-free one succeeds.
sbh1.3:25.4
sa cedākāṁkṣate |
If he wishes,
sbh1.3:25.5
.......... vimokṣā ............ syādvopasampadya vihareyaṁ |
[Damaged/Conjectural]: ‘[the eight] liberations [vimokṣa] may I attain and dwell [in them]’;
sbh1.3:25.6
ārūpyopagatānāmvā devā nāṁ sa bhāgatāyo yāmu papadyeya pūrvavat |
or ‘in the company of the formless-realm deities may I be born,’ [the rest] as before.
sbh1.3:26.1
sa cedākāṁkṣate atyantaniṣṭhanirvāṇamadhigaccheyamityadhigacchati |
If he wishes, ‘may I attain nirvāṇa as the ultimate end,’ he attains [it].
sbh1.3:27.1
tadgati śuddhatvācchīlānāṁ sarvatra ca vītarāgasya |
[This occurs] because his virtue is purified in that direction, and because he is dispassionate in every respect.
sbh1.3:27.2
ayaṁ daśamaḥ śīlānuśaṁso veditavyaḥ |
This is to be understood as the tenth benefit of virtue.
sbh1.3:28.1
nirdiṣṭaḥ śīlaskandho vibhāgaśaḥ, nirdiṣṭā vipattisampattiḥ |
[Thus] the virtue-aggregate has been set forth by [its] division; the ruin and the fullness [of virtue] have been set forth;
sbh1.3:28.2
nirdiṣṭāni paryāyanāmāni |
the synonyms have been set forth;
sbh1.3:28.3
nirdiṣṭā pariśuddhipratyavekṣā, nirdiṣṭo a nuśaṁsaḥ |
the review constituting purification has been set forth, [and] the benefit has been set forth.
sbh1.3:29.1
sa eṣa sarvākāraparipūrṇaḥ śīlasamvaraḥ saṁbhāraparigṛhīta ākhyātaḥ kathimo to vivṛtaḥ prakāśito yatrātmakāmaiḥ śrāmaṇyabrāhmaṇyakāmaiḥ kulaputraiḥ śikṣitavyaṁ |
This wholly complete restraint of virtue, included among the requisites, has been declared, set forth, opened up, [and] made manifest — in it sons of good family, desirous of their own good and of asceticism and brahminhood [brāhmaṇya], should train.
sbh1.3:30.1
uddānaṁ |
Summary verse:
sbh1.3:31.1
vibhaṁgastrividho jñeyaḥ sampad daśavidhā bhavet |
The division is to be known as threefold, the fullness as tenfold,
sbh1.3:31.2
paryāyaśca ṣaḍākāro viśuddhistrividhā matā |
the synonyms as six-fold; the purification is held as threefold,
sbh1.3:31.3
anuśanso śaṁso daśavidhaḥ eṣo ’sau śīlasamvaraḥ |
the benefit as tenfold — such is the restraint of virtue.
sbh1.3:32.1
indriyasamvaraḥ katamaḥ |
What is restraint of the senses?
sbh1.3:32.2
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ indriyairguptadvāro viharatyārakṣitasmṛtirnipakasmṛtiritti vistaraḥ |
When someone here dwells with guarded sense-doors, with mindfulness guarded, with alert mindfulness — [as elaborated in the standard formula].
sbh1.3:32.3
tatra kathamindriyairguptadvāro viharatyārakṣitasmṛtirbhavati |
How does he dwell with guarded sense-doors, with mindfulness guarded?
sbh1.3:32.4
nipakasmṛtiriti vistareṇa yāvadrarakṣati mana indriyaṁ mana indriyeṇa |
[And how] with alert mindfulness — [as elaborated] up through: he guards the mind-faculty by means of the mind-faculty.
sbh1.3:32.5
sa evaṁ samāpadyate |
He attains [the state] thus.
sbh1.3:32.6
evamindriyairguptadvāro viharati |
Thus he dwells with guarded sense-doors.
sbh1.3:32.7
tatra kathamārakṣitasmṛtirbhavati |
How, then, is his mindfulness guarded?
sbh1.3:32.8
yathāpīhaikatyenendriyaguptadvāratāmevādhipatiṁ kṛtvā śrutamudgṛhītaṁ bhavati |
When someone here, taking the very [practice of] guarded sense-doors as predominant, holds firmly to what has been heard,
sbh1.3:32.9
cintitamvā punarbhāvitamvā |
reflected upon, or cultivated.
sbh1.3:32.10
tena ca śrutacintābhāvanādhipateyā smṛtiḥ pratilabdhā bhavati |
And by that, he attains a mindfulness whose predominant ground is hearing, reflection, and cultivation.
sbh1.3:32.11
sa tasyā eva smṛteḥ pratilabdhāyāḥ asaṁpramoṣārthamadhigamārthamavināśārthaṁ kālena kālaṁ tasminneva śrute yogaṁ karotyabhyāsaṁ karoti, cintāyāṁbhāvanāyāṁ yogamabhyāsaṁ karoti |
Of that mindfulness once attained — so that it is not forgotten, is firmly grasped, is not lost — he applies effort and practice, from time to time, on that same teaching he heard, and he applies effort and practice in reflecting on it and cultivating it.
sbh1.3:32.12
na bhavati sa sta prayogā yi- kṛta prayoga evamanena tasyā ḥ śrutasamudāgamatā gatā yāścintā-samudāgatāyāḥ smṛteḥ kālena kālaṁ śrutacintābhāvanāyogakriyāyā ārakṣā kṛtā bhavati |
He does not let his application drop. Thus, of the mindfulness born from hearing, reflection, and cultivation, the guarding is performed from time to time through the practice of those very three.
sbh1.3:32.13
evamārakṣitasmṛtirbhavati |
Thus he becomes one of guarded mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.14
kathaṁ nipakasmṛtirbhavati |
How does he have alert mindfulness?
sbh1.3:32.15
sa tasyāmeva smṛtau nityakārī ca bhavati |
He becomes one constantly active in that very mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.16
niryāṇakārī ca bhavati |
And one acting toward going-forth.
sbh1.3:32.17
tatra yā nityakāritā iyamucyate sātatyakāritā |
Of these, nityakāritā (constant action) is what is called sātatya-kāritā (continual action).
The four-term cluster nityakāritā / sātatya-kāritā / niryāṇa-kāritā / satkṛtya-kāritā — 'constant action / continual action / action toward going-forth / action done with respect' — pairs each term in the question (32.15–16) with its definitional synonym (32.17–18). The Sanskrit iyam ucyate X construction here re-glosses the question's term, so 'constant' is defined as 'continual' and 'going-forth' is defined as 'with-respect'. Translation makes the pairing explicit so the reader can see the gloss structure rather than reading 32.17–18 as separate vague claims.
sbh1.3:32.18
tatra yā niryāṇakāritā iyamucyate satkṛtyakāritā |
Niryāṇa-kāritā (action toward going-forth) is what is called satkṛtya-kāritā (action done with respect).
sbh1.3:32.19
sa evaṁ sātatyakārī satkṛtyakārī nipakasmṛtirityucyate |
He who acts thus — continually and with respect — is called one of alert mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.20
sa tathārakṣitasmṛtirbhavati |
He thus has guarded mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.21
tathā tāṁ smṛtiṁ na saṁpramoṣayati |
Thus he does not lose that mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.22
sa tathānipakasmṛtirbhavati |
He thus has alert mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.23
tathā tasyāmevāpramuṣitāyāṁ smṛtau balādhānaprāpto bhavati |
Thus in that very unlost mindfulness he attains the establishment of power.
sbh1.3:32.24
yena śakto bhavati pratibalaśca rūpāṇāmabhibhavāya śabdānāṁ, gandhānāṁ, rasānāṁ, spraṣṭavyānāṁ, dharmāṇāmabhibhavāya |
By which he becomes able and fit for the overcoming of forms, sounds, smells, tastes, tangibles, dharmas.
sbh1.3:32.25
kathaṁ smṛtyārakṣitamānaso bhavati |
How is his mind guarded by mindfulness?
sbh1.3:32.26
cakṣuḥ pratītya rūpāṇi cotpadyate |
Depending on the eye and forms, there arises
sbh1.3:32.27
cakṣurvi jñānaṁ, ca - kṣurvijñānānantaramutpadyate |
eye-consciousness; immediately after eye-consciousness there arises
sbh1.3:32.28
vikalpakaṁ manovijñānaṁ yena vikalpakena manovijñānena priyarūpeṣu rūpeṣu saṁrajyate |
discriminative mind-consciousness; by that discriminative mind-consciousness, one is drawn toward pleasing forms
sbh1.3:32.29
apriyarūpeṣu rūpeṣu vyāpadyate |
and turns away from displeasing forms.
sbh1.3:32.30
sa bhā tā mevādhipatiṁ kṛtvā tasmādayoniśo vikalpāt saṁkleśasamutthāpakāttasmāt saṁrakṣati |
Taking [the discriminative mind-consciousness] itself as predominant, he guards [the mind] against the unwise discrimination that produces defilement,
sbh1.3:32.31
yathā saṁkleśo notpadyate |
so that defilement does not arise.
sbh1.3:32.32
evaṁ śrotraṁ ghrāṇaṁ jihvāṁ kāyaṁ manaḥ pratītya dharmāścotpadyate manovijñānaṁ |
Likewise, depending on ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind together with their respective objects, mind-consciousness arises.
sbh1.3:32.33
tacca manovijñānamasyāyoniśevikalpasahagataṁ saṁkleśasamutthāpakaṁ |
And that mind-consciousness, accompanied by unwise discrimination, produces defilement,
sbh1.3:32.34
yena priyarūpeṣu dharmeṣu saṁrajyate |
by which one is drawn toward pleasing dharmas
sbh1.3:32.35
apriyarūpeṣu dharmeṣu vyāpadyate |
and turns away from displeasing dharmas.
sbh1.3:32.36
sa tasmādayoniśovikalpāt saṁkleśasamutthāpakāttanmānasaṁ rakṣatyevamasya saṁkleśo notpadyate |
He guards that mental state against the unwise discrimination that produces defilement — and thus defilement does not arise in him.
sbh1.3:32.37
evaṁ smṛtyārakṣitamānasobhavati |
Thus he becomes one whose mind is guarded by mindfulness.
sbh1.3:32.38
kathaṁ samāvasthāvacārako bhavati |
How is he one who maintains a balanced state?
samāvasthā-avacāraka — literally 'one who explores / operates within an even state.' Rendered here as 'one who maintains a balanced state.' The earlier draft used 'acting in the state of equipoise,' which is technically defensible but sounded liturgical; 'balanced state' carries the same conceptual content with less ornament. Distinct from upekṣā (equanimity) proper — upekṣā names the affective neutrality, samāvasthā-avacāraka names the stable mode of operating from within it.
sbh1.3:32.39
samāvasthocyate |
‘Balanced state’ here means:
sbh1.3:32.40
upekṣā kuśalā vā, avyākṛtā vā |
equanimity, wholesome or indeterminate.
sbh1.3:32.41
sa tasmādayoniśo vikalpāt saṁkleśasamutthāpakāt tanmānasaṁ rakṣitvā kuśalāyāmvā upekṣāyāmavyākṛtāyāmvā avacārayati |
Having guarded that mental state from the unwise discrimination that produces defilement, he settles it in wholesome equanimity or in indeterminate equanimity.
sbh1.3:32.42
tenocyate samāvasthāvacārakaḥ |
Therefore he is called one who maintains a balanced state.
sbh1.3:32.43
evaṁ samāvasthāvacārako bhavati |
Thus he becomes one who maintains a balanced state.
sbh1.3:32.44
kathaṁ punastasmādayoniśo vikalpasaṁkleśasamutthāpakān mānasaṁ rakṣati |
And how, again, does he guard the mental state from the unwise discrimination that produces defilement?
sbh1.3:32.45
na nimittagrāhī bhavati |
He does not become a seizer of signs.
sbh1.3:32.46
teṣu rūpeṣu, śabdeṣu, gandheṣu, raseṣu, spraṣṭavyeṣu, dharmeṣu nānuvyaṁjanagrāhī bhavati yato a dhikaraṇamasya pāpakā akuśalā dharmāścittamanusraveyuḥ |
In those forms, sounds, smells, tastes, tangibles, and dharmas he does not become a seizer of secondary features — that on account of which evil unwholesome dharmas would flow into his mind.
sbh1.3:32.47
sa cet punaḥ smṛtisaṁpramoṣāt kleśapracuratayā vā vivarjayato a pi nimittagrāhama ho ’ nuvyaṁjanagrāha ḥ sa mutpadyate eva |
If, even so, through lapse of mindfulness or through the strength of his defilements, sign-seizing or secondary-feature-seizing arises despite his avoidance,
sbh1.3:32.48
pāpakā akuśalāto duḥgṛhīto pāpakādakuśalato durgṛhīto bhavati ke ye dharmā anusarantyeva |
and the evil unwholesome dharmas — ill-grasped — indeed follow upon them,
sbh1.3:32.49
cittaṁ teṣāṁ samvarāya pratipadyate |
he then applies the mind to their restraint.
sbh1.3:32.50
ābhyāṁ dvābhyāmākārābhyāṁ tasmāt saṁkleśasamutthāpakādayoniśovikalpāttanmānasaṁ rakṣitaṁ bhavati |
By these two modes, the mental state is guarded from the unwise discrimination that produces defilement.
sbh1.3:33.1
kathaṁ ca punastanmānasamābhyāmākārābhyāṁ saraṁkṣya kuśalāyāmvā upekṣāyāmavadhārayatyavyā kṛtāyāmvā |
And how, having guarded the mental state by these two modes, does he settle it in wholesome or indeterminate equanimity?
sbh1.3:33.2
dvābhyāmevākārābhyāṁ |
By two modes again.
sbh1.3:33.3
katamābhyāṁ dvābhyāṁ |
Which two?
sbh1.3:33.4
yathāha rakṣati cakṣurindriyaṁ cakṣurindriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
As [the standard formula] says: ‘he guards the eye-faculty, attains restraint by the eye-faculty,’
sbh1.3:33.5
yathā cakṣurindriyaṁ cakṣurindriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
and just as ‘he guards the eye-faculty, attains restraint by the eye-faculty,’
sbh1.3:33.6
me e vaṁ śrotraghrāṇajihvākāyāṁ yān , rakṣati mana indriyaṁ mana indriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
so for the ear, nose, tongue, and body — and he guards the mind-faculty, attains restraint by the mind-faculty.
sbh1.3:33.7
ābhyāṁ dvābhyomākārābhyāṁ kuśalāyāmvā, avyākṛtāyāmvā upekṣāyāṁ tanmānasameva cārayati |
By these two modes, he settles that mental state in wholesome or indeterminate equanimity.
sbh1.3:34.1
kathaṁ cakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu na nimittagrāhī bhavati |
How, with respect to the forms cognizable by the eye, does he not become a seizer of signs?
sbh1.3:34.2
nimittagrāha ucyate |
‘Seizing of sign’ here means:
sbh1.3:34.3
yaccakṣurvijñānagocaro rūpe tasya gocarasya grāhī bhavati |
of the form which is the range of eye-consciousness, becoming a seizer of that range
sbh1.3:34.4
cakṣurvijñānena |
by means of eye-consciousness.
sbh1.3:34.5
evaṁ nimittagrāhī bhavati |
That is what it means to become a seizer of signs.
sbh1.3:34.6
yaduta cakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu śrotraghrāṇajih vākāyamanovijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu aparā jāti r nimittaṁ |
Namely, with respect to forms cognizable by the eye — and likewise with respect to objects cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind — the sign is a further class [of object].
sbh1.3:34.7
sa cet punastaṁ gocaraṁ parivarjayati |
If, then, he avoids that range —
sbh1.3:34.8
cakṣurvijñānasyaivaṁ na ca na nimittagrāhī bha vati |
[the range] of eye-consciousness too — he does not become a seizer of signs
sbh1.3:34.9
cakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣvevaṁ śrotra ghrāṇajihvākāyamanovijñeyeṣu dharmeṣu |
in forms cognizable by the eye, and likewise in dharmas cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind-consciousness.
sbh1.3:34.10
kathaṁ nānuvyaṁjanagrāhībhavati |
How does he not become a seizer of secondary features?
sbh1.3:34.11
cakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu |
[That is, with respect to] forms cognizable by the eye.
sbh1.3:34.12
anuvyaṁjanagrāha ucyate |
‘Seizing of secondary feature’ here means:
sbh1.3:34.13
yasteṣvevacakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu cakṣurvijñānasyaiva samanantarasahotpannasya vikalpakasya manovijñānasya yo gocaraḥ saṁrāgāya vā, saṁdveṣāya, vā saṁmohāya vā taṁ gocaraṁ parivarjayati |
