in those very forms cognizable by the eye, the range of the discriminative mind-consciousness that arises immediately along with eye-consciousness — when [that range tends] toward lust, toward hatred, or toward delusion — he avoids that range,
sbh1.3:34.14
notpādayati tadālambanaṁ |
does not produce that object
sbh1.3:34.15
tanmanovijñānamevaṁ nānuvyaṁjanagrāhī bhavati |
for that mind-consciousness — and thus he does not become a seizer of secondary features
sbh1.3:34.16
yaduta cakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu |
in forms cognizable by the eye,
sbh1.3:34.17
evaṁ śrotraghrāṇajih vākāyamanovijñeyeṣu dharmeṣu aparā jātirnimittagrāhasyānuvyaṁjanagrāhasya ca |
or in dharmas cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind. This is the further class of sign-seizing and secondary-feature-seizing.
sbh1.3:34.18
tatra nimittagrāho yaccakṣuṣā rupāṇyābhāsagatāni tajjaṁ manaskāraṁ saṁmukhīkṛtya paśyati |
Concretely: sign-seizing is when, having brought to presence the attention born from it, he sees with the eye the forms that have appeared.
sbh1.3:34.19
tatrānuvyaṁjanagrāhaḥ |
And the seizing of secondary features [proceeds as follows]:
sbh1.3:34.20
tānyeva rūpāṇi cakṣuṣā ābhāsagatāni tajjaṁ manasikāraṁ saṁmukhīkṛtya paśyati |
Those same forms that have appeared to the eye: having brought to presence the attention born from them, he sees them,
sbh1.3:34.21
apitu parato a nusravapūrvakaṁ śrṛṇoti |
but further, hears about them from another in a follow-up way:
sbh1.3:34.22
santyevaṁ rūpāṇyevaṁ rūpāṇi cakṣurvijñeyāni rūpāṇīti yāni tāni tadanugatāni nāmāni padāni vyaṁjanāni yānya dhipatiṁ kṛtvā, yāni niśritya pratiṣṭhāyāyaṁ puruṣapudgalaḥ yathāśrutāni cakṣurvijñeyāni rūpāṇi vikalpayatyayamucyate |
‘Such are these forms — these eye-cognizable forms’ — and the names, words, and letters that follow upon those [reports], making them predominant, relying on them, established in them, this person discriminates the eye-cognizable forms in accord with what he has heard. This is called
sbh1.3:34.23
anuvyaṁjanagrāhaḥ |
seizing of secondary features.
sbh1.3:34.24
yathā cakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu, evaṁ śrotraghrāṇajih vākāyamanovijñeyeṣu dharmeṣu veditavyaḥ |
As with eye-cognizable forms, so it is to be understood with dharmas cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, mind.
sbh1.3:34.25
sa punarayaṁ nimittagrāho a nuvyaṁjanagrāhaśca asti yannidānamasya yadadhikaraṇaṁ yadadhipateyaṁ asya pāpakā akuśalā dharmāścittamanusravanti |
Such sign-seizing and secondary-feature-seizing — when there is some [form] on account of which, with respect to which, and by means of which as the predominant condition, his evil unwholesome dharmas flow into his mind:
sbh1.3:34.26
asti yacca tannidānaṁ ca tadadhikaraṇaṁ ca tadadhipateyaṁ pāpakā akuśalā dharmā ścittamanusravanti |
where there is such a cause, such an occasion, such a predominant condition for evil unwholesome dharmas to flow into the mind,
sbh1.3:34.27
tatra yo a yaṁ nimittagrāho a nuvyaṁjanagrāho ayoniśogrāhaḥ yannidānaṁ yadadhikaraṇaṁ yadadhipateyamasya pāpakā akuśalā dharmāścittamanusravanti |
the sign-seizing, the secondary-feature-seizing, the unwise seizing — that which is the cause, the occasion, the predominant condition for his evil unwholesome dharmas to flow into the mind:
sbh1.3:34.28
tadrūpamasau nimittagrāhamanuvyaṁjanagrāhaṁ ca parivarjayati |
that very form of sign-seizing and secondary-feature-seizing he avoids.
sbh1.3:34.29
pāpakā akuśalā dharmāḥ katame |
What are evil unwholesome dharmas?
sbh1.3:34.30
rāgaḥ, rāgasamutthāpitaṁ kāyaduścaritaṁ, vāgduścaritaṁ, manoduścaritaṁ |
Lust, and the bodily, verbal, and mental misconduct produced by lust;
sbh1.3:34.31
dveṣo, mohaḥ |
hatred and delusion,
sbh1.3:34.32
, mohasamutthāpitaṁ ca kāyaduścaritaṁ, vāgduścaritaṁ, manoduścaritamima ucyante pāpakā akuśalā dharmāḥ |
and the bodily, verbal, and mental misconduct produced by [hatred and] delusion — these are called evil unwholesome dharmas.
sbh1.3:34.33
kathamete cittamanusravanti |
How do they ‘flow into the mind’?
sbh1.3:34.34
yadālambanaṁ cittamanovijñānamutpadyate |
Mind-consciousness arises with some object —
sbh1.3:34.35
gacchati pratisarati |
it goes [there], it follows along.
sbh1.3:34.36
tadālambanāstadālambanāstena cittamanovijñānena saṁprayuktāḥ ḥ kāyavāṅmanoduścaritasamutthāpakā s te rāgadveṣamohā utpadyante, gacchanti pratisaranti |
Associated with that object of mind-consciousness, the bodily-, verbal-, and mental-misconduct-producing lust, hatred, and delusion arise, go, follow along.
sbh1.3:34.37
tenocyante te cittamanupravanti |
Therefore they are said to ‘flow into the mind.’
sbh1.3:35.1
evaṁ tāvannimittagrāheṇānuvyaṁjanagrāheṇa ca ya utpadyate, saṁkleśaścakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu yāvanmanovijñeyeṣu dharmeṣu so a sya notpadyate nimittagrāhamanuvyaṁjanagrāhaṁ ca parivarjayataḥ |
Thus, the defilement that would arise — through sign-seizing or secondary-feature-seizing — in eye-cognizable forms (and so on up to mind-cognizable dharmas) does not arise for one who avoids sign-seizing and secondary-feature-seizing.
sbh1.3:35.2
sa cet punaḥ smṛtisaṁpramoṣādvā, kleśapracuratayā vā, ekākino a pi viharataḥ pūrvadṛṣṭāni cakṣurvijñeyāni rūpāṇyadhipatiṁ kṛtvā pūrvānubhūtāṁ tān śrotraghrāṇajihvākāyamanovijñeyān dharmānadhipatiṁ kṛtvotpadyante pāpakā akuśalā dharmā s tānutpannānadhivāsayati, prajahāti, viśodhayati, vyantīkaroti |
If, even so, through lapse of mindfulness or through the strength of his defilements, evil unwholesome dharmas arise even while he is dwelling alone — taking previously seen eye-cognizable forms as predominant, or taking previously experienced dharmas cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind as predominant — once they have arisen he withstands them, abandons them, purifies them, and puts an end to them.
The response to arisen unwholesome dharmas is positive, not negative: adhivāsayati, prajahāti, viśodhayati, vyantīkaroti means he withstands/endures them, abandons them, purifies them, and puts an end to them. The prior 'does not accommodate them' inserted a negation not present in the Sanskrit.
sbh1.3:35.3
tenocyate teṣāṁ samvarāya pratipadyate |
Therefore he is said to apply himself to their restraint.
sbh1.3:35.4
sa yeṣu rūpeṣu cakṣuḥ prerayitavyaṁ bhavati |
On forms toward which the eye is [properly] to be directed,
sbh1.3:35.5
yeṣu śrotraghrāṇajih vākāyamanovijñeyeṣu dharmeṣu manaḥ prerayitavyaṁ bhavati |
and on dharmas cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind toward which the mind is [properly] to be directed,
sbh1.3:35.6
teṣu tathā prerayati |
to those he directs [his faculties],
sbh1.3:35.7
yathā na saṁkliśyate |
in such a way that he is not defiled.
sbh1.3:35.8
evamanena tasmāt saṁkleśānmana indriyaṁ rakṣitaṁ bhavati |
Thus the mind-faculty is guarded by him from that defilement.
sbh1.3:35.9
tenocyate rakṣati mana indriyaṁ |
Therefore it is said: ‘he guards the mind-faculty.’
sbh1.3:35.10
yeṣu punaścakṣurvijñeyeṣu rūpeṣu cakśurindriyaṁ na prerayitavyaṁ bhavati |
On forms cognizable by the eye toward which the eye-faculty is not to be directed,
sbh1.3:35.11
yeṣu śrotraghrāṇajih vākāyamanovijñeyeṣu dha rmeṣu mana indriyaṁ na prerayitavyaṁ bhavati |
and on dharmas cognizable by ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind toward which the mind-faculty is not to be directed —
sbh1.3:35.12
teṣu sarveṇa sarvaṁ sarvathā na prerayati |
to those he does not direct [his faculties] at all, in any way.
sbh1.3:35.13
tenocyatecakṣurindriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
Therefore it is said: ‘he attains restraint by the eye-faculty,’
sbh1.3:35.14
tenocyate yāvanmana indriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
and so on up through ‘he attains restraint by the mind-faculty.’
sbh1.3:35.15
ayaṁ tāvadvibhaṁgo vistareṇendriyasamvarasya vijñeya ḥ |
This much is the detailed exposition of sense-restraint, to be understood.
sbh1.3:35.16
samāsārthaḥ |
In summary:
sbh1.3:35.17
yena ca saṁvṛṇoti, yataśca saṁvṛṇoti, yathā ca saṁvṛṇoti, yā cāsau saṁvṛtiḥ |
by what one restrains, from what one restrains, how one restrains, and what the restraint itself is —
sbh1.3:35.18
tatsarvamekatyamabhisaṁkṣipyendriyasamvara ityucyate |
all this gathered into one is called restraint of the senses.
sbh1.3:35.19
tatra kena saṁvṛṇoti |
By what does he restrain?
sbh1.3:35.20
yā ārakṣitā ca smṛtistayā saṁvṛṇoti |
By guarded mindfulness — by that he restrains.
sbh1.3:35.21
kiṁ saṁvṛṇoti |
What does he restrain?
sbh1.3:35.22
cakṣurindriyaṁ saṁvṛṇoti |
He restrains the eye-faculty;
sbh1.3:35.23
śrotraghrāṇajih vākāyamanaindriyaṁ saṁvṛṇoti |
he restrains the ear-, nose-, tongue-, body-, and mind-faculty.
sbh1.3:35.24
idaṁ saṁvṛṇoti |
This is what he restrains.
sbh1.3:35.25
kutaḥ saṁvṛṇoti |
From what does he restrain?
sbh1.3:35.26
priyarūpāpriyarūpebhyo rūpebhyaḥ śabdebhyo yāvaddharmebhyo a taḥ saṁvṛṇoti saṁvṛṇoti |
From pleasing and displeasing forms, sounds, and so on up through dharmas — from these he restrains.
sbh1.3:35.27
kathaṁ saṁvṛṇoti |
How does he restrain?
sbh1.3:35.28
na nimittagrāhī bhavati nānuvyaṁjanagrāhī yato a dhikaraṇameva pāpakā akuśalā samvaramāpadyate |
He does not become a seizer of signs, does not become a seizer of secondary features — that on account of which evil unwholesome [dharmas would arise]; he attains restraint [from them].
sbh1.3:35.29
ityevaṁ saṁvṛṇoti |
Thus he restrains.
sbh1.3:35.30
kā punaḥ saṁvṛtiḥ |
And what is the restraint itself?
sbh1.3:35.31
yataḥ smṛtyārakṣitamānaso bhavati |
That by which he is one whose mind is guarded by mindfulness,
sbh1.3:35.32
samāvasthāvacārakaḥ |
and one who maintains a balanced state —
sbh1.3:35.33
iyamucyate saṁvṛtiḥ |
this is called restraint.
sbh1.3:35.34
punaraparaḥ samāsārthaḥ |
A further summary meaning:
sbh1.3:35.35
yaścasamvaropāyaḥ |
the means of restraint,
sbh1.3:35.36
yacca samvaraṇīyamvastu, yā ca saṁvṛtiḥ |
the object to be restrained, and the restraint itself —
sbh1.3:35.37
tadekatyamabhisaṁkṣipyendriyasamvara ityucyate |
these gathered into one are called restraint of the senses.
sbh1.3:35.38
tatra katamaḥ samvarovā saṁvaropāyaḥ |
What is the means of restraint?
sbh1.3:35.39
yadāha ārakṣitasmṛtirbhavati, nipakasmṛtiriti cakṣuṣā rūpāṇi dṛṣṭvā na nimittagrāhī bhavati, nānuvyaṁjanagrāhī, yāvanmanasā dharmān vijñāya na nimittagrāhī bhavati, nānuvyaṁjanagrāhī |
What is meant by [the standard formula]: ‘he becomes one of guarded mindfulness, alert mindfulness; seeing forms with the eye, he does not become a seizer of signs, does not become a seizer of secondary features — up through: cognizing dharmas with the mind, he does not become a seizer of signs, does not become a seizer of secondary features’ —
sbh1.3:35.40
yato a dhikaraṇameva pāpakā akuśalā dharmāścittamanusravanti |
‘that on account of which evil unwholesome dharmas would flow into the mind,
sbh1.3:35.41
teṣāṁ samvarāya pratipadyate |
to their restraint he applies himself;
sbh1.3:35.42
rakṣatīndriyamindriyeṇa samvaramāpadyate |
he guards the faculty, attains restraint by the faculty.’
sbh1.3:35.43
ayamucyate samvaropāyaḥ |
This is called the means of restraint.
sbh1.3:35.44
samvaraṇīyaṁ vastu katamat |
What is the object to be restrained?
sbh1.3:35.45
cakṣūrūpaṁ caivaṁ yāvanmanodharmāścedamucyate samvaraṇīyaṁ vastu |
Eye-and-form, and so on up through mind-and-dharmas — this is called the object to be restrained.
sbh1.3:35.46
tatra saṁvṛtiḥ katamā |
And what is the restraint itself?
sbh1.3:35.47
yadāha |
What [the formula] expresses by:
sbh1.3:35.48
smṛtyārakṣitamānaso bhavati |
‘he is one whose mind is guarded by mindfulness,
sbh1.3:35.49
samāvasthāvacāraka itīyamucyate saṁvṛtiḥ |
one who maintains a balanced state’ — this is called restraint.
sbh1.3:35.50
sa khalvayamindriyasamvaraḥ samāsato dvividhaḥ |
This restraint of the senses is, in brief, of two kinds:
The twofold distinction here is doctrinally important: pratisaṁkhyāna-bala (the power of reflective discrimination) is the analytical force that recognizes danger in sense-objects without yet uprooting it; bhāvanā-bala (the power of cultivation) actually uproots the latent tendency (anuśaya) at its karmic root. The first kind belongs to the path of requisites (saṁbhāra-mārga) — pre-realization. The second is operative within the dispassion-stage proper, i.e., from the path of seeing onward.
sbh1.3:35.51
pratisaṁkhyānabalasaṁgṛhī to bhāvanābalaṁsaṁgṛhī taśca |
that grounded in the power of reflective discrimination (pratisaṁkhyāna-bala), and that grounded in the power of cultivation (bhāvanā-bala).
sbh1.3:35.52
tatra pratisaṁkhyānabala saṁgṛhīto yena viṣyeṣvādīnavaṁ paśyati |
Of these, the one grounded in the power of reflective discrimination is that by which he sees danger in the sense-objects,
sbh1.3:35.53
no tu tamādīnavaṁ vyapakarṣati |
but does not yet remove that danger,
sbh1.3:35.54
prajahāti |
does not yet abandon it.
sbh1.3:35.55
tatra bhā vanābalasaṁgṛhīto yena viṣyeṣvādīnavaṁ paśyati, taṁ ca punarādīnavaṁ vyapakarṣati |
The one grounded in the power of cultivation is that by which he sees danger in the sense-objects and also removes that danger,
sbh1.3:35.56
prajahāti |
abandons it.
sbh1.3:35.57
tatra pratisaṁkhyānabalasaṁgṛhītenendriyasamvareṇa viṣayālambanaṁ kleśaparyavasthānaṁ notpādayati, na saṁmukhīkaroti |
By sense-restraint grounded in the power of reflective discrimination, the obsession of defilement that takes sense-objects as its object does not arise, does not come to manifest —
sbh1.3:35.58
na caivāśa śra yasanniviṣṭamanuśayaṁ pra jahā ti, samudghātayati |
but he does not yet abandon, does not yet eradicate, the latent tendency settled in the [karmic] basis.
sbh1.3:35.59
tatra bhāvanābalasaṁgṛhīte ne ndriyasamvareṇa viṣayālambanaṁ ca kleśaparyavasthānaṁ notpādayati, na sammukhīkaroti |
By sense-restraint grounded in the power of cultivation, that same obsession of defilement does not arise, does not come to manifest —
sbh1.3:35.60
sarvadā sarvakālamāśrayasanniviṣṭaṁ cānuśayaṁ prajahāti |
and at all times he also abandons the latent tendency settled in the [karmic] basis,
sbh1.3:35.61
samudghātayati |
eradicates it [at the root].
sbh1.3:35.62
ayamviśeṣaḥ, ayamabhiprāyaḥ |
This is the distinction; this is the import.
sbh1.3:35.63
ida ma nākaraṇaṁ pratisaṁkhyānabalasaṁgṛhītasya bhāvanābalasaṁgṛhītasya cendriyasamvarasya |
This [non-arising and non-manifestation of defilement] is the [shared] negative work of both kinds of sense-restraint — that grounded in reflective discrimination and that grounded in cultivation.
sbh1.3:35.64
tatra yo a yaṁ pratisaṁkhyānabalasaṁgṛhīta indriyasamvaro a yaṁ saṁbhāramārgasaṁgṛhītaḥ |
The sense-restraint grounded in the power of reflective discrimination belongs to the path of requisites (saṁbhāra-mārga).
sbh1.3:35.65
yaḥ punarbhāvanābalasaṁgṛhīta indriyasamvaraḥ |
The sense-restraint grounded in the power of cultivation,
sbh1.3:35.66
sa vairāgyabhūmipatito veditavyaḥ |
by contrast, is to be understood as falling within the dispassion-stage proper.
sbh1.3:36.1
bhojane mātrajñatā katamā |
What is knowing the right measure in food?
sbh1.3:36.2
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
When someone here takes food with [proper] reflection —
sbh1.3:36.3
na dravārthaṁ, na maṇḍanārthaṁ, na vibhūṣaṇārthamiti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
not for frivolity, not for adornment, not for embellishment, [as elaborated in the standard formula] above.
sbh1.3:36.4
kathaṁ pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
How does he take food with [proper] reflection?
sbh1.3:36.5
pratisaṁkhyocyate yayā prajñayā kabaḍaṁkārasyāhārasyādīnavaṁ samanupaśyatyādīnavadarśanena vidūṣayitvā vidūṣyā bhyavaharati |
‘Reflection’ here refers to that wisdom by which he sees the danger in solid food, and seeing the danger, having grasped the fault — and grasping the fault — he eats.
sbh1.3:37.1
tatpunarādīnavadarśanaṁ katamat |
What, again, is that danger-seeing?
sbh1.3:37.2
yaduta yasyaiva kabaḍaṁkārasya paribhogānvayo vā, vipariṇāmānvayo vā, paryeṣaṇānvayo vā |
[It is the danger seen] in that very solid food by way of the consequence of consumption, the consequence of transformation, or the consequence of seeking.
The three-fold danger schema (consumption / transformation / seeking, pariniṣpatti / pariṇāma / paryeṣaṇa) is a canonical Yogācāra analysis of food's faults. The seeking-danger then subdivides into six sub-types (37.31–35) — gathering, guarding, loss-of-affection, insatiability, lack-of-independence, misconduct — which structurally parallel the well-known kāma-ādīnava (drawbacks of sense-pleasures) lists in the Pāli Mahādukkhakkhandha-sutta (MN 13).
sbh1.3:37.3
paribhogānvaya ādīnavaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the consumption-consequence danger [in food]?
sbh1.3:37.4
yathāpīhaikatyo yasmin samaye āhāramāharati varṇasampannamapi, gandhasampannamapi, rasasampannamapi, supraṇītamapi |
When someone here, at the time of taking food endowed with color, endowed with smell, endowed with taste, very fine —
sbh1.3:37.5
tasya kabaḍaṁkāra āhāraḥ samanantarakṣipta eva āsye yadā dantayantracūrṇitaśca, lālāvisaraviklinnaśca bhavati |
of that food just put into the mouth, ground by the apparatus of the teeth, made wet by the flowing of saliva,
sbh1.3:37.6
lālāpariveṣṭitaśca bhavati |
and wrapped in saliva —
sbh1.3:37.7
sa tasmin samaye kaṇṭhanālīpraluṭhitaśca bhavati |
at the time when it has rolled down through the throat-tube,
sbh1.3:37.8
sa yāsau pūrvikā, purāṇā manāpatā na āpatā tāṁ sarveṇa sarvaṁ vijahāti |
that former, original agreeableness, the agreeable taste — that he wholly, entirely abandons,
sbh1.3:37.9
parāṁ ca vikṛtimāpadyate |
and [the food] reaches another transformation,
sbh1.3:37.10
yasyāṁ ca vikṛtau vartamānaśchanditakopamaḥ khyāti |
and in that transformation underway, it appears like vomit.
sbh1.3:37.11
tadavasthaṁ cainaṁ sa cedayaṁ bhoktā puruṣapudgalaḥ sa cedākārato manasi kuryāt samanusmarennāsya sarveṇa sarvamanyatrāpi tāvadavipariṇate, praṇīte bhojane bhogakāmatā santiṣṭheta |
And [as for] that very state — if the consumer-individual were to attend to its appearance, to recollect it, his desire to enjoy [it] would in no way remain, [not] even with respect to [as-yet] untransformed, fine food.
sbh1.3:37.12
kaḥ punarvādastatra tadavastha iti ya ebhirākauraranekavidhairanayānupūrvyā bhojanaparibhogamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā yāsau śubhā varṇanibhā antarīyate, ādīnavaśca prādurbhavati aśū śu cisaṁgṛhītaḥ |
What more is to be said of that [later] state? By these manifold modes, by this sequence, with food-consumption as the predominant [factor], this beautiful color is altered, and danger appears, [the food] coming under [the category of] the unclean.
sbh1.3:37.13
ayamucyate paribhogānvayaḥ |
This is called the consumption-consequence
sbh1.3:37.14
ādīnavaḥ |
danger,
sbh1.3:37.15
yaduta āhāre |
namely, in food.
sbh1.3:37.16
tatra katamo vipariṇāmānvaya ādīnava āhāre |
What is the transformation-consequence danger in food?
sbh1.3:37.17
tasya tamāhāramāhṛtavatastu ṣṭava taḥ yadā vipariṇamati rātryā madhyame vā yāme, paścime vā yāme, tadā sa rudhiramānsa māṁsa snāyvasthitvagādīnyanekavidhāni bahunānāprakārāṇi |
When he has taken that food and is satisfied, [and] when it undergoes transformation — in the middle watch of the night or in the last watch — it then generates and increases blood, flesh, sinew, bone, skin, and many other [tissues],
sbh1.3:37.18
asmin kāye aśucindravyāṇi vivardhayati saṁjanayati |
[as well as] impure substances in this body.
sbh1.3:37.19
arthataśca |
And in part,
sbh1.3:37.20
atipariṇataścādhobhāgī bhavati |
having undergone further transformation, it descends to the lower [part of the body],
sbh1.3:37.21
yadasya divase śocayitavyaṁ ca bhavati |
[the elimination of] which during the day must be attended to with distaste.
sbh1.3:37.22
tena ca yaḥ spṛṣṭo bhavati |
And what is touched by him [in this elimination] —
sbh1.3:37.23
hasto tau vā pādo dau vā, anyatamā nyatamajvā maṁvā aṁgapratyaṁgaṁ, bahirdhā gupyanīyaṁ gopanīyaṁ bhavatyātmanaḥ pareṣāṁ ca |
the hand or the foot, or some other limb or sub-limb — has thereafter to be hidden from outsiders, guarded both from himself and from others.
sbh1.3:37.24
tannidānāścāsyotpadyante |
And on that account [of food] arise
sbh1.3:37.25
kāye bahavaḥ kāyikā ābādhāḥ |
in his body many bodily afflictions,
sbh1.3:37.26
tadyathā gaṇḍaḥ, piṭakaḥ, dadrū, vicarcikā, kaṇḍū ḥ , kuṣṭhaḥ, kiṭibhaḥ, kivāso kilāso , jvaraḥ kāsaḥ śothaḥ, śoṣāpasmāra śoṣo ’pasmāra , āṭakkaraṁ, pāṇḍurogaḥ, rudhiraṁ, pittabhagandara itīme cānye bhairaṁbhagaṁyā a pyevaṁbhāgīyāḥ kāye kāyikā ābādhā utpadyante |
namely: tumor, boil, eczema, itching, leprosy, skin-disease, pallor-disease, fever, cough, swelling, consumption, epilepsy, intestinal infection, jaundice, [disease of the] blood, [and] bile-fistula — these and other such bodily afflictions arise in the body.
sbh1.3:37.27
bhuktamvā a sya vipadyate |
Or what was eaten goes bad,
sbh1.3:37.28
yenāsya kāye viṣūcikā santiṣṭhate |
on account of which cholera appears in his body.
sbh1.3:37.29
ayamucyate vipariṇāmācca ya ādīnavo yaduta āhāre |
This is called the danger of transformation, namely, in food.
sbh1.3:37.30
tatra katamaḥ paryeṣaṇānna nva yaādīnava āhāre |
What, then, is the seeking-consequence danger in food?
sbh1.3:37.31
paryeṣaṇānvaya ādīnavo a nekavidha ḥ - samudānanākṛtaḥ |
The seeking-consequence danger is of many kinds: caused by gathering,
sbh1.3:37.32
ārakṣākṛtaḥ |
caused by guarding,
sbh1.3:37.33
snehaparibhraṁśakṛtaḥ |
caused by loss of affection,
sbh1.3:37.34
atṛptikṛtaḥ |
caused by insatiability,
sbh1.3:37.35
asvātantryakṛtaḥ duścaritakṛtaśca |
caused by lack of autonomy, and caused by misconduct.
sbh1.3:37.36
tatra katama ādīnava āhāre |
What is the danger in food
sbh1.3:37.37
samudānanā kṛtaḥ |
caused by gathering?
sbh1.3:37.38
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ āhārahetorāhāranidānaṁ śīte śītena hanyamānaḥ, uṣṇe uṣṇena hanyamānaḥ, utsahate, ghaṭate, vyāyacchate |
When someone here, for the sake of food, on account of food, struck by cold in the cold, struck by heat in the heat, makes effort, strives, exerts himself —
sbh1.3:37.39
kṛṣiṇā vā, gorakṣyeṇa vā, vāṇijyena vā, lipigaṇanāvyasanasaṁkhyāmudrayā a nekavidhena śilpasthānakarmasthānenāpratilabdhasya vāhārasya pratilambhāya, upacayāya vā |
by farming, by cattle-tending, by trade, or by writing, calculating, measuring, accounting, [the use of] seals [mudrā], [and] by manifold arts and crafts, for the attainment or increase of food not yet attained.
sbh1.3:37.40
yathā āhārasye syai vamāhāranidānasya |
As with food, so with food’s cause.
sbh1.3:37.41
tasyaivamutsahato, ghaṭata to , vyāyacchata ḥ , sa cette karmāntā vipadyante |
For him, exerting and striving thus, that work-undertaking fails;
sbh1.3:37.42
sa taṁ nidānaṁ tannidānaṁ śocati klāmyati, paridevate |
he on that account grieves, [is] exhausted, laments,
sbh1.3:37.43
urastāḍayati |
beats his chest,
sbh1.3:37.44
krandati- saṁgo vā mā māpadyate |
wails: ‘Misfortune has come upon me;
sbh1.3:37.45
moho bata me svāyāso a tiphala iti |
alas, my exertion is fruitless.’
sbh1.3:37.46
ayamu yaṁ samu dānanā sahagataḥ ādīnavo yaduta āhāre |
This is the danger arising from gathering — namely, in food.
sbh1.3:37.47
sa cetsaṁpadyate sa tasyārakṣādhikaraṇahetostīvramautsukyamāpadyayate |
If he succeeds, then by reason of guarding [his gains] he becomes intensely anxious:
sbh1.3:37.48
kaccinme bhogā rājñā vā apahriyeraṁścaurairvā, agninā vā dahyerannudakena vā uhyeyuḥ |
‘May my enjoyments not be taken by kings or by thieves; may they not be burned by fire; may they not be washed away by water;
sbh1.3:37.49
kuniha hi tā vā nidhayaḥ praṇaśyeyuḥ kuprayuktā vā karmāntāḥ pralujyeran, apriyo yā vā dāyādā adhigaccheyuḥ |
may my buried treasures not be lost; may my poorly applied [investments] not collapse; may unloved heirs not obtain them;
sbh1.3:37.50
kulevā kulāṁgāra utpadyeta |
may household discord not arise;
sbh1.3:37.51
yastānbhogānanayena vyasanamāpādaye da yamārakṣāsahagata ādīnavo yaduta āhāre |
[may there be no one] who would bring those enjoyments to ruin’ — this is the danger arising from guarding, namely, in food.
sbh1.3:37.52
katama ādīnava ḥ snehaparibhraṁśakṛtaḥ |
What is the danger caused by loss of affection?
sbh1.3:37.53
yathāpi tadāhāranidānamāhārādhikaraṇahetormātā putrasyāvarṇambhāṣate |
When, by reason of food, on account of food, the mother speaks ill of the son,
sbh1.3:37.54
putro mātyaḥ mātuḥ , pitā putrasya, putro traḥ pituḥ, bhrātā bhaginyā, bhaginī bhrātuḥ |
the son of the mother, the father of the son, the son of the father, brother of sister, sister of brother,
sbh1.3:37.55
sahāyakaḥ |
companion
sbh1.3:37.56
sahāyakasya |
of companion —
sbh1.3:37.57
prāgeva jano janasya |
much more one person against another person.
sbh1.3:37.58
te |
They
sbh1.3:37.59
cānyonyaṁ vigṛhītā bhavanti, vivādamāpannāstathāudārā brāhmaṇakṣatriyagṛhapatimahāsālā āhārādhikaraṇahetorevaṁ vigṛhītāvivādamāpannāḥ anyonyaṁ pāṇinā praharanti |
are mutually opposed, fallen into dispute; even great brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas, [and] householder-mahāsālas, on account of food — thus opposed and fallen into dispute — strike each other with the hand,
sbh1.3:37.60
loṣṭhenāpi, daṇḍenāpi, śastreṇāpi praharantyayamucyate snehaparibhaṁśakṛta ādīnavaḥ |
[strike each other] with clod, with stick, with weapon. This is called the danger caused by loss of affection.
sbh1.3:37.61
tatra katamaḥ |
What is
sbh1.3:37.62
atṛptikṛta ādīnavaḥ |
the danger caused by insatiability?
sbh1.3:37.63
yathāpi tadrājānaḥ kṛtiyā kṣatriyā mūrddhābhiṣiktāḥ, sveṣu grāmanigamarāṣṭrarājadhānīṣu asaṁtuṣṭā viharanta ubhayato a bhyūhakāni saṁgrāmānīkāni pratisaranti |
When consecrated kṣatriya kings, discontent in their own villages, market-towns, kingdoms, [and] capitals, deploy on both sides in fortified battle-arrays —
sbh1.3:37.64
śaṁkhai ḥ kampa mpya mānaiḥ, paṭāhairvādyamānaiḥ, iṣubhiḥ kṣipyamāṇairvividhaiste tatra bhrāntenāśvena sārdhaṁ samāgacchanti |
with conches sounded, drums beaten, arrows shot, manifold [weapons drawn] — there they meet together with frenzied horses,
sbh1.3:38.1
bhrāntena hastinā, sthena, pattinā sārdhaṁ samāgacchanti |
with frenzied elephants, with chariots, [and] with foot-soldiers they clash.
sbh1.3:38.2
iṣubhiḥ śaktibhirvā apakṛttagātrā maraṇamvā ni vi gacchanti |
By arrows or by spears their limbs are cut off, [and] they go to their death;
sbh1.3:38.3
maraṇamātraṁ vā duḥkhamayamucyate |
or they suffer a pain nearly equal to death. This is called
sbh1.3:38.4
atṛptikṛta ādīnava iti yo vā punarapyevaṁbhāgīyaḥ |
the danger caused by insatiability — or whatever else is of such kind.
sbh1.3:39.1
tatra katamaḥ |
What is
sbh1.3:39.2
asvātantryakṛta ādīnavaḥ |
the danger caused by lack of autonomy [asvātantrya]?
The local Sanskrit is asvātantrya ('lack of autonomy / non-independence'), not pāratantrya. The English keeps the practical sense but the bracket now names the local term.
sbh1.3:39.3
yathāpi tadrājñaḥ pauruṣeyā āvarodhikāni nagarāṇyanupraskandataḥ nte |
When the king’s men storm fortified cities,
sbh1.3:39.4
taptenāpi ti tai lenāvasicyante |
they are doused with boiling oil,
sbh1.3:39.5
taptayā vasayā, taptayā gomayalo lau hikayā, taptena tāmreṇa, taptenāyasā, iṣubhiḥ santibhi śaktibhi ścāpakṛttagātrā maraṇaṁ vā ni vi gacchanti |
with boiling fat, with boiling cow-dung ash, with molten copper, with molten iron — [or] by arrows and spears their limbs are cut off, [and] they go to their death;
sbh1.3:39.6
maraṇamātrakaṁ vā duḥkhaṁ |
or they suffer a pain nearly equal to death.
sbh1.3:39.7
ayamucyate asvātantrayakṛta ādīnava iti yo vā punarapyevaṁbhāgīyaḥ |
This is called the danger caused by lack of autonomy — or whatever else is of such kind.
sbh1.3:39.8
tatra katamo duścaritakṛta ādīnavaḥ |
What is the danger caused by misconduct?
sbh1.3:39.9
yathāpi tadekatyenāhāranidānaṁ prabhūtaṁ kāyena duścaritaṁ kṛtaṁ bhavatyupacitaṁ, yathā kāyenaivamvācā, manasā |
When by someone, on account of food, much bodily misconduct has been done [and] accumulated — and likewise verbal and mental [misconduct] —
sbh1.3:39.10
sa ca ya thā kā ya ābādhiko bhavati, duḥkhito, bāḍhaglānaḥ, tasya tatpūrvakaṁ kāyaduścaritaṁ vāṅmanoduścarita taṁ , parvatānāṁ vā parvatakūṭānāmvā, sāyāhne yā cchayā cchāyā avalambate |
and when he becomes bodily afflicted, suffering, gravely ill, his prior bodily, verbal, and mental misconduct, like the shadow of mountains or mountain peaks in the evening, hangs over him,
sbh1.3:39.11
avyā dhya valambate |
bears down on him,
sbh1.3:39.12
abhipralambate |
looms over him.
sbh1.3:39.13
tasyai vaṁ bhavati |
It occurs to him:
sbh1.3:39.14
kṛtaṁ bata me pāpaṁ, na kṛtaṁ bata me puṇyaṁ, kāyena vācā, manasā, so a haṁ yā gati ḥ kṛtapāpānāṁ tāṁ gatiṁ pretya gamiṣyāmīti |
‘Evil has been done by me; merit has not been done by me, [whether] by body, speech, [or] mind. The destiny of those who have done evil — to that destiny shall I, after death, go.’
sbh1.3:39.15
vipratisārī kālaṁ karoti |
Remorseful, he dies,
sbh1.3:39.16
akālañca kṛtvāpāyeṣūpapadyate |
and dying in such an [evil] manner, he is reborn in the lower realms —
sbh1.3:39.17
yaduta narakeṣu, tiryakpreteṣu |
namely in the hells, the animal-realm, [or] the ghost-realm.
sbh1.3:39.18
ayamucyate duścarita kṛta ādīnavaḥ |
This is called the danger caused by misconduct.
sbh1.3:39.19
tasyaivambhavati |
Thus it occurs to him:
sbh1.3:39.20
ityayamāhāra ḥ paryeṣa ṣya māṇo a pi sādīnavaḥ |
‘This food, in its seeking, is dangerous;
sbh1.3:39.21
paribhujyamāno a pi sādīnavaḥ |
in its consumption too, [is] dangerous;
sbh1.3:39.22
paribhukto a pi pariṇāma ādīnavaḥ |
and once consumed, [its] transformation is dangerous.’
sbh1.3:39.23
evamasti punarasyāhārasya kācidanuśansa śaṁsa mātrā sā punaḥ katamā |
Yet there is for this food some slight benefit. What, then, is that?
sbh1.3:39.24
āhārasthitiko a ya yaṁ kāya āhāraṁ niśritya tiṣṭhati |
‘This body sustained by food stands depending on food,
sbh1.3:39.25
nānāhāra iyamasyānuśansa śaṁsa mātrā |
not without food’ — this is the benefit.
sbh1.3:39.26
evamāhārasthitiko a yaṁ kāyaṁ ḥ suciramapi tiṣṭhan varṣaśatamvā tiṣṭhati |
‘Thus this food-sustained body, standing for a long time, stands [even] up to a hundred years,
sbh1.3:39.27
kiṁcidvā punarbhūyaḥ samyak parihriyamāṇaḥ |
or somewhat longer when well looked after;
sbh1.3:39.28
asti cāsyārvāguparatiḥ |
and there is also its [possible] cessation in the meanwhile.’
sbh1.3:39.29
tatra yaḥ ye kāyasthitimātre pratipannāḥ na te supratipannā ḥ kāye kāyasthitimātrakena ṇa saṁtuṣṭā na na te asaṁtuṣṭā, na ca punaste āhārakṛtaṁ paripūrṇamanavadyamanuśansaṁ śaṁsaṁ pratyanubhavanti |
Those who undertake practice merely for body-maintenance are not well-practiced; those who are content with mere body-maintenance are not truly content, and they do not experience the full, blameless benefit of food.
sbh1.3:39.30
ye punarnakāyasthitimātrakeṇa na saṁtuṣṭā ḥ kāyasthitimātrake pratipannā, na te supratipannā, api tu tāmeva kāyasthitiṁ niściṁ tya niśritya brahmacaryaṁ rya samudāgamāya pratipannāḥ, supratipannāḥ, ta eva ca punaḥ paripūrṇamanavadyamanuśansaṁ śaṁsaṁ pratyanubhavanti |
But those who are not content with mere body-maintenance, and who, relying on that very body-maintenance, undertake practice for the fulfillment of the holy life — they are well-practiced, and they experience the full, blameless benefit.
sbh1.3:39.31
tanme pratimaṁ syād yada d vā pratyavareṇa āhārānuśansa śaṁsa mātrakeṇa saṁtuṣṭo vihareyaṁ |
‘It would not be fitting for me to dwell content with only this slight, inferior share of food’s benefit.
sbh1.3:39.32
na me pratirūpaṁ syādyadahaṁ bālasabhāgatāṁ bālasahadhārmikatāmadhyāpadyeyamevamāhāre sarvākāraṁ paripūrṇamādīnavaṁ jñātvā sa itaḥ pratisaṁkhyāyādīnava darśī, niḥsaraṇānveṣī cāhāraniḥsaraṇārthameva putramāṁ mān tasyaivaṁ bhavati |
It would not be fitting for me to fall into the class of fools, into Dharma-commonality with fools.’ Having thus known the wholly complete danger in food, with [proper] reflection he becomes a seer of danger and seeker of escape — for the sake of escape from food itself. It occurs to him:
sbh1.3:39.33
evamete dāyakadānapatayaḥ kṛcchreṇa bhogān samudānīya, mahāntaṁ paryeṣaṇākṛtamādīnavaṁ pratyanubhavantaḥ, prapīḍya, prapīḍya, tvaṅmānsa māṁsa śoṇitamasmākamanuprayacchanti |
‘These donor-patrons, having gathered their enjoyments with difficulty, undergoing great seeking-caused danger, as though pressing out their own skin, flesh, and blood, give [food] to us.
sbh1.3:39.34
ya ete a nukampāmupādāya viśeṣaphalārthinaḥ tasyāsmākaṁ tathā pratilabdhasya piṇḍapātasyāyamevaṁ rūpo a nurūpaḥ paribhogaḥ syādyadahaṁ tathā paribhūtamātmānaṁ |
These [patrons] give out of compassion, seeking a distinguished fruit. For this alms-food thus received by us, the fitting mode of use would be this:
sbh1.3:39.35
sthāpayitvā paribhuṁjīya, yathā teṣāṁ kārāḥ kṛtā atyarthaṁ mahāphalā ḥ syurmahānuśaṁsā, mahādyubha ta yo, mahāvistārāḥ, candropamaśca kulānyupasaṁkrameyaṁ vyavakṛṣya kāyaṁ, vyavakṛṣya cittaṁ, hrīmānapragalbhaḥ, anātmotkarṣī aparapansī, yathāsvena lābhena citta sucittaḥ syāṁ, sumanāḥ, evaṁ parasyāpi lābhena citta sucittaḥ syāṁ sumanā, evaṁ cittaśca punaḥ kulānyusaṁkrameyaṁ |
that, having established myself in such self-restraint, I should consume it so that their acts of service become very fruitful, of great benefit, great glory, [and] great extension; and that, like the moon, I should approach their families — withdrawn in body, withdrawn in mind, possessed of moral shame, non-brazen, non-self-aggrandizing, non-deprecating-of-others, with mind well-clear and glad at my own gain, and equally with clear and glad mind at another’s gain — and with such a mind I would again approach their families.
sbh1.3:39.36
tatkuta etallabhyaṁ pravrajitena parakuleṣu yadvadatra pare me māna naṁ dadatu |
How could this be proper for one gone forth [into the homeless life], [going] among others’ families: ‘let others give me food’ —
sbh1.3:39.37
satkṛtya, mā asatkṛtya, prabhūtaṁ mā stokaṁ, praṇītaṁ mā lūhaṁ, tvarita ṁ mā gatvaṁ baddham |
honoringly, not without honor; plentifully, not scantily; finely, not crudely; swiftly, not slowly; properly.
sbh1.3:39.38
evaṁ caritasya me kulānyusaṁkramataḥ sa cet pare na dadyustenāhaṁ na teṣāmantike āghātacittatayā pratighacittatayā vyavadīyeyaṁ |
[I should resolve:] if, while I am walking thus and approaching families, others do not give, on their account I should not sully myself with a mind of resentment or a mind of hostility,
sbh1.3:39.39
na ca punastannidānaṁ kāyasya bhedādapāyopapattyā vighātamāpadyeya yaṁ |
and further, on that account, with the breaking up of the body, I should not encounter the dejection of rebirth in the lower realms —
sbh1.3:39.40
yaduta tāmevāghātacittatā tāṁ pratighacittā madhipatiṁ kṛtvā sa cedasatkṛtya na satkṛtya, sa cetstokaṁ na prabhūtaṁ |
namely, by taking that very resentful and hostile mind as predominant. [And if] they give without honor rather than with honor, scantily rather than plentifully,
sbh1.3:39.41
sa cellūhaṁ na praṇītaṁ, sa cedvaddhaṁ na tvaritaṁ dadyuḥ |
crudely, not finely; slowly, not swiftly —
sbh1.3:39.42
da ta yāhamāghātacittatayā, pratighacittatayā ca vyavadīyeyamiti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
[I should not] on that account sully myself with a mind of resentment and hostility,’ [the rest] as elaborated above.
sbh1.3:39.43
imaṁ cāhaṁ kabaḍīkāramāhāraṁ niśritya tathā tathā pratipadyeya yaṁ , tāñca mātrāṁ prativedhyeyaṁ |
‘And relying on this morsel-made food, I should practice in such a way, and should know that very measure
sbh1.3:39.44
yena me jīvitendriyanirodhaśca na syānnaca piṇḍakena klāmyeyaṁ |
by which there would neither be cessation of the life-faculty, nor would I be worn out by alms-food,
sbh1.3:39.45
brahmacaryānugrahaśca me syādevaṁ ca me śrava ma ṇabhāve, pravrajitabhāve sthitasyāyaṁ piṇḍapātaparibhogarūpaśca |
and there would be support for the holy life; and likewise, while I stand in the state of a recluse, the state of one gone forth, this mode of using alms-food too
sbh1.3:39.46
pariśuddhaścānavadyaśca syādebhi rākā raiḥ sa pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
would be pure and blameless.’ By these modes he, with [proper] reflection, takes food.
sbh1.3:39.47
āhāraḥ punaḥ katamaḥ |
What, again, is food?
sbh1.3:39.48
catvāra āhārāḥ |
[There are] four [kinds of] food:
The four kinds of food (catur āhāra) — morsel-made (kavaḍīkāra-āhāra), contact (sparśa), mental volition (manaḥsaṁcetanā), and consciousness (vijñāna) — is canonical abhidharma. The text narrows the bhojane mātrajñatā discussion to the first only, kavaḍīkāra-āhāra — actual physical food taken by the body. The other three are subtle nutriments that sustain saṁsāric continuity at deeper levels.
sbh1.3:39.49
kabaḍaṁkāraḥ, sparśo, manaḥsaṁcetanā, vijñānaṁ cāsmiṁstvarthe kabaḍaṁkāra āhāro ’bhipretaḥ |
morsel-made [food], contact, mental volition, [and] consciousness. In this context, morsel-made food is intended.
sbh1.3:39.50
sa punaḥ katamastadyathā manthā vāpūpā vā odanakulmāṣamvā, sarpistailaṁ, phaṇitaṁ, māṁsaṁ, matsyā, vallūrā, lavaṇaṁ, kṣīraṁ, dadhi, navanītamitīmāni cānyāni caivaṁ rūpāṇyupakaraṇāni yāni kabaḍāni kṛtvā a bhyavahriyante |
And what is that? Namely: barley-meal or cakes or rice or porridge, ghee, oil, syrup, meat, fish, dried meat, salt, milk, curd, butter — these and other such kinds of substances which, made into morsels, are eaten.
sbh1.3:39.51
tasmāt kabaḍaṁkāra ityucyate |
Therefore it is called morsel-made.
sbh1.3:39.52
āharatīti bhuṁkte |
‘He takes [it]’ [means] he eats;
sbh1.3:39.53
pratiniṣevatyabhyavaharati, khādati, bhakṣayati |
partakes of, takes, chews, devours,
sbh1.3:39.54
svādayati, pibati, cūṣatīti paryāyāḥ |
tastes, drinks, sucks — these are synonyms.
sbh1.3:39.55
na dravārthamiti |
‘Not for frivolity’ — that is,
The formula here reads na dravārtham, not the more familiar na darpārtham. The local explanation at 39.57-58 explicitly glosses drava as sensual play/frivolity connected with lust and intercourse; English therefore uses 'frivolity' rather than 'arrogance' for this member of the food-reflection formula. Mada remains 'intoxication' in the next member.
sbh1.3:39.56
yaścaite ye caite kāmopabhogina ityarthaḥ |
[it is] those who enjoy sense-pleasures [who do otherwise], is the meaning.
sbh1.3:39.57
yāharanti ya āharanti yadvayamāhāreṇa prīṇitagātrāḥ saṁtarpitagātrāḥ pratyupasthite sāyāhnakāle samaye, atikrāntāyāṁ rajanyāṁ, maulībaddhikābhiḥ sārddhaṁmalābu-romaśabāhubhiḥ kandukastanibhirnārībhiḥ kandukastanībhirnārībhiḥ krīḍato nto , ramamāṇāḥ, paricārayan nta , auddhatyaṁ dravaṁ prāviṣkariṣyāma iti |
They take food in such a way [thinking]: ‘by [this] food, we — of pleased body, satisfied body, with evening time at hand or when night has passed — with crowned-haired, ribbon-armed, girdled, breast-cup-breasted women, sporting [and] taking pleasure, will display elation and play.’
sbh1.3:39.58
drava eṣa ārye dharmavinaye yadutakāmarāgopasaṁhitā, maithunopasaṁhitā ḥ pāpakā akuśalā dharmā, vitarkā, yairayaṁ khādyamāno, bādhyamāna, uddhatendriyo bhavatyanuddhatendriyaśca, drutamānasaḥ, plutamānasaḥ, asthitamānaso a vyupaśāntamānasaḥ, te punaratyantamāhāramāharanto dravārthamāharantītyucyate |
‘Frivolity,’ in the noble Dharma-Vinaya, is what consists of evil, unwholesome dharmas connected with sensual lust, connected with intercourse — the applied thoughts by which, being enjoyed and gnawed at [in the mind], one becomes of unrestrained senses, of unrestrained mind, of swift mind, of leaping mind, of unsettled mind, of un-calmed mind. They, taking food in this excessive [way], are said to ‘take food for frivolity.’
sbh1.3:39.59
śrutavāṁstvāryaśrāvaka ḥ pratisaṁkhyānabalika ādīnavadarśī niḥsaraṇaṁ prajānaṁ nan paribhuṁkte |
The learned noble disciple, however, possessing the power of reflection, seeing the danger [and] knowing the escape, enjoys [food] —
sbh1.3:39.60
na tathā yathā te kāmopabhogino bhuṁjante |
not in the way those enjoyers of sense-pleasures enjoy [it].
sbh1.3:39.61
tenāha- na dravārthaṁ na madārthaṁ, na maṇḍanārthaṁ na vibhūṣaṇārthamiti |
Therefore he says: ‘not for frivolity, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for embellishment.’
sbh1.3:39.62
yathāpi ta eva kāmopabhogina ityartha māhāramāharanti |
Now, [as for] how those enjoyers of sense-pleasures take food:
sbh1.3:39.63
adya vayamāhāramāhṛtavanto yaduta prabhūtañca tṛptito yathāśaktyābalaṁ |
‘Today we have taken food — namely, plentifully and to satisfaction in accord with [our] strength and capacity:
sbh1.3:39.64
snigdhaṁ ca, vṛṣyañca, bṛṁhaṇīyañca, varṇasaṁpannaṁ, gandhasampannaṁ, rasasampannaṁ |
smooth, strengthening, fattening, complexion-enhancing, smell-endowed, taste-endowed [food].
sbh1.3:39.65
endhābhūte aindhībhūte , nirgatāyāṁ rajanyāṁ śaktā bhaviṣyāmaḥ |
When the kindled-state [of digestion] arises [and] when night has departed, we shall be able,
sbh1.3:39.66
pratibalā, vyāyāmakaraṇo ṇā , yaduta atartkāyā ātatīkriyayā vā, nirghātena, vyāyāmaśilayā vā, ulloṭhanene na vā, pṛthivīkhātena vā, bāhuvyāyāmena vā, pādāvaṣṭambhanena vā, plavanena vā, a laṁghanena vā |
[being] fit, to do exercise — namely by tarka or atatī, by jolting, by yāmāyāma, by ulloṭhana, by digging the earth, by arm-exercise, by foot-pressing, by leaping, by jumping.
sbh1.3:39.67
tatra vyāyāmena bāha hu ñca punarvyāyāmaṁ niśritya balavantobhaviṣyāmaḥ |
[Doing] external exercise [and] much [other] exercise, depending on it we shall become strong,
sbh1.3:39.68
a vyāyatagātrā, dīrghaṁ cārogāḥ, cirakālaṁ cāsmākaṁ yauvanamanuvartakaṁ bhaviṣyati, no tu tvaritaṁ |
with well-exercised limbs, long-time healthy, and for a long time our youth will continue, [and] the disfiguring [process of] aging will not swiftly
sbh1.3:39.69
virūpakaraṇī jarā dehamabhibhaviṣyantī tī ti |
overcome the body.
sbh1.3:39.70
cirataraṁ ca jīviṣyāma iti |
And we shall live longer,
sbh1.3:39.71
prabhūtabhakṣaṇe ca pratibalā bhaviṣyāmaḥ |
and in plentiful eating we shall be fit,
sbh1.3:39.72
bhuktaṁ ca bhuktaṁ samyakpariṇamiṣyati |
and what we eat will be well digested,
sbh1.3:39.73
doṣāṇāṁ cāpaca kṣa yaḥ kṛto bhaviṣyati |
and the diminution of the doṣas [bodily humors] will be effected.’
sbh1.3:39.74
ityārogyamadārthaṁ, yauvana madārthaṁ, jīvitamadārthaṁ paribhuṁjate |
Thus they enjoy [food] for intoxication with health, intoxication with youth, intoxication with life.
sbh1.3:39.75
teṣāṁ punarevaṁ bhavati |
It further occurs to them:
sbh1.3:39.76
kṛtavyāyāmā vayaṁ snātrasaṁvidhānaṁ kariṣyāmo, yaduta-śucinā toyena gātrāṇi prakṣāliṣyāmaḥ |
‘Having exercised, we shall make bathing-arrangements — namely, with clean water we shall wash our limbs,
sbh1.3:39.77
prakṣālitagātrāśca keśāni ca keśāṁśca prasādhayiṣyāmaḥ |
and with washed limbs we shall dress our hair,
sbh1.3:39.78
vividhena cānulepanena kāyamanupalipya -manulipya vividhairvastrairvividhairmālyairvividhairalaṁ kāraiḥ kāyaṁ bhūṣayiṣyāmaḥ |
and, having anointed the body with various ointments, [we shall] with various cloths, with various garlands, with various ornaments adorn the body.’
sbh1.3:39.79
tatra yat snānaprasādhanānulepanamidamucyate |
That bathing, dressing, [and] anointing — this is called
sbh1.3:39.80
teṣāṁ maṇḍanaṁ |
their adornment.
sbh1.3:39.81
tathā maṇḍanajātānāṁ yadvastramālyābharaṇadhāraṇamidamucyate |
And of those so adorned, the wearing of cloths, garlands, [and] ornaments — this is called
sbh1.3:39.82
vibhūṣaṇamiti |
embellishment.
sbh1.3:39.83
maṇḍanārthaṁ vibhūṣaṇārthaṁ paribhuṁja to ’ta evaṁ paribhuṁjanto ’ta evaṁ madamattā maṇḍanajātivibhūṣitagātrāḥ |
Enjoying [food] for adornment, for embellishment — thus enjoying, thus intoxicated by pride, adorned, with embellished limbs,
sbh1.3:39.84
madhyāhnasamaye, sāyāhna samaye vā, bhaktasamaye tṛṣitā bubhukṣitāśca, pareṇa harṣeṇa, parayā nandyā, pareṇāmodena |
at midday time, at evening time, at meal time, thirsty and hungry, with great delight, great joy, great pleasure,
sbh1.3:39.85
ādīnavadarśino niḥsaraṇamaprajānanta nto yathopapannamāhāramāharanti |
not seeing the danger, not knowing the escape, they take food as obtained,
sbh1.3:39.86
yāvadeva punaḥ punardravārthaṁ, maṇḍanārthaṁ, vibhūṣaṇārtha ca |
up to: again and again for frivolity, for adornment, for embellishment.
sbh1.3:39.87
śrutavāṁstvāryaśrāvakaḥ |
The learned noble disciple, however,
sbh1.3:39.88
pratisaṁkhyānabalika ādīnavadarśī niḥsaraṇaṁprajānan paribhuṁkte |
possessing the power of reflection, seeing the danger [and] knowing the escape, enjoys [food] —
sbh1.3:39.89
na tu tathā yathā te kāmopabhoginaḥ paribhuṁjate |
but not in the way those enjoyers of sense-pleasures enjoy [it].
sbh1.3:39.90
nānyatremamasaṁniveṣaṇāprahātavyamāhāraṁ pratiniṣevamāṇa eva prahāsyāmīti |
‘[I shall not abandon this food] otherwise than by abandonment-through-non-establishment [aniṣṭha-prahāṇa] — [that is,] I shall continue to use it [while not establishing-attachment to it].’
aniṣṭha-prahāṇa — literally 'abandonment by non-establishment.' The point is that for food, the practitioner does not abandon by ceasing-to-use (impossible while embodied); rather, he abandons attachment to it while continuing the physical act of eating. The phrase is technical: this is the same abandonment-without-cessation that recurs for the requisite-items (pariṣkāra) generally, where the bodily activity must continue but the affective grip on it must be released.
sbh1.3:39.91
yāvadevāsya kāyasya sthitaye iti bhuktvā nābhuktvā yaśca jīvitasya kāyasthitirityucyate |
‘Only for the sustaining of this body’ — that, depending on the eating or non-eating [of which], the maintenance of life and body-sustenance [proceed] — is what is so called.
sbh1.3:39.92
so a hamimamāhāramāhṛtya jīviṣyāmi, na mariṣyāmīti āhārati |
‘I, having taken this food, shall live, shall not die,’ thus [thinking] he eats.
sbh1.3:39.93
tenāhaṁ ha yāvadevāsya kāyasya sthitaye |
Therefore he says: ‘only for the sustaining of this body.’
sbh1.3:39.94
kathaṁ yāpanāyai āharati |
How does he take food ‘for its support’?
sbh1.3:39.95
dvividhā yātrā-asti kṛcchreṇa astyakṛcchreṇa |
Continuation [yāpana] is twofold: there is the difficult and there is the not-difficult.
sbh1.3:39.96
kṛcchreṇa yātrā katamā |
What is difficult continuation?
sbh1.3:39.97
yadrūpamāhārato jighatsā daurbalyaṁ vā bhavati |
When, from [improper] food, hunger or weakness arises,
sbh1.3:39.98
duḥkhito vā bāḍhaglānaḥ |
or suffering, severe illness;
sbh1.3:39.99
adharmeṇa vā piṇḍapātaṁ paryeṣate, na dharmeṇa |
or [when] he seeks alms-food by non-Dharma, not by Dharma;
sbh1.3:39.100
raktaḥ paribhuṁkte, saktaḥ, gṛddho, grathito, mūrchito ’dhyavasito a dhyavasāyamāpannaḥ |
[when he is] enjoying with attachment, with clinging, with greed, with binding, with infatuation, with fixation, with fixation-state;
sbh1.3:39.101
guruko vāsya kāyo bhavatyakarmaṇyaḥ, aprahāṇakṣamaḥ, yenāsya dhandhaṁ cittaṁ samādhiyate dhīyeta |
or [when] his body becomes heavy, unworkable, unfit for [pursuit of] abandonment, by which his mind is [only] slowly concentrated;
sbh1.3:39.102
kṛcchreṇa vā āśvāsa-praśvāsāḥ pravartante |
or [when] in-and-out breaths proceed with difficulty;
sbh1.3:39.103
styānamiddhaṁ vā cittaṁ paryavahīya paryavanahyatīya mucyate kṛcchreṇa yātrā |
or [when] sloth-and-torpor envelops the mind — this is called difficult continuation.
sbh1.3:39.104
akṛcchreṇa yātrā katamā |
What is non-difficult continuation?
sbh1.3:39.105
yathāpi tadrūpamāhāramāharato yathā jighatsā daurbbalyaṁ vā na bhavati |
When, [from] taking such food, no hunger or weakness arises,
sbh1.3:39.106
nābhyadhiko bhavati |
[and these] do not become abundant —
sbh1.3:39.107
duḥkhito vā bāḍhaglānaḥ |
[nor] suffering, [nor] severe illness;
sbh1.3:39.108
dharmeṇa vā piṇḍapātaṁ paryeṣate, na vā a dharmeṇa |
or [when] he seeks alms-food by Dharma, not by non-Dharma;
sbh1.3:39.109
surakto vā paribhuṁktesaktaḥ paribhuṅkate ’saktaḥ , agṛdhraḥ, agrathitaḥ, anadhyavasito a nadhyavasāyamāpannaḥ, na cāsya kāyo guruko bhavati |
or [when he] enjoys without attachment, without clinging, without greed, without binding, without fixation, without fixation-state; and his body does not become heavy,
sbh1.3:39.110
karmaṇyo bhavati |
becomes workable,
sbh1.3:39.111
prahāṇakṣamaḥ |
fit for [pursuit of] abandonment,
sbh1.3:39.112
yenāsya tvaritaṁ cittaṁ samādhīyate |
by which his mind is swiftly concentrated;
sbh1.3:39.113
alpakṛcchreṇāśvāsapraśvāsāḥ pravarttante |
[when] in-and-out breaths proceed with little difficulty;
sbh1.3:39.114
styānamiddhaṁ cittaṁ na paryavana hya tīyamucyate alpakṛcchreṇa yātrā |
[when] sloth-and-torpor does not envelop the mind — this is called non-difficult continuation.
sbh1.3:39.115
tatra yā kṛcchreṇa yātrā tayā jīvitasthitirbhavati |
There, by the difficult continuation, life-maintenance occurs,
sbh1.3:39.116
kāyasya sāvadyā sasaṁkliṣṭā |
but it is a faulty and defiled maintenance of the body.
sbh1.3:39.117
tatra yeyamalpakṛcchreṇa yātrā tayā jīvitasthitirbhavati |
By the less-difficult continuation, life-maintenance occurs
sbh1.3:39.118
kāyasya |
of the body
sbh1.3:39.119
sā ca punaranavadyā |
and it is blameless,
sbh1.3:39.120
asaṁkliṣṭā |
undefiled.
sbh1.3:39.121
tatra śrutavānāryaśrāvakaḥ |
There, the learned noble disciple
sbh1.3:39.122
sāvadyāṁ saṁkliṣṭāṁ yātrāmparivarjayati |
avoids the faulty, defiled continuation, [and]
sbh1.3:39.123
anavadyāmasaṁkliṣṭāṁ yātrāṁ gacchati |
turns toward
sbh1.3:39.124
pratiṣevate |
[and] resorts to the blameless, undefiled continuation.
sbh1.3:39.125
tenāha yāpanāyai |
Therefore he says: ‘for support.’
sbh1.3:39.126
sā punaranavadyā asaṁkliṣṭā yātrā yā pūrvamuktā |
That blameless, undefiled continuation which was earlier described —
sbh1.3:39.127
tāṁ kathaṁ yāpayati |
how does he maintain it?
sbh1.3:39.128
āha |
Answer:
sbh1.3:39.129
yadyayaṁ jighatsoparataye, brahmacaryānugrahāya iti, paurāṇāṁ ca vedanāṁ prahāsyāmi navāñca notpādayiṣyāmi |
‘[This eating] is for the stilling of hunger, for the supporting of the holy life; old feelings I shall abandon and new ones I shall not produce.
sbh1.3:39.130
yātrā ca me bhaviṣyati |
I shall have continuation,
sbh1.3:39.131
balaṁ ca, sukhaṁ cānavadyatā ca, sparśavi hāra tā ceti |
strength, comfort, blamelessness, [and] ease of contact.’
sbh1.3:39.132
evaṁ pratiṣevamāṇaḥ anavadyāmasaṁkliṣṭāṁ yātrāṁ kalpayati |
Thus resorting [to this reflection], he arranges a blameless, undefiled continuation.
sbh1.3:39.133
kathaṁ ca punarjighatsoparataye āharati |
And how, again, does he take food ‘for the stilling of hunger’?
sbh1.3:39.134
pratyupasthite bhaktasamaye, utpannāyāṁ kṣudhāyāṁ, yadā paribhuṁkte tasyaiva kṣutparyavasthānasya jighatsādaurbalyasya ca prativigamāya tāñca mātrāṁ paribhuṁkte |
When the meal-time has come and hunger has arisen, in enjoying [food] he enjoys just that measure [needed] for the removal of that very hunger-obsession and hunger-weakness,
sbh1.3:39.135
yathāsya bhuktavataḥ akāle punarjighatsādaurbalyanna bādhate |
such that for him, having eaten, hunger-weakness does not afflict [him] at the untimely time —
sbh1.3:39.136
sāyāhnasamaye vā, abhi ti krāntāyāmvā rajanyāṁ, śvobhūte, pratyupasthite bhaktasamaye |
at evening time, or when night has passed, on the next day, [until] the meal-time has come.
sbh1.3:39.137
evaṁ jighatsoparataye āharati |
Thus he takes food for the stilling of hunger.
sbh1.3:39.138
kathaṁ brahmacaryānugrahāyārati |
How does he take food ‘for the supporting of the holy life’?
sbh1.3:39.139
tāṁ mātrāṁ paribhuṁkte tadrūpamāhāramāharati |
He enjoys such a measure, takes such food,
sbh1.3:39.140
yenāsya kuśalapakṣe prayuktasya dṛṣṭa eva dharme bhuktasamanantaraṁ tasminneva vā divase agurukaḥ kāyo bhavati |
that for him, applied to the wholesome side, [whether] in the visible-here-and-now, immediately after eating or on that very day, the body is not heavy,
sbh1.3:39.141
karmaṇyaśca bhavati, prahāṇakṣamaśca, yenāsya tvaritatvaritaṁ cittaṁ samādhīyate |
and is workable, [and] fit for [pursuit of] abandonment, by which his mind is swiftly concentrated,
sbh1.3:39.142
alpakṛcchreṇāśvāsapraśvāsāḥ pravartante |
in-and-out breaths proceed with little difficulty,
sbh1.3:39.143
styānamiddhaṁ cittaṁ na paryavanaha hya ti |
sloth-and-torpor does not envelop the mind,
sbh1.3:39.144
yenāyaṁ bhavyo bhavati |
by which he becomes fit
sbh1.3:39.145
pratibalaśca |
and capable,
sbh1.3:39.146
kṣipramevāprāptasya prāptaye, anadhigatasyādhigamāya, asākṣātkṛtasya sākṣātkriyāyai |
[and] swiftly [becomes fit] for the attainment of the not-yet-attained, for the realization of the not-realized, for directly realizing what has not yet been directly realized.
sbh1.3:39.147
evaṁ brahmacaryānugrahāyāharati |
Thus he eats for the supporting of the holy life.
sbh1.3:40.1
kathaṁ paurāṇāṁ vedanāṁ prahāsyāmītyāharati |
How does he eat [under the rubric of] ‘old feelings I shall abandon’?
sbh1.3:40.2
tathāpi tadatītamadhvānamupādāya |
When, with respect to the past,
sbh1.3:40.3
amātrayā vā paribhuktambhavatyapaśyamvā pathyaṁ vā , apariṇate taṁ vā, yenāsya vividhaḥ kāyika ābādhaḥ samutpanno bhavati |
he has eaten either out of measure, or unwholesomely, or [eaten what is] undigested — by which manifold bodily affliction has arisen in him,
sbh1.3:40.4
tadyathā kaṇḍū ḥ , kuṣṭa ḥ , kiṭibha ḥ kilāsa iti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
namely itching, leprosy, skin-disease, pallor-disease — [as elaborated] above,
sbh1.3:40.5
tasya cābādhanidānā utpadyante śārīrikā vedanā duḥkhāstīvrāḥ, kharāḥ, kaṭukā, amanāpā amana āpā ḥ |
and on that account there arise in him bodily feelings — painful, intense, harsh, biting, disagreeable —
sbh1.3:40.6
tasyābādhasyopaśamāya tāsāṁ ca tannidānānāṁ duḥkhānāṁ vedanānāmupaśamāya hitaṁ paśyamanu pathyamanu kūlamānulomikaṁ vaidyopadiṣṭena vidhinā bhaiṣajyaṁ pratiṣevate |
[then,] for the calming of that affliction and the calming of those painful feelings caused by it, seeing what is wholesome, what is suitable, what is favorable, what is conformable, by the method prescribed by the physician he uses medicine,
sbh1.3:40.7
sāṁpreyaṁ cāhāramāharati |
and eats food in conformity [with the prescription],
sbh1.3:40.8
yenāsyotpannasyābādhasya tannidānānāṁ ca duḥkhānāṁ vedanānāṁ prahāṇaṁ bhavatyevaṁ paurāṇāmvedanāṁ prahāsyāmītyāhāramāharati |
by which the abandonment of that arisen affliction and the painful feelings caused by it occurs. Thus he takes food [thinking] ‘old feelings I shall abandon.’
sbh1.3:40.9
sa varttamānamadhvānamupādāya sukhī, arogo, balavānnāmātrayā vā paribhuṁkte |
With respect to the present, [being] healthy, well, [and] strong, he does not eat out of measure,
sbh1.3:40.10
apathya tā apariṇate, kāye nāsyāgatamadhvānamupādāya cāsyānāgatamadhvānamupādāya śvo vā, uttaraśvo vā, viṣūcikā vā kāye saṁtiṣṭheta |
[nor anything] unwholesome [or] undigested in the body, [so that] with respect to his future state — tomorrow or the day after — cholera would not arise in his body,
sbh1.3:40.11
anyatamānyatamo vā kāye kāyika ābādhassamutpadyeta |
nor any other bodily affliction arise —
sbh1.3:40.12
tadyathā kaṇḍū ḥ , kuṣṭa ḥ , kiṭibha ḥ , kilāsa iti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
namely itching, leprosy, skin-disease, pallor-disease — [as elaborated] above,
sbh1.3:40.13
yannidānā utpadyeranchā ñchā rīrikā vedanā ḥ pūrvavat |
on account of which bodily feelings would arise [as] above.
sbh1.3:40.14
evaṁ ca navāṁ vedanāṁ notpādayiṣyāmītyāharati |
Thus also [under the rubric] ‘new feelings I shall not produce’ — he eats.
sbh1.3:40.15
kathaṁ yātrā me bhaviṣyati |
How [is the rubric] ‘continuation shall be for me’?
sbh1.3:40.16
varṇaṁ ca sukhaṁ cānavadyatāṁ tā ca |
[And] ‘complexion and comfort and blamelessness
sbh1.3:40.17
sparśavihāratā cetyāharati |
and ease of contact [shall be for me]’ — [thus] he eats.
sbh1.3:40.18
yattāvad bhukto jīvatītyevaṁ yātrā bhavati |
That, having eaten, he lives — thus there is continuation.
sbh1.3:40.19
yatpunarjighatsādaurbalyamu ma panayati |
That hunger-weakness is removed —
sbh1.3:40.20
evamasya varṇaṁ bhavati |
thus his complexion is [maintained].
sbh1.3:40.21
yatpunaḥ paurāṇāṁ vedanāṁ prajahāti |
That he abandons old feelings
sbh1.3:40.22
navāṁ co ca no tpādayatyevamasya sukhaṁ bhavati |
and does not produce new ones — thus there is his comfort.
sbh1.3:40.23
yat punardharmeṇa piṇḍapātaṁ paryeṣṭyāraktaḥ paryeṣyāraktaḥ , asaktaḥ iti vistareṇa pūrvavadevamanavadyatā bhavati |
That he seeks alms-food by Dharma, unattached, unclinging — [as elaborated] above — thus there is [his] blamelessness.
sbh1.3:40.24
yatpunarbhuktavato na gurukaḥ kāyo bhavati, karmaṇyaśca bhavati, prahāṇakṣamo vistareṇa pūrvavadevamasya sparśavihāratā bhavati |
That, for one who has eaten, the body is not heavy, is workable, fit for [pursuit of] abandonment — [as elaborated] above — thus there is his ease of contact.
sbh1.3:40.25
tenāha pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
Therefore he says: ‘with reflection he takes food,
sbh1.3:40.26
na dravārthaṁ, na madārthaṁ, na maṇḍanārthamiti |
not for frivolity, not for intoxication, not for adornment,’
sbh1.3:40.27
vistareṇa pūrvavadayaṁ tāvad bhojane mātrajñatāyā vistaravibhāgaḥ |
[the rest] as elaborated above. — This is the detailed division of knowing the right measure in food.
sbh1.3:40.28
samāsārthaḥ punaḥ katama ḥ āha |
What is the summary meaning? Answer:
sbh1.3:40.29
yaśca yañca paribhuṅakte |
what and what he enjoys,
sbh1.3:40.30
yathā ca paribhuṁkte |
and how he enjoys [it].
sbh1.3:40.31
yaduta kabaḍaṁkāramāhāraṁ, manthā vā, a pūpā vā, odanakulmāṣaṁ vā vistareṇa pūrvavat |
Namely, morsel-made food: barley-meal, cakes, rice, porridge, [as elaborated] above.
sbh1.3:40.32
kathaṁ paribhuṁkte |
How does he enjoy [it]?
sbh1.3:40.33
pratisaṁkhyāya paribhuṁkte |
With reflection he enjoys [it],
sbh1.3:40.34
na dravārthaṁ, na madārthaṁ na maṇḍanārthamiti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
not for frivolity, not for intoxication, not for adornment, [as elaborated] above.
sbh1.3:40.35
punaraparaṁ ḥ samāsārthaḥ |
Further, another summary meaning:
sbh1.3:40.36
pratipakṣaparigṛhītaṁ ca paribhuṁkte |
he enjoys [food] taken hold of by the antidote,
sbh1.3:40.37
kāmasukhallikānta vi varjitañca |
avoiding the extreme of sense-pleasure,
sbh1.3:40.38
ātmaklamathāntavivarjitañca bahmacaryānugrahāya |
and avoiding the extreme of self-mortification — for the supporting of the holy life.
sbh1.3:40.39
yadāha |
[How is the sense-pleasure extreme avoided?] When he says,
sbh1.3:40.40
pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
‘with reflection he takes food,’
sbh1.3:40.41
kathaṁ kāmasukhallikāntavivarjitaṁ |
by this the sense-pleasure-extreme is avoided;
sbh1.3:40.42
yadāha |
[also when] he says
sbh1.3:40.43
na dravārthaṁ, na madārthaṁ, na maṇḍanārtha, na vibhūṣaṇārthamiti |
‘not for frivolity, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for embellishment.’
sbh1.3:40.44
kathamātmaklamathāntavivarjitaṁ |
How is the self-mortification extreme avoided?
sbh1.3:40.45
yadāha |
When he says:
sbh1.3:40.46
jighatsoparataye, paurāṇāṁ ca vedanāṁ prahāsyāmi |
‘for the stilling of hunger; old feelings I shall abandon
sbh1.3:40.47
navāñca notpādayiṣyāmi |
and new ones I shall not produce;
sbh1.3:40.48
yātrā ca me bhaviṣyati |
I shall have continuation,
sbh1.3:40.49
balaṁ ca sukhaṁ ceti |
strength, and comfort.’
sbh1.3:40.50
kathaṁ bahmacaryānugrahāya paribhuṁkte |
How does he enjoy [food] for the supporting of the holy life?
sbh1.3:40.51
yadāha |
When he says:
sbh1.3:40.52
brahmacaryānugrahāya |
‘for the supporting of the holy life,
sbh1.3:40.53
anavadyatā ca |
and blamelessness,
sbh1.3:40.54
sparśavihāratā ca me bhaviṣyatīti |
and ease of contact will be for me.’
sbh1.3:40.55
punaraparaḥ samāsārthaḥ |
Further, another summary meaning:
sbh1.3:40.56
dvayamidaṁ bhojanaṁ, cābhojanaṁ ca |
this [taking of food] is twofold — eating and non-eating.
sbh1.3:40.57
tatrābhojanaṁ yat sarveṇa sarvaṁ sarvathā kiṁcinna paribhuṁkte |
Non-eating: when, completely, entirely, in every way, he does not enjoy anything,
sbh1.3:40.58
abhuṁjānaśca mriyate |
and, not eating, dies.
sbh1.3:40.59
tatra bhojanaṁ dvividhaṁ |
Eating is twofold:
sbh1.3:40.60
samabhojanaṁ, viṣamabhojanaṁ ca |
balanced eating and unbalanced eating.
sbh1.3:40.61
tatra samabhojanaṁ |
Balanced eating:
sbh1.3:40.62
yannātyalpaṁ nātiprabhūtaṁ, nāpathyaṁ, nāpariṇatena saṁkliṣṭaṁ |
[eating] neither too little nor too much, not unwholesome, not defiled by what is undigested.
sbh1.3:40.63
tatra viṣamabhojanaṁ |
Unbalanced eating: [eating]
sbh1.3:40.64
yadya da tyalpamatiprabhūtaṁ ca |
either too little or too much;
sbh1.3:40.65
apariṇate taṁ vā, apathyaṁ vā, saṁkliṣṭaṁ vā paribhuṁkte |
[or] he enjoys [what is] undigested, unwholesome, or defiled.
sbh1.3:40.66
tatra samabhojane nātyalpabhojane jighatsādaurbalyamanutpannaṁ |
By balanced eating — by non-too-little eating — unarisen hunger-and-weakness
sbh1.3:40.67
notpādayati |
he does not produce,
sbh1.3:40.68
utpannaṁ prajahāti |
[and] arisen [hunger-and-weakness] he abandons.
sbh1.3:40.69
tatra nātiprabhūtabhojane na sama viṣamabhojanena gurukaḥ kāyo bhavatyakarmaṇyaḥ aprahāṇakṣamo vistareṇa pūrvavat |
In non-too-much eating, by balanced eating, the body does not become heavy, unworkable, unfit for [pursuit of] abandonment, [as elaborated] above.
sbh1.3:40.70
tatra pariṇatabhojanena, samabhojanena paurāṇāṁ ca vedanāṁ prajahāti |
By digested eating — by balanced eating — he abandons old feelings
sbh1.3:40.71
navāñca notpādayiṣyatyevamasya yātrā bhavati |
and does not produce new ones; thus there is his continuation,
sbh1.3:40.72
balaṁ ca, sukhaṁ ca, asaṁkliṣṭabhojanena |
strength, and comfort. By undefiled eating,
sbh1.3:40.73
samabhojanena anavadyatā ca bhavati |
[and] by balanced eating, there is blamelessness
sbh1.3:40.74
sparśavihāratā ca |
and ease of contact.
sbh1.3:40.75
tatrātyalpabhojanaṁ yena jīvati |
Too-little eating: that by which he barely lives.
sbh1.3:40.76
atiprabhūtabhojanaṁ |
Too-much eating:
sbh1.3:40.77
yenāsya gurubhārādhyākrāntaśca kāyo bhavati |
that by which his body is overburdened
sbh1.3:40.78
na ca kālena bha bhu ktampariṇamati |
and what is eaten is not digested in time.
sbh1.3:40.79
tatrāpariṇatabhojanena viṣūcikā kāye saṁtiṣṭhate |
By undigested eating, cholera lodges in the body,
sbh1.3:40.80
anyatamānyatamo vā kāye kāyika ābādhaḥ |
or some other bodily affliction
sbh1.3:40.81
samutpadyate |
arises.
sbh1.3:40.82
yathā apariṇatabhojanenaivamapathyabhojanena |
As with undigested eating, so with unwholesome eating.
sbh1.3:40.83
tatrāyamapathyabhojane viśeṣaḥ |
The distinction in unwholesome eating is this:
sbh1.3:40.84
doṣaḥ pracayaṁ gacchati |
the doṣa [humoral imbalance] increases,
sbh1.3:40.85
kharaṁ vābādhaṁ spṛśati |
or a harsh affliction touches him.
sbh1.3:40.86
tatra saṁkliṣṭabhojanena adharmeṇa piṇḍapātaṁ paryeṣya raktaḥ paribhuṁkte |
By defiled eating, [the meaning is that] having sought alms-food by non-Dharma, he enjoys [it] with attachment,
sbh1.3:40.87
sakto, gṛddho, grathita iti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
[with] clinging, [as elaborated] above.
sbh1.3:40.88
iti yaḥ samabhojanaṁ ca paribhuṁkte |
Whoever, then, enjoys balanced eating
sbh1.3:40.89
viṣamabhojanaṁ ca parivarjayati |
and avoids unbalanced eating
sbh1.3:40.90
tasmād bhojane samakārītyucyate |
is therefore called ‘acting evenly with regard to food’ [bhojane samakārī].
Bhojane samakārī ('acting evenly with regard to food') is the local summary label at 40.90; bhojane mātrajñatā ('knowing the right measure in food') names the larger requisite at 40.121. The measure is glossed concretely by the analysis at 40.59-74: balanced (sama) versus unbalanced (viṣama) eating, where balanced cuts across four dimensions — quantity (not too little, not too much), digestibility, wholesomeness, and freedom-from-defilement-in-procurement. The Vinaya formula paryāptaṁ (sufficient) and yathāpramāṇa (according to measure) point to the same idea.
sbh1.3:40.91
bhojane samakāritaiṣā ebhirākaurarākhyātā, uttānā, vivṛtā, saṁprakāśitā |
This even-acting with regard to food is declared, made manifest, opened up, [and] proclaimed by these modes —
sbh1.3:40.92
yaduta pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
namely, ‘with reflection he takes food,
sbh1.3:40.93
na dravārthaṁ, na madārthaṁ, na maṇḍanārthaṁ, na vibhūṣaṇārthamiti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
not for frivolity, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for embellishment,’ [as elaborated] above.
sbh1.3:40.94
tatra yastā yattā vadāha |
In that he first says,
sbh1.3:40.95
pratisaṁkhyāyāhāramāharati |
‘with reflection he takes food,
sbh1.3:40.96
na dravārthaṁ, na madārthaṁ, na maṇḍanārthaṁ, na vidū bhū ṣaṇārthaṁ |
not for frivolity, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for embellishment,
sbh1.3:40.97
, yāvadevāsya kāyasya sthitaye, yāpanāyai, anena tāvadabhojanaṁ na ca pratikṣipati |
only for the sustaining of this body, for its support,’ — by this much, non-eating is rejected.
sbh1.3:40.98
yatpunarāha |
Again, when he says,
sbh1.3:40.99
jighatsoparataye, brahmacaryānugrahāya vistareṇa yāvat sparśavihāratāyai, anena viṣamabhojanaṁ pratikṣipati |
‘for the stilling of hunger, for the supporting of the holy life,’ [and so on] up through ‘ease of contact’ — by this unbalanced eating is rejected.
sbh1.3:40.100
kathaṁ ca punarviṣamabhojanaṁ punaratiprabhūtabhojanaṁ pratikṣipati |
How is unbalanced eating — and specifically too-much eating — rejected?
sbh1.3:40.101
yattāvadāha brahmacaryānugrahāyānenātiprabhūtabhojanaṁ pratikṣipati |
When he first says ‘for the supporting of the holy life,’ by this too-much eating is rejected.
sbh1.3:40.102
yadāha |
When he says:
sbh1.3:40.103
paurāṇāṁ ca vedanāṁ prahāsyāmi |
‘old feelings I shall abandon
sbh1.3:40.104
, navāṁ ca notpādayiṣyāmītyanenāpariṇatabhojanata me tāma pathyabhojanatāṁ ca pratikṣipati |
and new ones I shall not produce,’ by this the undigested-eating-state and the unwholesome-eating-state are rejected.
sbh1.3:40.105
yadāha |
When he says,
sbh1.3:40.106
yātrā ca me bhaviṣyati, balaṁ cānenātyalpabhojanatāṁ pra tāmapra bhūtabhojanatāṁ ca darśayati pratikṣipati |
‘I shall have continuation and strength,’ by this he indicates [the right amount, neither] the too-little-eating-state, and rejects the too-much-eating-state.
sbh1.3:40.107
yadāha |
When he says,
sbh1.3:40.108
sukhaṁ ca me bhaviṣyatītyanena pariṇatabhojanatāṁ ca darśayati |
‘comfort will be for me,’ by this he indicates the digested-eating-state.
sbh1.3:40.109
yadāha |
When he says,
sbh1.3:40.110
sukhaṁ ca me bhaviṣyatīti pathyabhojanatāṁ ca darśayati |
‘comfort will be for me,’ he also indicates the wholesome-eating-state.
sbh1.3:40.111
yadāha |
When he says,

‘blamelessness will be for me, and ease of contact,’ he indicates the undefiled-eating-state.
sbh1.3:40.113
yosāvadharmeṇa piṇḍapātaṁ paryeṣya raktaḥ paribhuṁkte |
He who, having sought alms-food by non-Dharma, enjoys [it] with attachment,
sbh1.3:40.114

[with] clinging, [as elaborated] above —
sbh1.3:40.115

he enjoys [defiled food], and faulty for him is [his] eating.
sbh1.3:40.116
tasyaiva ca kuśalapakṣaprayuktasya pratisaṁlayane, yoge, manasikāre, svādhyāye, arthacintāyāṁ ta eva pāpakā akuśalā vitarkāścittamanuvasravanti ye a sya taṁ nityāṁ tannityāṁ , tatpravaṇāṁ, tatprābhorāṁ tatprābhārāṁ cittasantatiṁ pravarttayanti |
For one applied to the wholesome side — in seclusion, in yoga, in mental engagement, in recitation, in reflection on the meaning — those very evil, unwholesome thoughts flow into the mind, which conduct his mental continuum oriented toward that [defilement], leaning toward that, abundant in that,
sbh1.3:40.117
yenāsya sparśavihāreṇa sparśavihāro na bhavati |
by which there is for him no ease-of-contact-dwelling.
sbh1.3:40.118
sā ceyaṁ dvividhā sparśavihāratā atiprabhūtabhojanaparivarjanācca yenāsya na gurukaḥ kāyo bhavatyakarmaṇyaḥ, aprahāṇakṣama iti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
And this ease-of-contact-dwelling is twofold: [first,] by avoidance of too-much-eating, by which the body does not become heavy, unworkable, unfit for [pursuit of] abandonment, [as elaborated] above;
sbh1.3:40.119

by the other [aspect] — non-making of relish — by which there is no non-ease-of-contact-dwelling caused by thought-disturbance.
sbh1.3:40.120
tadevaṁ sati sarvairebhiḥ padairbhojane samakāritā vyākhyātā bhavati |
Such being the case, all these terms become explanatory of acting evenly with regard to food.
sbh1.3:40.121
iyamucyate bhojane mātrajñatā |
This is called knowing the right measure in food,
sbh1.3:41.1
vistarataḥ saṁkṣepataśca |
in detail and in brief.
sbh1.3:42.1
pūrvarātrāpararātraṁ jāgarikānuyuktatā katamā |
What is application to wakefulness in the early and late watches of the night?
sbh1.3:42.2
tatra katamaḥ pūrvarātraḥ -mat pūrvarātram |
What is the early [watch]?
sbh1.3:42.3
katamo mada pararātraḥ tram |
What is the late watch?
sbh1.3:42.4
katamojāgarikāyogaḥ |
What is application to wakefulness?
sbh1.3:42.5
katamā jāgarikāyogasyānuyuktatā |
What is application to wakefulness-yoga?
sbh1.3:42.6
tatrāya yaṁ - tredaṁ sāyāhnaṁ ardharātraḥ traṁ , sāyāhnaṁ sūryāstaṁgamanamupādāya yo rātryāḥ pūrvabhāgaḥ, so ’tirekaṁ prahāraṁ sātirekaḥ praharaḥ |
Here, the early watch is the evening period: taking its start from sunset, it is the first part of the night, a little more than one watch.
The printed/extracted line is noisy around sāyāhnaṁ ardharātraḥ ... sāyāhnaṁ, but the functional definition is clear from the following words: the early watch begins at sunset and occupies the first part of the night, a little more than one watch. The late watch is defined separately at 42.165-166.
sbh1.3:42.7
tatrāyaṁ jāgā ga rikāyogaḥ |
[Now] this application to wakefulness —
sbh1.3:42.8
yadāha |
[the text describes it] when it says:
sbh1.3:42.9
divā caṁkramaniṣadyābhyāmāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
‘by day, by walking and sitting, he purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas;
sbh1.3:42.10
pariśodhya, bahi r vihārasya pādau prakṣālya vihāraṁ praviśya dakṣiṇena pārśvena śayyāṁ kalpayati |
having purified [the mind], washing his feet outside the dwelling, entering the dwelling, he lies down on the right side,
sbh1.3:42.11
pāde pādamādhāya ālokasaṁjñī smṛtaḥ |
foot placed on foot, perceiving light, mindful,
sbh1.3:42.12
saṁprajāna n utthānasaṁjñāmeva manasi kurvan, sa rātryāḥ paścime yāmelaghu laghveva prativibudhya caṁkramaniṣadyābhyāmāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
fully aware, attending to the perception of rising; in the last watch of the night, rising lightly, very lightly, by walking and sitting he purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas.’
sbh1.3:42.13
tatreyaṁ jāgarikānuyogasyānuyuktatā |
This is the application of the wakefulness-yoga.
sbh1.3:42.14
yathāpi tadbuddhasya bhagavataḥ śrāvakajāgarikāyogasya śrotā |
When [the practitioner] hears of the wakefulness-yoga of the Blessed Buddha’s disciples,
sbh1.3:42.15
tatra śikṣitukāmo bhavati |
he there becomes desirous of training:
sbh1.3:42.16
yathābhūtasyāsya yajjāgarikāyogamārabhya buddhānujñātaṁ jāgarikānuyogaṁ sampādayiṣyāmīti yaśchando, vīyaṁ vīryaṁ , vyāyāmo, niṣkramaḥ |
‘Just as it [really] is, taking up this wakefulness-yoga authorized by the Buddhas, I shall accomplish the application to wakefulness’ — that desire, [that] energy,
sbh1.3:42.17
parākramasthānaprārambhaḥ |
[that] effort, [that] vigor,
sbh1.3:42.18
utsāha utsūḍhiraprativāṇiścetasaḥ |
advance, beginning of [the] stamina-stance,
sbh1.3:42.19
saṁgrahaḥ sāvadyaṁ dyaḥ |
enthusiasm, lifting-up, non-turning-back of mind,
sbh1.3:42.20
tatra kathaṁ caṁkramaniṣadyābhyāmāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
[and] mental gathering [saṁgraha] — [Damaged/Conjectural: all these are] blameless [marks of right effort].
The local root has saṁgrahaḥ and then a damaged/suspect sāvadyaṁ dyaḥ. The earlier bracket samādāna was not local. The English now follows saṁgraha as 'mental gathering' and marks the likely intended 'blameless' evaluation conjectural/corrupt, since printed sāvadya would mean blameworthy.
sbh1.3:42.21
divā ucyate |
How, by walking and sitting, does he purify the mind of obscuring dharmas?
sbh1.3:42.22
sūya rya syābhyudgamanasamayamupādāya yāvadastagamanasamayā c , caṁkrama ucyate |
[On the terms used:] ‘Day’ —
sbh1.3:42.23
āyatāni āyatanāni |
is taken as the period from the sun’s rising up to its setting. ‘Walking’ means
sbh1.3:42.24
vipulamāpite pṛthivīpradeśe gamanapratyāgamanapra yoga yuktaṁ |
extended
sbh1.3:42.25

back-and-forth movement on a wide, prepared piece of ground,
sbh1.3:42.26
yathāpīhaikatyo maṁcevā, pīṭhe vā, tṛṇasaṁstare vā niṣīdati |
[as] a bodily practice. ‘Sitting’ —
sbh1.3:42.27
paryaṅkamābhujya |
is when someone here sits on a couch, on a stool, on a grass-mat,
sbh1.3:42.28
ṛjuṁ kāyaṁ praṇidhāyābhimukhīṁ smṛtimupasthāpya |
folding the legs cross-wise,
sbh1.3:42.29
āvaraṇānyucyante pañca nivaraṇāni |
having set the body upright, establishing mindfulness to the fore.
sbh1.3:42.30
āvaraṇīyā dharmā ye nivaraṇasthānīyā dharmā nivaraṇāparakāste punaḥ katame |
‘Obscurations’ means: the five hindrances [nīvaraṇa].
sbh1.3:42.31
kāmachando kāmacchando , vyāpāda ḥ , styāna naṁ , middhauddhatyaṁ, kaukṛtyaṁ, vicikitsā, a śubhatā, pratighanimittamandhakāraḥ, jñātijanapadāmaravitarkapaurāṇasya ca hasitakrīḍitarasitaparivāritasyānusmṛtiḥ, trayaścādhvānaḥ |
Obscuring dharmas are those that stand in [the same] place as the hindrances. What other obscuration-place dharmas [are there]? Sense-desire, ill-will, sloth-and-torpor, restlessness-and-regret, doubt; the perception of attractiveness, the sign of resistance, [mental] darkness, recollection of kin, country, [or] recreation, past laughter, play, sound, gatherings; and [unwise reflection on] the three times —
The obscuring-dharmas list here follows the standard hindrance-food pattern: attractiveness-perception feeds sense-desire, the resistance-sign feeds ill-will, darkness feeds sloth-and-torpor, and so on. The extracted a śubhatā is therefore read contextually as śubhatā ('attractiveness'), not aśubhatā ('unattractiveness'), which would be an antidote rather than an obscuring condition.
sbh1.3:42.32
tryadhvagatā cāyoniśo dharmacintā |
[that is,] unwise dharma-reflection that pertains to the three times.
sbh1.3:42.33
ebhyaḥ kathaṁ caṁkramaṇacittaṁ pariśodhayati |
From these, how, by walking, does he purify the mind?
sbh1.3:42.34
katibhyaśca pariśodhayati |
[And] from how many [does he so purify it]?
sbh1.3:42.35
styānamiddhātsyānamiddhāhārakāccāvaraṇāt pariśodhayati |
From sloth-and-torpor and from the [other] obscuration that is the food of sloth-and-torpor he purifies [the mind].
sbh1.3:42.36
ālokanimittamanena sādhu ca, suṣṭhu ca, sugṛhītaṁ bhavati |
The light-sign [āloka-nimitta] is by him well, thoroughly grasped,
The local term is āloka-nimitta ('light-sign'), not āloka-saṁjñā ('perception of light'). The latter is a related practice term elsewhere, but here the practitioner has grasped a sign, so the bracket follows the root.
sbh1.3:42.37
sumanasikṛtaṁ, suja ju ṣṭaṁ |
well attended to, well savored,
sbh1.3:42.38

He, with mind accompanied by light, accompanied by radiance — under cover or in the open, while walking on the walking-path — by some clarifying object brightens the mind,

sbh1.3:42.44

namely, [from the twelve aṅgas:] sūtra, geya, vyākaraṇa, gāthā, udāna, nidāna, avadāna, itivṛttaka, jātaka, vaipulya, adbhuta-dharma, [and] upadeśa — wherein, in many ways, sloth-and-torpor is censured and deplored,
sbh1.3:42.45

and the abandonment of sloth-and-torpor
sbh1.3:42.46

sbh1.3:42.47

sbh1.3:42.48

sbh1.3:42.49

sbh1.3:42.50

or fixes his gaze on the four constellations of stars and planets, [or] moistens the face with water.
sbh1.3:42.51
evamasya tatstyānamiddhaparyavasthānaṁ anutpannaṁ ca notpadyate, utpannaṁ ca prativigacchatyevamanena tasmādāvaraṇīyā d dharmāccittaṁ pariśodhitaṁ bhavati |
Thus his obsession by sloth-and-torpor, when unarisen, does not arise; and when arisen, departs. Thus by him the mind is purified of that obscuring dharma.

By sitting, from which obscuring dharmas does he purify the mind?
sbh1.3:42.53

From sense-desire, ill-will, restlessness-and-regret, doubt, and the [other] dharmas that feed them.
sbh1.3:42.54
sa utpanne vā kāmacchandaparyavasthāne prativinodanāyānutpanne vā dūrīkaraṇāya, niṣadya, paryaṅkamābhujya, ṛjuṁ kāyaṁ praṇidhāya, pratimukhāṁ khīṁ smṛtimupasthāpya, vinīlakaṁ vā, vipūyakamvā, vima bha drāma tma kamvā, vyādhmātakamvā, vi khādi takamvā, vilohitakamvā, asthiṁ asthi vā, śaṁkalikāṁ vā, anyatamānyatamaṁ vā bhadrakaṁ samādhinimittaṁ manasi karoti |
When the obsession of sense-desire has arisen, for its dispelling — or when it has not arisen, for its remoteness — sitting cross-legged, having set the body upright, having established mindfulness to the fore, he attends to the dark-livid, the festering, the worm-eaten, the swollen, the chewed, the bloody, the bone, the skeleton, or some other excellent sign of concentration;
sbh1.3:42.55
ye vā dharmāḥ kāmarāgaprahāṇamevārabhya kāmarāgaprahāṇāyodgṛhītā bhavanti |
or [he draws on] those dharmas which, [touching on] the abandonment of sense-lust itself, have been grasped for the abandonment of sense-lust,
sbh1.3:42.56
dhṛtā, vacasā parijitā, manasā anvīkṣitā ḥ , dṛṣṭyā supratividdhā ḥ , tadyathā sūtraṁ, geyaṁ, vyākaraṇamiti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
retained, mastered by voice, examined by mind, well penetrated by view — namely sūtra, geya, vyākaraṇa, [as elaborated] above:
sbh1.3:42.57
ye anekaparyāyeṇa kāmarāgaṁ, kāmacchandaṁ, kāmālayaṁ, kāmaniyantiṁ, kāmādhyavasānaṁ vigarhanti, vivarṇayanti |
those [passages] which in many ways censure and depreciate sense-lust, sense-desire, sensual attachment, sensual compulsion, sensual fixation,
Kāmaniyanti belongs to the sense-lust cluster (kāmarāga, kāmacchanda, kāmālaya, kāmaniyanti, kāmādhyavasāna). It is a binding/compulsion toward sensuality, not sense-restraint. The English now keeps the sequence as sense-lust, sense-desire, sensual attachment, sensual compulsion, and sensual fixation.
sbh1.3:42.58
vijugupsayanti |
deplore them,
sbh1.3:42.59

and praise the abandonment of sense-lust in many ways,

sitting thus, with a mind accompanied by loving-kindness — free of enmity, free of opposition, free of harm, vast, become great, boundless, well cultivated — resolving on one direction, having brought it to mind, having attained [the state], he dwells;
sbh1.3:42.65

Likewise the second, the third, the fourth — upward, downward, sideways, the entire endless world — having recollected, having remembered, he attains-and-abides.

sbh1.3:42.67

Restored from PDF 1401-1404. The pre-existing JSON had a stitched clause anantaṁ lokaṁ tatra vicikitsānivaraṇe viśeṣaḥ collapsing the end of the vyāpāda nivaraṇa variant and the start of the vicikitsā variant, dropping the closing of the vyāpāda discussion (with the canonical smāritvā smṛtvā upasampadya viharati refrain) and the entire auddhatya-kaukṛtya nivaraṇa variant (with its śamatha-style instruction: cittaṁ sthāpayati saṁsthāpayati saṁviśodhayati ekotīkaroti samādhatte). The five-nivaraṇa structure now reads in source order: kāmacchanda → vyāpāda → auddhatya-kaukṛtya → vicikitsā (with styānamiddha implicit in the earlier yathā kāmacchande tathā pattern).
