sbh1.3:42.93
yadutānityato vā, duḥkhato vā, śūnyato vā, anātmato vā , anityeṣu, duḥkheṣu, śūnyeṣu, anātmasu dharmeṣu sa evaṁ yoniśo manasi kurvan, buddhe a pi niṣkāṅkṣo bhavati, nirvicikitsaḥ |
namely as impermanent, as suffering, as empty, as non-self — [seeing] impermanent, suffering, empty, non-self dharmas [as such]. He, thus wisely attending, is without doubt and without indecision about the Buddha,
sbh1.3:42.94
dharme, saṁghe, duḥkhe, samudaye, nirodhe, mārge, hetau, hetusamutpanneṣu dharmeṣu niṣkāṅkṣo bhavati |
[without doubt] about the Dharma, the Saṅgha, suffering, origin, cessation, path, cause, [and] about dependently-originated dharmas — without doubt,
sbh1.3:42.95
nirvicikitsaḥ |
without indecision.
sbh1.3:42.96
śeṣaṁ pūrvavat |
The rest [is to be understood] as above.
sbh1.3:42.97
tatra vyāpāde vaktavyaṁ |
Now, regarding ill-will, it should be said:
sbh1.3:42.98
yo a nena pratighaṁ pratighanimittaṁ cārabhya, tasya ca prahāṇāya, dharmā udgṛhītā iti vistaraḥ |
the [counter-]dharmas grasped by him with respect to resistance and the resistance-sign, [and grasped] for its abandonment, [as detailed] above;
sbh1.3:42.99
auddhatyakaukṛtye vaktavyaṁ |
regarding restlessness-and-regret, it should be said:
sbh1.3:42.100
anenauddhatyakaukṛtyamārabhya tasya ca prahāṇāya dharmā udgṛhītā iti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
the [counter-]dharmas grasped, with respect to restlessness-and-regret, [and grasped] for its abandonment, [as elaborated] above;
sbh1.3:42.101
vicikitsāyāmvaktavyaṁ |
regarding doubt, it should be said:
sbh1.3:42.102
ye anena vicikitsāmārabhya tasyāśca prahāṇāya dharmā udgṛhītā iti vistareṇa pūrvavadityanena kāmacha ccha ndanivaraṇād vyāpādastyānamiddhauddhatyakaukṛtyavicikitsānivaraṇā c cittaṁ viśodhitaṁ bhavati |
those [counter-]dharmas grasped by him with respect to doubt, [and grasped] for its abandonment, [as elaborated] above. By this [practice], the mind is purified of the sense-desire-hindrance, [and] of the ill-will-, sloth-and-torpor-, restlessness-and-regret-, [and] doubt-hindrances,
sbh1.3:42.103
tadāhārakebhyaśca dharmebhya āvaraṇīyebhyastenāha caṁkramaniṣadyābhyāmāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
and of the obscuring dharmas that are their food. Therefore he says: ‘by walking and sitting he purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas.’
sbh1.3:42.104
yā caiṣā dharmādhipateyā āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittasya pariśodhanā |
And this is the dharma-predominant [purification of mind from obscuring dharmas].
The three ādhipateyya (predominances) — dharma-, self-, and world-predominance — is a canonical Yogācāra schema applied to motivation for abandoning the hindrances. Dharma-predominance (42.21–103) is the practitioner's own analytical work with antidotes; self-predominance (42.106–109) is the motive of self-respect ('let me not be known as a hindrance-maker'); world-predominance (42.110–117) is the motive of accountability before the Teacher, the deities, the wise, and the saṅgha. The threefold framework reappears in the Yogācārabhūmi as the standard motivational analysis whenever a single practice has multiple subjective angles.
sbh1.3:42.105
asti punarātmādhipateyā, lokādhipateyā cāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścitta pariśodhanā |
There is also the self-predominant and world-predominant purification of mind from obscuring dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.106
ātmādhipateyā katamā |
What is the self-predominant?
sbh1.3:42.107
yathāpi tadutpanne anyatamānyatamasminnivaraṇe ātmata eva pratirūpatāmviditvā, utpannaṁ nivaraṇaṁ nādhivāsayate ti |
When, on the arising of some particular hindrance, recognizing its unsuitability for oneself, he does not accommodate the arisen hindrance —
sbh1.3:42.108
prajahāti, vinodayati, vyantīkaroti |
he abandons, dispels, puts an end to [it, thinking]:
sbh1.3:42.109
tena nivaraṇenātmānaṁ na jñāyamānaḥ, cetasa upakleśakareṇa, prajñādaurbalyakareṇa, vighātapakṣyeṇaivamasāvātmānamevādhipatiṁ kṛtvā āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
‘Let me not be known on account of that hindrance as a maker of mental corruption, a maker of wisdom-weakness, [as one] on the side of dejection.’ Thus, making the self predominant, he purifies the mind from the obscuring dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.110
kathaṁ lokamadhipatiṁ kṛtvā āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
How, making the world predominant, does he purify the mind from obscuring dharmas?
sbh1.3:42.111
ihāsyā sya nivaraṇe samutpanne, utpattikāle vā pratyupasthite |
Here, when a hindrance has arisen for him, or its time of arising is present,
sbh1.3:42.112
evaṁ bhavatyahaṁ cedanutpannaṁ nivaraṇamutpādayeyaṁ, śāstā me apavaded, devatā api, vijñā api, sabrahmacāriṇo a dharmatayā vigarhayeyuriti |
it occurs to him: ‘If I should produce an unarisen hindrance, the Teacher would reproach me, and the deities, the wise, [and] my co-religionists would censure me, regarding [it] as non-Dharma.’
sbh1.3:42.113
sa lokamevādhipatiṁ kṛtvā āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
[Thus,] making the world predominant, he purifies the mind from the obscuring dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.114
ihāsya nivaraṇe samutpanne, utpattikāle vā pratyupasthite, evaṁ bhavatyahaṁ cedanutpannaṁ nivaraṇamutpādayeyaṁ |
Here, when a hindrance has arisen for him, or its time of arising is present, it occurs to him: ‘If I should produce an unarisen hindrance,
sbh1.3:42.115
śāstā me apavaded, devatā api |
the Teacher would reproach me, and the deities,
sbh1.3:42.116
vijñā api, sabrahmacāriṇo a dharmatayā vigarhayeyuriti |
the wise, [and] my co-religionists would censure me, regarding [it] as non-Dharma.’
sbh1.3:42.117
sa lokamevādhipatiṁ kṛtvā, anutpannaṁ ca nivaraṇaṁ notpādayati, utpannaṁ ca śayanāsanapratiguptyarthaṁ punarlokācārañcānuvṛtto bhaviṣyatīti yāvadrātryāḥ prathame yāme caṁkramaniṣadyābhyāmāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
He, making the world predominant, does not produce an unarisen hindrance; and when [one] has arisen, for the sake of guarding his lodging and seat, he will again conform to worldly conduct. [In this way,] in the first watch of the night, by walking and sitting, he purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.118
āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhya bahirvihārasya pādau prakṣālayati |
Having purified the mind of obscuring dharmas, washing his feet outside the dwelling,
sbh1.3:42.119
prakṣālya, vihāraṁ praviśya, śayyāṁ kalpayati |
[and] having washed, entering the dwelling, he lies down —
sbh1.3:42.120
yāvadeva svasyo yau pacayikānāmmahābhūtānāṁ upacayāya |
only for the [re-]invigoration of his own native great elements [dhātu], [thinking]:
sbh1.3:42.121
upacito a yaṁ kāyaḥ karmaṇyataraśca bhaviṣyati |
‘This body, when [thus] replenished, will be more workable
sbh1.3:42.122
anukūlataraśca sātatyenai ka pakṣye kuśalapakṣaprayoge |
and more amenable in the continuous, one-sided application to the wholesome.’
sbh1.3:42.123
kenakāraṇena pārśvena śayyāṁ kalpayati |
For what reason does he lie down on [his right] side?
sbh1.3:42.124
siṁhasya prāṇinaḥ sādharmyeṇa |
Because it shares a nature with the lion.
sbh1.3:42.125
kiṁ punaratra sādharmyaṁ |
What, here, is the same-nature?
sbh1.3:42.126
siṁhaḥ prāṇaṁ prāṇī sarveṣāṁ tiryagyonigatānāṁ prāṇināṁ vikrānta, utsāhī, dṛḍhaparākramaḥ |
The lion, among all animals of the animal-womb, is the boldest, the most enthusiastic, of firm valor.
sbh1.3:42.127
bhikṣurapi jāgarikānuyukta ārabdhavīryo viharati |
The monk too, applied to wakefulness, with aroused energy, dwells
sbh1.3:42.128
vikrānta, utsāhī, dṛḍhaparākramaḥ |
bold, enthusiastic, of firm valor.
sbh1.3:42.129
atastasya siṁhopamaiva śayyā pratirūpā bhavadi ti , no tu pretaśayyā, devaśayyā, na kā ma bhogaśayyā |
Hence for him the lion-couch is appropriate — not the ghost-couch, the deity-couch, nor the sense-pleasure-enjoyer’s couch —
sbh1.3:42.130
tathā hi te sarva eva kusīdā, hīnavīryā, ca ma ndabalaparākramāḥ |
for they [whose couches those are] are all indolent, of low vigor, of slow strength and valor.
sbh1.3:42.131
api tu dharmataiṣā yaddakṣiṇena pārśvena siṁhopamāṁ śayyāṁ kalpayato na tathā gātrāṇāṁ vikṣepo bhavati |
Moreover, this is dharma-nature: for one who lies down on the right side in lion-fashion, there is not such scattering of the limbs,
sbh1.3:42.132
na ca śayānasya smṛtisaṁpramoṣo bhavati |
no forgetfulness of mindfulness for one [thus] sleeping,
sbh1.3:42.133
na ca gāḍhaṁ svapiti |
and he does not sleep deeply,
sbh1.3:42.134
pāpakāṁśca svapnāṁ pnān na paśyati |
and he does not see evil dreams.
sbh1.3:42.135
anyathā tu śayyāṁ kalpayato viparyayeṇa sarve doṣā veditavyāḥ |
For one who lies down otherwise, the contrary faults are to be understood.
sbh1.3:42.136
tenāha dakṣiṇena pārśvena śayyāṁ kalpayati |
Therefore he says: ‘he lies down on the right side.’
sbh1.3:42.137
ālokanimittamanena sūdgṛhītaṁ bhavati |
The light-sign is by him well grasped,
sbh1.3:42.138
sumanasikṛtaṁ |
well attended to,
sbh1.3:42.139
sujuṣṭaṁ, supratividdhaṁ |
well savored, well penetrated.
sbh1.3:42.140
yadeva manasi kurvan sa prabhā sahagatena cittena śayyāṁ kalpayati |
Just attending to it, with mind accompanied by light, he lies down,
sbh1.3:42.141
suptasyāpi cāsya yena na bhavati cetasaḥ |
and for him, even sleeping, the mind does not enter a
sbh1.3:42.142
andhakārāyitatvamevamālokasaṁjñīśayyāṁ kalpayati |
darkened state. Thus, perceiving light, he lies down.
sbh1.3:42.143
kathaṁ smṛta ḥ śayyāṁ kalpayati |
How, mindful, does he lie down?
sbh1.3:42.144
ya ye anenadharmā ḥ śrutā bhavanti |
Those dharmas heard by him,
sbh1.3:42.145
cintitā, bhāvitā vā, kuśalā arthopasaṁhitāstadanvayā a sya smṛtiryāvatsvapanakālānuvarttinī bhavati |
reflected on, cultivated, wholesome, connected with meaning — their consequence is mindfulness, [continuing] up to the time of sleep,
sbh1.3:42.146
yathāsya suptasyāpi ta eva dharmā jāgrato vā abhilapanti, teṣveva ca dharmeṣu taccittaṁ bahulamanuvicarati |
such that, even while he sleeps, those very dharmas speak to him as if [he were] awake, and the mind moves abundantly in those very dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.147
iti yathāsmṛtyā yathāsmṛtaḥ |
Thus, as it were by mindfulness, with mindfulness,
sbh1.3:42.148
kuśalacittaśayyāṁ kalpayati |
with wholesome mind, he lies down —
sbh1.3:42.149
avyākṛtacitto vā |
or [at least] with indeterminate [non-defiled] mind.
sbh1.3:42.150
evaṁ smṛtaḥ śayyāṁ kalpayati |
Thus, mindful, he lies down.
sbh1.3:42.151
kathaṁ saṁprajānanto jānan śayyāṁ kalpayati |
How, fully aware, does he lie down?
sbh1.3:42.152
suptasyāsya tathā smṛtasya yasminsamaye a nyatamānyatamenopakleśena cetasaḥ saṁkleśo bhavati |
For him, sleeping thus mindfully, when at any time, by some secondary defilement, mental defilement occurs,
sbh1.3:42.153
sa utpadyamānameva taṁ saṁkleśaṁ samyageva prajānāti, nādhivāsayati, prajahāti, pratividhyati |
of that arising defilement he is rightly fully aware; he does not accommodate, he abandons, he penetrates [it],
sbh1.3:42.154
pratyudāvarttayati mānasaṁ |
he turns the mind back [from it].
sbh1.3:42.155
tenocyate saṁprajānaṁ nan śayyāṁ kalpayati |
Therefore he is said to lie down fully aware.
sbh1.3:42.156
kathamutthānasaṁjñāmeva manasikurvan śayyāṁ kalpayati |
How, attending to the perception of rising, does he lie down?
sbh1.3:42.157
sa vīryasaṁpragṛhītaṁ cittaṁ kṛtvā śayyāṁ kalpayati |
He lies down having made the mind held [steady] by energy,
sbh1.3:42.158
supratibuddhikayā suharṣakta suhṛṣṭacitta stadyathā āraṇyako mṛgaḥ |
with well-awakened, well-uplifted, well-gladdened mind, just like a forest deer;
sbh1.3:42.159
no tu sarveṇa sarvaṁ viddhamavakramaṇanimnaṁ cittaṁ karoti |
he does not, completely, entirely, leave the mind plunged in [sloth],
sbh1.3:42.160
tatpravaṇaṁ |
[nor] oriented to it,
sbh1.3:42.161
na tatprābho bhā ramapi |
nor abundant in it.
sbh1.3:42.162
cāsyaivaṁ bhavati |
It occurs to him:
sbh1.3:42.163
aho batāhaṁ buddhānujñātāṁ jāgaritāṁ kāṁ sarveṇa sarvaṁ sarvathā sampādayeyamiti |
‘Oh, may I in every respect, in every way, accomplish the wakefulness authorized by the Buddhas!’
sbh1.3:42.164
tasyāśca sampādanārthaṁ tvāśaṁsena, rasena, prayogeṇa, chandagato viharatyabhiyuktaśca |
And for its accomplishment he dwells with hope, with relish, with application, with desire, and is intensely yoked.
sbh1.3:42.165
api cāsyaivaṁ bhavati |
Moreover, it occurs to him:
sbh1.3:42.166
yathāhamadya jāgarikārthamārabdhavīryo vyahārṣaṁ, kuśalānāñca dharmāṇāṁ bhāvanāyai, dakṣo a nalasa, utthānasampannaḥ, śvaḥ prabhāte, nirgatāyāṁ ca rajanyāṁ, bhūyasyā mātrayā ārabdhavīryo vihariṣyāmi utthānasampanna iti |
‘Just as today, for the sake of wakefulness with aroused energy, I have dwelt — and, for the cultivation of wholesome dharmas, skilled, non-lazy, endowed with [the capacity for] rising — tomorrow at dawn, when night has gone forth, in still greater measure I shall dwell with aroused energy, endowed with [the capacity for] rising.’
sbh1.3:42.167
tatraikayā utthānasaṁjñayā gāḍhaṁ svapiti |
Now, by [the first kind of] rising-perception he [avoids] sleeping deeply,
sbh1.3:42.168
yenāhaṁ yaṁ śaknoti laghu laghveva utthānakāle utthātuṁ, na kālātikrāntaṁ pratibudhyati |
by which he is able, lightly very lightly at rising-time, to rise — not waking after the time has passed.
sbh1.3:42.169
dvitīyayotthānasaṁjñayā buddhānujñātāṁ siṁ haśa yyāṁ kalpayatyanyūnāmanadhikāṁ |
By the second [kind of] rising-perception, he arranges the lion-couch authorized by the Buddhas, neither subtracting nor adding [from it].
sbh1.3:42.170
tṛtīyayotthānasaṁjñayā chandaṁna sransa sraṁsa yati |
By the third [kind of] rising-perception, the desire [for wakefulness-yoga] he does not let slacken.
sbh1.3:42.171
sati smṛtisaṁpramoṣe, satyuttaratrottaratra samādānāya prayukto bhavati |
When forgetfulness arises, in each subsequent moment he applies himself to renewed undertaking.
sbh1.3:42.172
evamutthānasaṁjñāmeva manasi kurvan śayyāṁ kalpayati |
Thus, attending to the rising-perception, he lies down.
sbh1.3:42.173
sa rātryāḥ paścime yāme laghu laghveva prativibudhyāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayatīti |
He, in the last watch of the night, lightly very lightly waking, purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.174
paścimo yāma uccate |
‘The last watch’
sbh1.3:42.175
yo a pararātraḥ sātirekapraharaṁ raḥ |
is [also] said to be the late-night, somewhat-more-than-a-watch portion.
sbh1.3:42.176
sa cāyamālokasaṁjñī smṛtaḥ |
He, perceiving light, mindful,
sbh1.3:42.177
saṁprajāna n , utthānasaṁjñāmeva manasi kurvan, madhyamaṁ yāmaṁ sātirekaṁ praharaṁ middhamavakrāmayitvā krāmya , yasmiṁ smin samaye vyuttiṣṭhate |
fully aware, attending to the perception of rising, having let the middle watch — [also] somewhat-more-than-a-watch — pass in torpor, in [proper] time he rises up.
sbh1.3:42.178
tatra tasmiṁ smin samaye vyuttiṣṭhate |
[Thus] at that time he rises up,
sbh1.3:42.179
karmaṇyakāyo bhavati |
[being] workable-bodied.
sbh1.3:42.180
utthāya, nādhimātreṇa styānamiddhaparyavasthānenābhibhūtaḥ |
Having risen, not overcome by strong sloth-and-torpor obsession,
sbh1.3:42.181
yenāsyottiṣṭhato dhandhāyitatvaṁ vā syānmaṁdāyitacca tva mvā, ālasyakausīdyamvā |
by which there would be in his rising slowness, sluggishness, [or] laziness and indolence;
sbh1.3:42.182
asati vā punastasmindhandhāyitatve, mandāyitatve, ālasyakausīdye, laghu laghvevotthānaṁ bhavatyābhogamātrādeva no saṁgrāmaṁ vā kṛtvā āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaḥ pariśuddhiḥ pūrvavadvedi tavyāḥ vyā |
rather, in the absence of that slowness, sluggishness, laziness and indolence, his rising is lightly, very lightly, by mere effort, without [internal] battle. The purification from the obscuring dharmas is to be understood as above.
sbh1.3:42.183
ayaṁ tāvatpūrvarātrāparaṁrātraṁ jāgarikānuyogasya vistaravibhāgaḥ |
This much is the detailed division of application to wakefulness in the early and late watches of the night.
sbh1.3:42.184
samāsārthāḥ punaḥ katame |
What are the summary meanings?
sbh1.3:42.185
iha jāgarikāyogamanuyuktasya puruṣapudgalasya catvāri samyakkaraṇīyāni bhavanti |
Here, for the person applied to wakefulness-yoga, there are four right tasks.
The four samyak-prayoga (right tasks) of wakefulness-yoga — sustained wholesome practice while awake, sleeping at the right time, sleeping with undefiled mind, [and] waking at the right time — are paired (42.196–213) one-to-one with the four phrases of the canonical wakefulness-formula (purifies-by-walking-and-sitting / lies-down-foot-on-foot / mindful-and-fully-aware / wakes-in-the-last-watch). This pairing is a Yogācāra hermeneutic technique: a brief sūtra-formula is fully unfolded by mapping each phrase onto a named element of a corresponding doctrinal list.
sbh1.3:42.186
katamāni catvāri |
Which four?
sbh1.3:42.187
yāvajjāgrati gati tāvatkuśalapakṣaṁ na riṁcati |
[1] As long as he is awake, he does not let go of the wholesome side,
sbh1.3:42.188
sātatyenai ka pakṣyakuśaladharmabhāvanāyāṁ kālena ca śayyāṁ kalpayati |
[remaining] in continuous, one-sided cultivation of wholesome dharmas; [2] and at the [proper] time he lies down,
sbh1.3:42.189
nākālena |
not out of time;
sbh1.3:42.190
suptaścāsaṁkliṣṭacitto middhamavakrāmayati |
[3] and while sleeping, with mind undefiled he lets torpor descend,
sbh1.3:42.191
na saṁkliṣṭacittaḥ |
not with defiled mind;
sbh1.3:42.192
kālena ca prativibudhyate ti |
[4] and at the [proper] time he wakes,
sbh1.3:42.193
notthānakālamativartate |
[and] does not overstep the rising-time.
sbh1.3:42.194
itīmāni catvāri samyakkaraṇīyānyāramya bhagavatā śrāvakāṇāṁ jāgarikānuyogo deśitaḥ |
Taking up these four right tasks, the application to wakefulness has been taught by the Blessed One to the disciples.
sbh1.3:42.195
kathaṁ ca punardeśitaḥ |
How, again, is it taught?
sbh1.3:42.196
yattāvadāha |
When he first says:
sbh1.3:42.197
divā caṁkramaniṣadyābhyāmāvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayati |
‘by day, by walking and sitting, he purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas;
sbh1.3:42.198
evaṁ rātryāḥ prathamayāmami i tyanena tāvat prathamaṁ samyakkaraṇīyamākhyātaṁ |
thus in the first watch of the night,’ by this the first right task is declared,
sbh1.3:42.199
yaduta yāvajjāgrati garti tāvat kuśalapakṣaṁ na riṁcati |
namely: as long as he is awake, he does not let go of the wholesome side,
sbh1.3:42.200
sātatyenai ka pakṣyakuśaladharmabhāvanāyāṁ |
[remaining] in continuous, one-sided cultivation of wholesome dharmas.
sbh1.3:42.201
yasmāt punarāha |
When he then says:
sbh1.3:42.202
bahirvihārasya pādau prakṣālya, vihāraṁ praviśya, dakṣiṇena pārśvena śayyāṁ kalpayati |
‘washing the feet outside the dwelling, entering the dwelling, he lies down on the right side,
sbh1.3:42.203
pāde pādamādhāyetyanena dvitīyaṁ samyakkaraṇīyamākhyātaṁ |
foot placed on foot,’ by this the second right task is declared,
sbh1.3:42.204
yaduta kālena śayyāṁ kalpayati |
namely: at the [proper] time he lies down,
sbh1.3:42.205
nākālena |
not out of time.
sbh1.3:42.206
yatpunarāha |
When he then says:
sbh1.3:42.207
ālokasaṁjñī smṛtaḥ |
‘perceiving light, mindful,
sbh1.3:42.208
saṁprajāna n , utthānasaṁjñāmeva manasi kurvan, śayyāṁ kalpayatītyanena tṛtīyaṁ samyakkaraṇīyamākhyātaṁ |
fully aware, attending to the perception of rising, he lies down,’ by this the third right task is declared,
sbh1.3:42.209
yaduta asaṁkliṣṭacitto middhamavakrāmayati kramate |
namely: with mind undefiled he lets torpor descend,
sbh1.3:42.210
na saṁkliṣṭacitta iti |
not with defiled mind.
sbh1.3:42.211
yat punarāha rātryāḥ paścime yāme laghu laghveva prativibudhya āvaraṇīyebhyo dharmebhyaścittaṁ pariśodhayatītyanena caturthaṁ samyakkaraṇīyamākhyātaṁ |
When he then says: ‘in the last watch of the night, lightly very lightly waking, he purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas,’ by this the fourth right task is declared,
sbh1.3:42.212
yaduta kālena prativibudhyati |
namely: at the [proper] time he wakes,
sbh1.3:42.213
notthānakālamativartate |
[and] does not overstep the rising-time.
sbh1.3:42.214
iti tatra yaduktamālokasaṁjñī smṛtaḥ, saṁprajānan, vyutthānasaṁjñāmeva manasi kurva n śayyāṁ kalpayatīti |
Thus where it is said ‘perceiving light, mindful, fully aware, attending to the rising-perception, he lies down,’
sbh1.3:42.215
ato dvavābhyāṁ kāraṇābhyāṁ asaṁkliṣṭacitto middhamavakrāmayati |
from [these] two causes, with mind undefiled he lets torpor descend —
sbh1.3:42.216
yaduta smṛtyā, saṁprajanyena, dvābhyāṁ punaḥ kāraṇābhyāṁ kālena prativibudhyate |
namely, by mindfulness and full awareness; [and] from two further causes he wakes in time,
sbh1.3:42.217
no tu kālamativartate |
[and] does not overstep the time —
sbh1.3:42.218
yadutālokasaṁjñayā, utthānasaṁjñayā ca |
namely, by perception of light and perception of rising.
sbh1.3:42.219
kathaṁ punaḥ kṛtvā kuśalamālambanaṁ parigṛhya svapiti |
How, again, having grasped a wholesome object, does he [go to] sleep?
sbh1.3:42.220
saṁprajanyena, tasmāt, kuśalādālambanāccyavamānaṁ, saṁkliśyamānaṁ cittaṁ laghu laghveva samyakprajānāti |
By full awareness, [namely] that, when his mind has fallen away from the wholesome object [and] become defiled, he rightly knows [this] lightly very lightly.
sbh1.3:42.221
evamasyābhyāṁ dvābhyāṁ kāraṇābhyāmasaṁkliṣṭacittasya śayyā bhavati |
Thus by these two causes his sleep is undefiled.
sbh1.3:42.222
tatrālokasaṁjñayā, utthānasaṁjñayā ca |
And by perception of light and perception of rising,
sbh1.3:42.223
na gāḍhaṁ svapiti |
he does not sleep deeply,
sbh1.3:42.224
nāsya dūrānugataṁ tanmiddhaparyavasthānaṁ bhavati |
[and] his torpor-obsession does not become far-going.
sbh1.3:42.225
ityābhyāṁ dvābhyāṁ kāraṇābhyāṁ kālena prati vibudhyate ti |
Thus by these two causes he wakes in time,
sbh1.3:42.226
notthānakālamativartate |
[and] does not overstep the rising-time.
sbh1.3:42.227
ayaṁ jāgarikānuyogasya samāsārtho rthaḥ |
This, then, is the summary meaning of wakefulness-application:
sbh1.3:42.228
yaśca pūrvako vistaravibhāgaḥ, yaścāyaṁ samāsārthaḥ |
[the preceding] detailed analysis, and this summary meaning.
sbh1.3:42.229
iyamucyate pūrvarātrāpararātraṁ jāgarikānuyuktā |
This is called the wakefulness-application of the first and last watches of the night.
sbh1.3:43.1
saṁprajānadvihāritā katamā |
[Next:] dwelling with full awareness [saṁprajāna-vihāritā]. What is it?
saṁprajāna-vihāritā (dwelling-with-full-awareness) is introduced here as the next major topic (paragraphs 43–47): the practitioner's continuous knowing of bodily and mental activity across the full waking day. Sets up the breakdown into abhikrama / pratikrama and the larger nine-pair list of postures and activities that follows.
sbh1.3:43.2
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ abhikramapratikrame saṁprajānadvihārī bhavati |
When someone here dwells with full awareness in setting forth and returning,
sbh1.3:43.3
ālokitavyavalokite, saṁmiṁjita sāmmiñjita prasārite, saṁghāṭīcīvarapātradhāraṇe |
in looking ahead and looking around, in bending and stretching [the limbs], in wearing the saṅghāṭi-robe and [carrying the] bowl,
sbh1.3:43.4
aśite, pīte, khādite, svādite, niṣaṇṇe, śayite, jāgṛte, pra la pite, tūṣṇīṁbhāve, nidrāklamavinodane saṁprajānadvihārī bhavati |
in eating, drinking, chewing, tasting, in sitting, lying, [and] waking, in speaking, in silence, in the dispelling of sleep-fatigue — he dwells with full awareness.
sbh1.3:43.5
tatra katamo a bhikramaḥ |
What is setting forth?
sbh1.3:43.6
pratikramaḥ katamaḥ |
What is returning?
sbh1.3:43.7
abhikramapratikrame samprajānadvihāritā katamā |
What is dwelling with full awareness in setting forth and returning?
sbh1.3:43.8
tatrābhikramaḥ |
Setting forth:
sbh1.3:43.9
yathāpīhaikatyo grāmamvā upakrāmati |
when someone here approaches a village
sbh1.3:43.10
grāmāntaramvā, kulamvā, kulāntaramvā, vihāramvā , vihārāntaramvā |
or another village, a family or another family, a dwelling or another dwelling.
sbh1.3:43.11
tatra pratikramaḥ |
Returning:
sbh1.3:43.12
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ grāmāntarāmvā grāmādvā pratinivarttate |
when someone here returns from another village or from a village,
sbh1.3:43.13
grāmāntarādvā, kulādvā, kulāntarādvā, vihārādvā, vihārāntarādvā |
from another village, from a family, from another family, from a dwelling, from another dwelling.
sbh1.3:43.14
tatra saṁprajānadvihāritā yāḥ |
[Now,] dwelling with full awareness [in setting forth and returning]:
sbh1.3:44.1
abhikrama mā ṇe ṇo abhikramāmīti samyageva prajānāti |
Setting forth, he rightly knows ‘I am setting forth’;
sbh1.3:44.2
atra mayā abhikramitavyaṁ |
‘here I should set forth,
sbh1.3:44.3
atra mayā punarnābhikramitavyamiti |
here I should not set forth’ —
sbh1.3:44.4
samyageva prajānati |
he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:44.5
ayamvā me abhisaṁkramaṇakālaḥ ayaṁ nābhisaṁkramaṇakāla iti |
‘this is for me a time of approach, this is not a time of approach’ —
sbh1.3:44.6
samyageva prajānāti |
he rightly knows.
sbh1.3:44.7
idamasyocyate saṁprajanyaṁ |
This is called for him full awareness.
sbh1.3:44.8
sa cettena saṁprajanyena samanvāgataḥ |
He, endowed with that full awareness,
sbh1.3:44.9
abhikramamāṇaḥ prajānātyabhikramāmīti |
setting forth, knows ‘I am setting forth’;
sbh1.3:44.10
yatra cānenābhikramitavyaṁ bhavati |
where he should set forth,
sbh1.3:44.11
tatra cābhikramati |
there he sets forth,
sbh1.3:44.12
kālena cābhikramati nākālena |
and at the [proper] time he sets forth, not out of time,
sbh1.3:44.13
yathā cābhikramitavyaṁ |
[and] as he should set forth,
sbh1.3:44.14
yadrūpayā ca ya rya yā ācāreṇākalpena īryāpathena tathā abhikramatīyamasyocyate saṁprajānādvihāritā yadutābhikramapratikramaḥ mayoḥ |
with such conduct, with such deportment, he sets forth. This is called for him dwelling with full awareness, namely in setting forth and returning.
sbh1.3:44.15
tatra katamadālokitaṁ, katamad vyavalokitaṁ |
What is looking ahead, what is looking around?
sbh1.3:44.16
katamā ālokitavyavalokita saṁprajānādvihāritaḥ tā |
What is dwelling with full awareness in looking ahead and looking around?
sbh1.3:44.17
tasyāsya pūrvaparikī rttiteṣu dhamaṣu abhikramataḥ, pratikramataśca yadabuddhipūrvakamacchandapūrvakaṁ |
For him, [while engaged] in the previously proclaimed dharmas of setting forth and returning, that seeing of forms with the eye which, without prior thought, without prior desire,
sbh1.3:44.18
a ntare ṇa cakṣuṣā rūpadarśanamidamucyate ālokitaṁ |
occurs in between — this is called looking ahead.
sbh1.3:44.19
yatpunarupasaṁkrāntasya buddhipūrvakaṁ prayatnapūrvakaṁ, kṛtyapūrvakaṁ cakṣuṣā rūpadarśanaṁ, tadyathā rājñā jñāṁ rājyā jā mātrāṇāṁ, naigamānāṁ, jānapadānāmvā, brāhmaṇānāmvā, dhanināṁ, śreṣṭhināṁ, sārthavāhānāṁ, tadanyeṣāmbāhyakānāṁlayanānāṁ, mārutānāmavarakāṇāṁ, pra prā sādānāṁ, harmyatalānāmiti |
When, having approached [a sight], with prior thought, with prior effort, with prior intent, [there is] the seeing of forms with the eye — namely of kings, of kings’ ministers, townsmen, country-folk, brāhmaṇas, the wealthy, chiefs, caravan-leaders; or of other recluse-places, of huts, of pavilions, of stalls, of palaces, of mansion-stories;
sbh1.3:44.20
yadvā punaranyeṣāṁ lokacitrāṇāṁ darśanamidamucyate |
or again, the seeing of other worldly spectacles — this is called
sbh1.3:44.21
vyavalokitaṁ |
looking around.
sbh1.3:44.22
yatpunarālokitaṁ ca vyavalokitaṁ ca svalakṣaṇataḥ samyageva prajānāti |
When he rightly knows looking-ahead and looking-around by their specific characteristics,
Svalakṣaṇa in the full-awareness section is rendered as 'specific characteristic,' matching the project's broader technical usage in the vipaśyanā and skill-object chapters. The earlier 'own-marks' was literal but inconsistent and less readable.
sbh1.3:44.23
yathā āloki kayi tavyaṁ, yathā vyavalokayitavyaṁ tadapisamyageva prajānāti |
[and] how one should look ahead, how one should look around — this too he rightly knows.
sbh1.3:44.24
idamasyocyate saṁprajanyaṁ |
This is called for him full awareness.
sbh1.3:44.25
sa tena saṁprajanyena samanvāgataḥ |
He, endowed with that full awareness,
sbh1.3:44.26
sacedavalokayamāno jānātyavalokayāmīti |
if looking around, knows ‘I am looking around’;
sbh1.3:44.27
yaccāvalokayitavyaṁ vyavalokayitavyaṁ tadālokayati vyavalokayati |
what should be looked at ahead or around, that he looks at;
sbh1.3:44.28
yadā ālokayitavyaṁ, vyavalokayitavyaṁ |
when one should look ahead or around,
sbh1.3:44.29
tadā ālokayati, vyavalokayati |
then he looks ahead, [then he] looks around;
sbh1.3:44.30
yathā ālokayitavyaṁ vyavalokayitavyaṁ tathā ālokayati, vyavalokayati |
as one should look ahead or around, so he looks ahead, [so he] looks around.
sbh1.3:44.31
iyamasyocyate |
This is called for him
sbh1.3:44.32
saṁprajānadvihāritā |
dwelling with full awareness,
sbh1.3:44.33
yadutālokitavyavalokite |
namely in looking ahead and looking around.
sbh1.3:44.34
tatra katamat saṁmiji sammiñji taṁ, katamat prasāritaṁ, katamā saṁmiji sammiñji taprasārite saṁprajānadvihāritā |
What is bending, what is stretching, what is dwelling with full awareness in bending and stretching?
sbh1.3:44.35
sa tathā ālokayamāno, vyavalokayamānaśca |
He, thus looking ahead and looking around,
sbh1.3:44.36
abhikramapūrvakaṁ, pratikramapūrvakañca, yatpādau vā sammiṁjayati, prasārayati |
preceded by setting forth and preceded by returning, whether he bends his feet or stretches [them],
sbh1.3:44.37
bāhū vā saṁmi miṁ jayati |
or bends his arms,
sbh1.3:44.38
prasārayati |
stretches [them],
sbh1.3:44.39
hastau vā saṁmi miṁ jayati, prasārayati |
or bends his hands, stretches [them] —
sbh1.3:44.40
ityanyatamānyatamamvā aṁgapratyaṁgaṁ sammiñjiyati |
thus whichever limb or sub-limb he bends
sbh1.3:44.41
prasārayatīdamucyate |
or stretches — this is called
sbh1.3:44.42
saṁmi miṁ jita prasāritaṁ |
bending and stretching.
sbh1.3:44.43
sacet saṁmi miṁ jitaprasāritaṁ svalakṣaṇataḥ prajānāti saṁmi miṁ jitavyaṁ |
If he knows bending-and-stretching by their specific characteristics, [and] what should be bent,
sbh1.3:44.44
prasāritavyañca |
what should be stretched,
sbh1.3:44.45
samyageva prajānāti |
he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:44.46
yādi ca saṁmi miṁ ji jayi tavyaṁ, yadā prasāri rayi tavyaṁ tadapi samyageva prajānāti |
when one should bend, when one should stretch — that too he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:44.47
yathā ca saṁmi miṁ ji jayi tavyaṁ, yathā prasāri rayi tavyaṁ, tadapi samyageva prajānāti, idamasyocyate saṁprajanyaṁ |
as one should bend, as one should stretch — that too he rightly knows. This is called for him full awareness.
sbh1.3:44.48
sa tena saṁprajanyena samanvāgataḥ |
He, endowed with that full awareness,
sbh1.3:44.49
sa cet saṁmija sammiñja yamānaḥ yan , prasārayamāṇaḥ yan |
[when] bending [or] stretching,
sbh1.3:44.50
jānāti |
knows:
sbh1.3:44.51
saṁmi miṁ jayāmi |
‘I am bending,
sbh1.3:44.52
prasārayāmīti |
I am stretching’;
sbh1.3:44.53

what should be bent or stretched, that he bends [or] stretches;
sbh1.3:44.54

when one should bend [or] stretch, then he bends [or] stretches. This is called for him
sbh1.3:44.55
saṁprajānadvihāritā yaduta saṁmiṁji sammiñji ta prasārite |
dwelling with full awareness, namely in bending and stretching.
sbh1.3:45.1
tatra katamat saṁghāṭīdhāraṇaṁ |
What is wearing the saṅghāṭi?
Dhāraṇa covers both wearing robes and carrying the bowl. The English therefore uses 'wearing/carrying' rather than the mechanical 'bearing,' which produced unreadable phrases such as 'bowl-bearing should be borne' while adding no philological precision.
sbh1.3:45.2
katamaccīvaradhāraṇaṁ, katamat, pātradhāraṇaṁ, katamā mat sāṁ saṁ ghāṭīcīvarapātradhāraṇaṁ ṇe |
What is wearing robes? What is carrying the bowl? What is dwelling with full awareness in wearing the saṅghāṭi and robes, and carrying the bowl?
sbh1.3:45.3
saṁprajānadvihāritā |
[Now,] dwelling with full awareness:
sbh1.3:45.4
yattāvadasya jyeṣṭhaṁ cīvaraṁ ṣaṣṭhikhannamvā khaṇḍaṁ vā navatikhannamvā khaṇḍaṁ vā , dviguṇasīvitamvā, ekaguṇasīvitamvā iyamucyate sāṁghāṭī saṁghāṭī |
that uppermost robe of his — of sixty patches or of ninety patches, double-stitched or single-stitched — this is called saṅghāṭi;
sbh1.3:45.5
tasya prāvaraṇaṁ paribhogaḥ |
using it as a covering is its use,
sbh1.3:45.6
samyageva pariharaṇaṁ dhāraṇamityucyate |
[and] properly wearing it is called wearing.
sbh1.3:45.7
yatpunarasya madhyamvā, kanīyo vā, ādhiṣṭhānikamvā cī varaṁ atirekacīvaraṁ vā |
And that middle [robe], the smaller [robe], the determined robe, [or] the extra robe —
Ādhiṣṭhānika cīvara is rendered as 'determined robe,' i.e. a robe formally determined for use, not a 'base-cloth.' The adjacent atireka-cīvara vikalpanārha is an extra robe fit for assignment/designation; the translation keeps the Vinaya register without inventing a misleading English category.
sbh1.3:45.8
ādhiṣṭhānikaṁ vā |
or the determined [robe],
sbh1.3:45.9
cīvaraṁ atirekacīvaramvā vikalpanārhaṁ vikalpayati taccīvaramityucyate |
or the extra robe fit for assignment — these are called robes;
sbh1.3:45.10
tasya prāvaraṇaṁ paribhogaḥ |
using them as coverings is their use,
sbh1.3:45.11
samyageva pariharaṇaṁ dhāraṇamityucyate |
[and] properly wearing them is called wearing.
sbh1.3:45.12
yatpunarasyādhiṣṭhānāni kamā rmā yasamvā, mṛnmayamvā bhaikṣabhojanamidamucyate |
And that determined vessel, whether iron or earthenware, for alms-food — this is called
sbh1.3:45.13
pātraṁ, tasya paribhogaḥ |
the bowl; carrying it is its use,
sbh1.3:45.14
samyageva pariharaṇaṁ dhāraṇamityucyate |
[and] properly carrying it is called carrying.
sbh1.3:45.15
sa cetpunarayaṁ tāṁ sāṁ saṁ ghāṭīṁ, cīvaraṁ, pātraṁ, dhāraṇamvā svalakṣaṇataḥ samyageva prajānāti |
If, [furthermore,] he rightly knows this saṅghāṭi, robe, [and] bowl, [along with their] wearing or carrying, by their specific characteristics;
sbh1.3:45.16
yacca sāṁ saṁ ghāṭīcīvarapātradhāraṇaṁ |
[and] what wearing or carrying of saṅghāṭi, robe, [or] bowl
sbh1.3:45.17
kalpikaṁ, akalpikaṁ vā, tadapi samyageva prajānati |
is allowable or not allowable, that too he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:45.18
yadā ca sāṁ saṁ ghāṭīcīvarapātradhāraṇaṁ dhārayitavyaṁ karttavyaṁ |
when the saṅghāṭi, robe, [or] bowl should be worn or carried,
sbh1.3:45.19
tadā samyageva prajānāti |
[that] he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:45.20
yathā ca dhārayitavyaṁ, tadapi samyageva prajānāti |
as it should be worn or carried, that too he rightly knows.
sbh1.3:45.21
idamasyocyate |
This is called for him
sbh1.3:45.22
saṁprajanyaṁ |
full awareness.
sbh1.3:45.23
sa tena saṁprajanyena samanvāgataḥ |
He, endowed with that full awareness,
sbh1.3:45.24
sacet sāṁ saṁ ghāṭī ṭīṁ , cīvaraṁ, pātraṁ dhārayamāṇo jānāti dhārayāmīti |
if he wears the saṅghāṭi or robe, or carries the bowl, he knows, ‘I am wearing or carrying [it]’;
sbh1.3:45.25
yacca dhārayitavyaṁ taddhārayati |
what should be worn or carried, he wears or carries;
sbh1.3:45.26
yadā ca dhārayitavyaṁ tadā dhārayati |
when it should be worn or carried, then he wears or carries [it];
sbh1.3:45.27
yathā ca dhārayitavyaṁ tathā dhārayatīyamasyocyate saṁprajānadvihāritā |
as it should be worn or carried, so he wears or carries [it]. This is called for him dwelling with full awareness,
sbh1.3:45.28
yaduta sāṁ saṁ ghāṭīcīvarapātradhāraṇe |
namely in wearing the saṅghāṭi and robe, and carrying the bowl.
sbh1.3:45.29
tatra katamadaśitaṁ |
What is eaten?
sbh1.3:45.30
katamatpītaṁ |
What is drunk?
sbh1.3:45.31
katamat khāditaṁ |
What is chewed?
sbh1.3:45.32
katamatsvāditaṁ |
What is tasted?
sbh1.3:45.33
katamā aśitapītakhāditāsvādite ṣu saṁprajānadvihāritā |
What is dwelling with full awareness in eating, drinking, chewing, [and] tasting?
sbh1.3:45.34
yaḥ kaścit piṇḍapātaparibhogaḥ sarvaṁ tadaśitamityucyate |
Whatever use of alms-food [there is] — all that is called ‘eaten.’
The food taxonomy distinguishes the broad category aśita ('eaten' / alms-food use), then divides it into khādita ('chewed,' eaten but not drunk) and svādita ('tasted,' items treated as eaten and also drunk). This is a Vinaya-style use-classification, not a modern nutrition taxonomy; the English avoids the compressed and misleading 'eaten-but-not-drunk' compound.
sbh1.3:45.35
tasya punardvidhā bhedaḥ khāditaṁ; svāditaṁ ca |
Its further division is twofold: chewed and tasted.
sbh1.3:45.36
tatra khāditaṁ |
Chewed:
sbh1.3:45.37
manthā vā, a pūpā vā, odanakulmāṣamveti yadvā punaranyatamābhisaṁskārikamannaṁ vikṛtaṁ bhojyaṁ prāṇasaṁdhāraṇamidamucyate khāditamaśitamapītaṁ |
barley-meal or cakes, rice or porridge, or some other prepared food — variously cooked, edible, life-sustaining — this is called chewed, as something eaten and not drunk.
sbh1.3:45.38
svāditaṁ katamat |
What is tasted?
sbh1.3:45.39
tadyathā kṣīraṁ, dadhi, navanītaṁ, sarpistailaṁ, madhu, śo phā ṇitaṁ, mānsaṁ māṁsaṁ , matsyā, vallūrā, lavaṇaṁ, vanaphalamvā, bhakṣa kṣya prakāraṁ vā idamucyate svāditamaśitamapi pī taṁ |
Namely milk, curd, butter, ghee, oil, honey, syrup, meat, fish, dried meat, salt, forest-fruit, or [other] kinds of edibles — this is called tasted, as something eaten and also drunk.
sbh1.3:46.1
yatpunaḥ pīyate khaṇḍarasaṁ so vā, śarkarārasamvā so vā , kāṁcikamvā, dadhimaṇḍamvā |
What is drunk: candied juice, sugary juice,
sbh1.3:46.2
śuktamvā, takramvā, antataḥ pānīyamapi |
rice-water, curd-whey, sour gruel, buttermilk, or even water —
sbh1.3:46.3
idamucyate pītaṁ |
this is called drunk.
sbh1.3:46.4
sa cedaśitapītakhāditāsvāditaṁ svalakṣaṇataḥ samyageva prajānāti |
If he rightly knows what is eaten, drunk, chewed, [and] tasted by their specific characteristics;
sbh1.3:46.5
yaccāśitavyaṁ, pātavyaṁ, khāditavyaṁ, svādi dayi tavyaṁ tadapi samyageva prajānāti |
[and] what should be eaten, drunk, chewed, [or] tasted — that too he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:46.6
yadā cāśitavyaṁ, pātavyaṁ khāditavyaṁ, svādi dayi tavyaṁ tadeva tadapi samyageva prajānāti |
when it should be eaten, drunk, chewed, [or] tasted — that too he rightly knows;
sbh1.3:46.7
yathā cāśitavyaṁ, pātavyaṁ, khāditavyaṁ |
as it should be eaten, drunk, chewed,
sbh1.3:46.8
svādi dayi tavyaṁ |
[or] tasted —
sbh1.3:46.9
tadapi samyageva prajānāti |
that too he rightly knows.
sbh1.3:46.10
idamasyocyate saṁprajanyaṁ |
This is called for him full awareness.
sbh1.3:46.11
sa tena saṁprajanyena samanvāgataḥ aśamānaḥ aśan , pibamānaḥ piban , khādamānaḥ khādan , svādayamānaḥ svādayan sa cejjānāti |
He, endowed with that full awareness — when eating, drinking, chewing, [or] tasting — knows,
sbh1.3:46.12
aśnāmi, pibāmi, khādāmīti |
‘I am eating, drinking, chewing, [or tasting]’ —
sbh1.3:46.13
yaccāśitavyaṁ, pātavyaṁ, khāditavyaṁ, tadaśnātiyāvatsvādayati |
what should be eaten, drunk, [or] chewed, that he eats, [and so on] up through tasting;
sbh1.3:46.14
yadā cāśitavyaṁ yāva tsvādayitavyaṁ |
when it should be eaten, [and so on] up through tasted —
sbh1.3:46.15
tadā aśnāti, svādayati |
then he eats, [up through] tastes;
sbh1.3:46.16
yathā cāśitavyaṁyāvatsvādayitavyaṁ tathā a śnāti |
as it should be eaten, [and so on] up through tasted — so he eats,
sbh1.3:46.17
yāvatsvādayitavyami yāvatsvādayatī yamasyocyate yāvat saṁprajānadvihāritā |
[and so on] up through tastes. This is called for him, [and so on] up through, dwelling with full awareness,
sbh1.3:46.18
yadutāśitapītakhāditāsvādite ṣu |
namely in eating, drinking, chewing, [and] tasting.
sbh1.3:47.1

What is dwelling with full awareness in going, standing, sitting, lying down, being awake, speaking, being silent, [and] in the dispelling of sleep-fatigue?
sbh1.3:47.7
yathāpīhaikatyaścaṁkrame caṁkramyate mate , sahadharmikāṇāṁ copasaṁkrāmati |

sbh1.3:47.10

sbh1.3:47.11

preceptors, instructors, gurus, [or] persons occupying the place of guru — this is called standing.
sbh1.3:47.12
yathāpīhaikatyo maṁce, vā, pīṭhe vā, tṛṇasaṁstaraṇe vā, sanniviśati vā, sanniṣīdati vā |
When someone here sits or settles on a couch, stool, [or] grass-mat,
sbh1.3:47.13
paryaṅkamābhujya, ṛjuṁ kāyaṁ praṇidhāya, pratimukhāṁ khīṁ smṛtimupasthāpyedamucyate |
folding the legs cross-wise, having set the body upright, having established mindfulness to the fore — this is called
The sitting definition uses the same pratimukhī smṛti formula as sbh3.0:1.170-175. Rendered consistently as 'mindfulness to the fore,' not the earlier 'facing forward' or the mechanical 'fronting-before.'
sbh1.3:47.14
niṣaṇṇaṁ |
sitting.
sbh1.3:47.15

When someone here, washing the feet outside the dwelling, entering the dwelling, lies down on the right side in lion-fashion,
sbh1.3:47.16
pāde pādamādhāya, mañce vā, pīṭhe vā, tṛṇasaṁstarake ṇe vā, araṇye, vṛkṣamūle vā, śūnyāgāre vā |

sbh1.3:47.18

When someone here by day, by walking and sitting, purifies the mind of obscuring dharmas, [and] likewise in the first watch and the last watch of the night —
sbh1.3:47.19

sbh1.3:47.20

[as elaborated] above;

sbh1.3:47.25

sbh1.3:47.26
kālena kālamālapati |
[and] from time to time greets
sbh1.3:47.27

sbh1.3:47.28

sbh1.3:47.29
yathāpīhaikatyo yathāśrutānāṁ, yathā parya vā taptānāndharmāṇāmmanasā parijitānāṁ, ekākī rahogato a rthaṁ ci ntaya ti, tulayatyupaparīkṣate |
When someone here — of the dharmas heard, mastered, [and] mentally examined — alone, secluded, reflects on the meaning, weighs, examines [them];
sbh1.3:47.30

or, withdrawn, establishes the mind inwardly,

concentrates it;
sbh1.3:47.36

or applies himself in yoga to insight — this is called being silent.
sbh1.3:47.37
yathāpīhaikatyaḥ grīṣmasamaye pratyupasthite, uttaptagrīṣmaparidāhe kāle vartamāne, uṣṇana vā bādhyate |
When someone here, when summer-time has come, in the burning heat of the hottest summer, is afflicted by heat,
sbh1.3:47.38

or becomes weary,

for one fatigued there arises

this is called sleep-fatigue.

And he, regarding going and so on up through the dispelling of sleep-fatigue,

rightly knows each by its specific characteristic;

sbh1.3:48.1
saṁprajānadvihāritāyā ḥ katamā ānupūrvo, katamā ca stutibhāvanā, yathāpīhaikatyo yaṁ yameva grāmaṁ vā nigamamvopa ni śritya viharati |
What is the sequence and cultivation of dwelling with full awareness? When someone here, depending on whichever village or market-town he dwells in,
Although the chapter heading at 43.1 introduces saṁprajāna-vihāritā, paragraphs 48–55 actually open the next saṁbhāra — kalyāṇa-mitratā (association with good friends) — by detailing the conduct on the alms-round and in solitary practice (47–50) and then enumerating the eight qualities (51–52) and four roles (53) of the good friend himself, closing with the summary at 54.1. The two topics are bridged because what one learns through saṁprajāna in daily activity is precisely the conduct modeled by the kalyāṇa-mitra.
sbh1.3:48.2
tasyaivaṁ bhavati |
it occurs to him:
sbh1.3:48.3
mayā khalvayaṁ grāmo vā, nigamo vā piṇḍāyopa saṁ kramitavyaḥ |
‘This village or market-town is to be approached by me for alms.
sbh1.3:48.4
piṇḍāya caritvā, punareva vihāraṁ prati niṣkramitavyaṁ |
Having gone for alms, I should return again to the dwelling.
sbh1.3:48.5
santi |
There are
sbh1.3:48.6
punaratra kulāni grāme vā, nagare vā, yāni mayā nopasaṁkramitavyāni |
in this village or town families that are not to be approached by me.
sbh1.3:48.7
tāni punaḥ katamāni |
And what are they?
sbh1.3:48.8
tadyathā |
Namely:
sbh1.3:48.9
ghoṣaṁ ṣaḥ , pānāgāraṁ, veśyaṁ, rājakulaṁ, caṇḍālakaṭhinamiti |
a cowherd settlement, a tavern, a prostitute’s house, the royal household, a caṇḍāla quarter,
Ghoṣa is a cowherd settlement or encampment, not a brothel; the prostitute/prostitute's house is the following item, veśya. The list names unsuitable places for alms-round approach: cowherd settlement, tavern, prostitute's house, royal household, and caṇḍāla quarter.
sbh1.3:48.10
yāni vā punaḥ kulānyekāntena pratihatānyapratyudāvarttāni |
or families wholly hostile [or] not [worth] returning to.
sbh1.3:48.11
santi ca punaḥ kulāni yāni mayopasaṁkramitavyāni |
And there are families to be approached by me —
sbh1.3:48.12
tadyathā kṣatriyamahāsālakulāni vā, brāhmaṇamahāsālakulāni vā, naigamakulāni vā, jānapadakulāni vā, dhanikulāni vā, śreṣṭhikulāni vā, sārthavāhakulāni vā mayopasaṁkramitavyāni, tāni nātisāyamupasaṁkramitavyāni |
namely, kṣatriya-mahāsāla families, brāhmaṇa-mahāsāla families, market-town families, country families, wealthy families, chief families, [and] caravan-leader families — [these] are to be approached, but not too late in the evening,
sbh1.3:48.13
tānivikālaṁ, na ca kāryavyagreṣu dāyakadānapatiṣu, na krīḍāratimaṇḍanayogamanuyukteṣu, na grāmyadharmāya pravṛtteṣu, na kupiteṣu |
not at the wrong time, not when the donor-patrons are busy with work, not when [they are] engaged in play or pleasure or adornment, not when devoted to village-affairs, not when angry.
sbh1.3:48.14
tathā copasaṁkramitavyāni yathopasaṁkramannahanna bhrāntena hastinā sārdhaṁ samāgaccheyaṁ, na bhrāntena sthena, punaḥ caṇḍenāśvena, na caṇḍayā gavā, na caṇḍena kukkureṇa, na gahanaṁ, na kaṇṭakāvāṭaṁ vā mardeyaṁ |
And I should approach in such a way that, approaching, I would not encounter a raging elephant, a raging horse, a fierce horse, a fierce cow, [or] a fierce dog; would not tread on dense undergrowth or thorny barricades,
sbh1.3:48.15
na śvabhre, palvale, prapāte prapateyaṁ |
would not fall into a pit, a muddy pool, a precipice,
sbh1.3:48.16
na syandikāyāṁ, na gūthakaṭhillacandropamaśca kulānyupasaṁkrameyaṁ |
not into refuse-water, not into cesspits. Like the moon I should approach the families,
sbh1.3:48.17
hrīmānapragalbhaḥ vyavakṛṣya kāyaṁ, vyavakṛṣya cittaṁ, na lābhakāmo na satkārakāmaḥ |
possessing moral shame, non-brazen, withdrawn in body, withdrawn in mind, not desiring gain, not desiring honor.
sbh1.3:48.18
yathā svena lābhena sucittaḥ, sumanāstathā parasyāvipralābhena sucittaḥ, sumanāḥ ananyotkarṣī, aparapaṁsakaḥ |
As I am clear-minded [and] glad at my own gain, so I should be clear-minded [and] glad at another’s gain — non-self-aggrandizing, non-other-deprecating,
sbh1.3:48.19
anukampācitto, dayācittaḥ |
of compassionate mind, of pitying mind.
sbh1.3:48.20
evaṁ ca punarupasaṁkramitavyāni |
And thus again I should approach.
sbh1.3:48.21
tatkuta etallabhyaṁ pravrajitena parakuleṣu yaddadatu me pare, mā vā dadatu, yāvattvaritaṁ mā dhandhamiti vistareṇa |
How is this to be obtained by one gone forth, [going] in others’ families — that others should give me, or not give, [and so on] up through swiftly, not slowly,’ [as elaborated] above.
sbh1.3:48.22
upasaṁkramya ca me mayā pratigrahe mātrā karaṇīyā |
‘Having approached, in receiving [I] should observe due measure,
sbh1.3:48.23
na ca lābhahetoḥ kuhanā karaṇīyā |
and not for the sake of gain should I make hypocrisy;
sbh1.3:48.24
lapanā, naimittikatā, naiṣpeṣikatā, lābhena lābhaniścikīrṣatā karaṇīyā ḥ |
talkative chatter, insinuation, extortion, gain-seeking-by-gain should not be made;
sbh1.3:48.25
sa ca lābhaḥ araktena paribhoktavya ḥ , asaktenāgṛddhenāgrathitenāmūḍhitena, anadhyavasitenādhyavasāyamāpannena |
and that gain should be enjoyed unattached,
sbh1.3:48.26
yāni ca rūpāṇi tatropasaṁkramatā upasaṁkrāntena vā draṣṭavyāni bhavanti |
unclinging, ungreedy, unbinding, unbeguiled, unfixated, [and] not fallen into fixation.
sbh1.3:48.27
tānyekatyāni draṣṭavyāni |
And [as for] those forms which in approaching or having approached are [normally] to be seen,
sbh1.3:48.28
tatra yāni na draṣṭavyāni teṣu utkṣiptacakṣuṣā bhavitavyaṁ |
only some of those are to be looked at;
sbh1.3:48.29
susaṁhṛtendriyeṇa, yāni punardraṣṭavyāni teṣu sūpasthitāṁ smṛtimupasthāpya |
toward those not [proper] to be seen, one should be with eyes [merely] raised,
sbh1.3:48.30
kathaṁ rūpāṇi punā rūpāṇi nāvalokayitavyāni |
with well-collected senses; toward those that are [proper] to be seen, [one should be] with well-established mindfulness.
sbh1.3:48.31
tadyathā na narttako, na hāsako, na lāsaka iti |
What kind of forms are not to be looked at?
sbh1.3:48.32
yadvā punaranyaccāvaraṇajātaṁ nṛtte vā, gīte vā, vādite vā pravṛttaṁ |
Namely: a dancer, a comedian, a jester,
sbh1.3:48.33
tathā mātṛgrāmo viśeṣeṇa punaḥ śiśurudāravarṇo ra ñjanī ya iti yāni ca punā rūpāṇi dṛṣṭāni brahmacaryopaghātibhiḥ brahmacaryāntarāyāya, pāpakānāṁ cākuśalānāmvitarkāṇāṁ samudācārāya samvartteran |
or another obscuring [performer] engaged in dance, song, music;
sbh1.3:48.34
tadrūpāṇi kathaṁ rūpāṇi punā rūpāṇi draṣṭavyāni |
likewise especially a young, fine-complexioned, lust-arousing woman — and [in general] those forms whose seeing would harm the holy life, would be an obstacle to the holy life, would conduce to the manifestation of evil, unwholesome thoughts.
sbh1.3:48.35
tadyathā jīrṇamvā, vṛddhamvā, mahallakamvā, khuru khuru praśvāsakāyaṁ, purataḥ prābho bhā rakāyaṁ daṇḍabhavaṣṭabhya, pravepamānakāyena ābādhikamvā, duḥkhitambāḍhaglānaṁ, ādhmātapādamādhmātahastamādhmātodaramādhmātamukhaṁ, pāṇḍukavivarṇaṁ, dadrūlamvā, kacchū ṇḍū lamvā, kuṣṭhitamvā, duḥkhitahatagātraṁ |
What kind of forms are to be seen?
sbh1.3:48.36
pakvagātramupahatendriyaṁ |
Namely: the aged, old, decrepit, of wheezing breath, of forward-bowed body, leaning on a staff, of trembling body; or one afflicted, suffering, gravely ill, with swollen feet, swollen hands, swollen belly, swollen face, pale, eczema-ridden, scabies-ridden, leprous, suffering, of struck-down limbs;
sbh1.3:48.37
mṛtamvā kālagataṁ, ekāhamṛtamvā, dvāhamṛtamvā, saptāhamṛtamvā |
or [one] dead, time-gone, one-day dead, two-days dead, seven-days dead,
sbh1.3:48.38
kākaiḥ kuraraiḥ khādyamānaṁ, gṛddhaiḥ, śvabhiḥ, śrṛgālairvividhairvā tiryagjātigataiḥ prāṇibhirbhakṣyamāṇamarhniyamāṇamvā, maṁce āropyopari vitānena prasāritena, puna ra ḥ pṛṣṭhataśca mahājanakāyena rodamānena, krandamānena, tasmā syā vakīrṇapramuktakeśena, tathā śokajātaṁ, duḥkhajātaṁ |
devoured by crows, ospreys, vultures, dogs, [or] jackals, eaten by various animal-creatures; or [a corpse being] carried — placed on a bier with canopy spread above, with a great crowd behind, weeping, lamenting, with hair scattered and loosed, in sorrow, in suffering,
sbh1.3:48.39
paridevajātaṁ |
in lamentation,
sbh1.3:48.40
daurmanasyajātaṁ, upāyāsajātaṁ yanmayā draṣṭavyamityevaṁ rūpāṇi cānyāni caivaṁbhāgīyāni rūpāṇi draṣṭavyāni |
in dejection, in distress — these are what should be seen; and other such forms are to be seen,
sbh1.3:48.41
yāni brahmacaryānugrahāya, kuśalānāṁ ca vitarkāṇāṁ samudācārāya ṁ samvarttante |
[namely] those that conduce to the support of the holy life and to the manifestation of wholesome thoughts.
sbh1.3:48.42
na kāyapracālakamupasaṁkrāmitavyaṁ, na bāhupracālakaṁ, na śīrṣapracālakaṁ, noccagmikayā na hastāvalagnikayā, na soḍḍhakkikayā, nānanujñātena |
One should not approach with shaking body, shaking arms, [or] shaking head; not with raised voice; not with the hand hanging loosely, not with shoulder-shaking.
sbh1.3:48.43
nā ā sane niṣattavyaṁ |
One should not sit on a seat without permission,
sbh1.3:48.44
nā a pratyavekṣyāsanaṁ, na sarvakāyaṁ samavadhāya, na pāde pādamādhāya, na sakthi sakthinā, nābhisaṁkṣipya pādau, nābhivikṣipya pādau, nodguṇṭhikayā kṛ vṛ tena, nocca ddha stikayā, na vitastikayā, na paryastikayā, parimaṇḍalaṁ cīvaraṁ prāvṛtya, nātyutkṛṣṭaṁ, nātya pakṛ ṣṭaṁ, na hamdhī sti śuṇḍakaṁ, na tālavṛntakaṁ, na nāgaphalakaṁ |
without examining the seat; not laying the whole body upon it, not [with] foot on foot, not [with] thigh on thigh, not [with] feet contracted, not [with] feet spread, not with [the body] covered up, not [in a] high-handed [manner], not with fingers spread, not cross-legged — having put on the robe round about, not too high, not too low, not on the side of the chin, not as a palmyra-fan, not as a snake-board.
sbh1.3:48.45
na kulmāṣapiṇḍikaṁ prāvaritavyaṁ, nānāgate khādanīye pātramupanāmayitavyaṁ, na ca khādanīyabhojanīyasyopari kāra yitavyaṁ |
One should not cover [the bowl] with a porridge-lump; one should not present the bowl when food is not yet brought, nor [act improperly] with regard to chewable and edible [items];
sbh1.3:48.46
nānāstīrṇe pṛthivīpradeśe prapāte prābho bhā re pātraṁ sthāpayitavyaṁ, sāvadānaṁ ḥ piṇḍapātaṁ taḥ paribhoktavyaṁ vyaḥ |
one should not place the bowl on uncovered ground or in a precipitous, sloping place; the alms-food should be enjoyed carefully.
sbh1.3:48.47
no na o danena snāpikaṁ pratichā cchā dayitavyam |
One should not cover the soup with rice,
sbh1.3:48.48
na sūpikenodenamatittinikāyogamanuyuktena sūpikena, na odanena, na tittinikāyogamanuyuktena paribhoktavyaṁ, nātisthūlaṁ, nātiparīttaṁ parimaṇḍalayālopamālopayitavyaṁ ālopa ālopayitavyaḥ |
[nor] enjoy [it] with the soup engaged on the rice — [eating] not too thick, not too thin, with round morsels of food,
sbh1.3:48.49
na hastāvalehakaṁ kaḥ , na pātrāvalehakaṁ kaḥ |
not licking the hand, not licking the bowl,
sbh1.3:48.50
na hastasaṁthūnakaṁ kaḥ , na pātrasaṁthūnakaṁ kaḥ |
not slurping the hand, not slurping the bowl,
sbh1.3:48.51
na kabaḍacchedakaṁ kaḥ piṇḍapātaṁ taḥ paribhoktavyaṁ vyaḥ |
not breaking [up] the morsel — [thus] alms-food should be enjoyed.
sbh1.3:49.1
vihāragatenāpi me mayā tebhyaḥ kulebhyaḥ pratyāgatena, pratyāgatena pratiniṣkrāntena, divā vā, rātrau vā, prātipudgalike caṁkrame caṁkramitavyam |
‘And when I have returned to the dwelling from those families, having come and again gone out, by day or by night, I should walk on a private walking-path,
sbh1.3:49.2
na parakṣye parokṣe |
not on another’s,
sbh1.3:49.3
aviśvāsya, apravāritena, anuddiṣṭena, śrāntakāyena, klāntakāyena, nauddhatyābhinigṛhīte citte kuśalapakṣaprayuktenāmanasikārānugatenāntargatairindriyairabahirgatena mānasena, nātidrutaṁ, nāticapalaṁ, naikāntena gamanapratyāgamanapratisaṁyuktena, kālena kālaṁ gacchatā, kālena kālaṁ tiṣṭhatā, tathā sve vihāre |
[on one] not entrusted [to others], uninvited [by others to use], unallotted [to others]; with body unfatigued, body unwearied, with mind restrained from agitation, applied to the wholesome side, accompanied by attention, with faculties turned inward, with mind not going outward, not too swiftly, not too restlessly, not solely engaged in going-and-returning — going from time to time, standing from time to time. Thus in his own dwelling,
The phrase is read as manasikāra-anugata ('accompanied by attention'), not a-manasikāra ('non-attention'). In this full-awareness/alms-round context the practitioner is applied to the wholesome side with attention and inward-turned faculties; 'non-attention' would undermine the practice instruction.
sbh1.3:49.4
sve parigaṇe, svakyāṁ kuṭikāyāṁ, uddeśikāyāṁ, prātipaudgalikāyāṁ, na parapakṣyāyāmaviśvāsyāyāmapravāritāyāṁ, tathā maṁce vā, pīṭhe vā, tṛṇasaṁstarake ṇe vā, araṇye vā, vṛkṣamūle vā, śūnyāgāre vā, niṣattavyaṁ |
in his own group, in his own hut, in the appointed place, the private place — not in another’s place, [which is] not entrusted, uninvited — thus on couch or stool or grass-mat, in forest or at the foot of a tree or [in an] empty hut, one should sit,
sbh1.3:49.5
paya rya ṅkamābhujya, ṛjuṁ kāyaṁ praṇidhāya, pratimukhāṁ khīṁ smṛtimupasthāpya, rātryā madhsame yāme svapitavyam |
folding the legs cross-wise, having set the body upright, having established mindfulness to the fore. In the middle watch of the night one should sleep,
sbh1.3:49.6
divāpūrvakañca śca yāmaṁ maḥ kuśalapakṣeṇātināmayitavyaṁ vyaḥ |
and the day and first watch [of the night] should be passed on the wholesome side,
sbh1.3:49.7
evaṁ ca punaḥsvapitavyaṁ |
and thus one should sleep,
sbh1.3:50.1
ālokasaṁjñinā smṛtena saṁprajā nā nena, utthānasaṁjñāmeva manasi kurvatā, rātryā ḥ paścime yāme laghuladhveva prativibudhya, bhāṣye vā, svādhyāyakriyāyāṁ vā yogaḥ karaṇīyaḥ |
perceiving light, mindful, fully aware, attending to the perception of rising. In the last watch of the night, lightly very lightly waking, application should be made to study or recitation,
sbh1.3:50.2
prahāṇe vā, pratisaṁlayane, dharmacintāyāṁ, lokāyatāśca maṁtrā vivarjayitavyāḥ |
or to abandonment, retreat, [or] Dharma-reflection — and the worldly-doctrine mantras should be avoided,
sbh1.3:50.3
citrākṣaraścitrapadavyaṁjanā, anarthopasaṁhitā ye nābhijñāyai, na sambodhāya, na nirvāṇāya samvartante |
[those] of varied letters, varied words and phrases, connected with meaninglessness, which lead not to higher knowledge, not to awakening, not to nirvāṇa.
sbh1.3:50.4
ye vā punardharmāstathāgatabhāṣitā, gaṁbhīrā, gaṁbhīrābhāsā ḥ , śūnyatāpratisaṁyuktā, idaṁpratyayā yatā pratītyasamutpādānulomāste satkṛtyodgṛ dgra hītavyāḥ |
Those dharmas, however, spoken by the Tathāgata, deep, of deep appearance, connected with emptiness, conforming to this-conditioned-arising — those should be grasped with respect,
sbh1.3:50.5
dṛḍhañca, sthirañca, sūdgṛhītāśca, na nāśayitavyāḥ |
firmly, stably, well grasped, not destroyed,
sbh1.3:50.6
pratipattyā ḥ sampādanārthaṁ, na lābhasatkāra hetoḥ |
for the accomplishment of [meditative] practice, not for gain and honor;
sbh1.3:50.7
te ca punardharmā vacasā suparijitāḥ kartavyāḥ |
and those dharmas should be well mastered by voice.
sbh1.3:50.8
na ca saṁgaṇikayā atināmayitavyaṁ, na karmārāmatayā, na bhāṣyārāmatayā, kālena ca kālamupasthitayā smṛtyā vijñāsya ssa brahmacāriṇaḥ, ālapitavyāḥ, saṁlapitavyāḥ, pratisaṁbodhayitavyāḥ, paripṛcchana jātī yena ca bhavitavyaṁ, kiṁkuśalagaveṣiṇā |
And one should not pass time in company, in love of work, in love of speech; from time to time, with established mindfulness, having met co-religionists, one should speak with them, converse, awaken them — being of the inquiring sort, a seeker of what is wholesome,
sbh1.3:51.1
anupalaṁbhacittena, mitavādinā, yuktabhāṇinā, praśāntabhāṇinā ca |
with a non-accusatory mind, of measured speech, of suitable speech, of calm speech.
sbh1.3:52.1
pareṣāṁ dhā dha rmyāṁ kathāṁ kathayitvā tvakāmena tūṣṇīṁbhāvena ca ye pāpakā akuśalā vitarkā nātitarkayitavyāḥ |
Having spoken a Dharma-talk to others, in the desire for silence, evil unwholesome thoughts should not be excessively entertained,
sbh1.3:52.2
na cāyoniśodharmacintāyuktena |
and one should not be applied to unwise dharma-reflection.
sbh1.3:52.3
evaṁ rūpā anena bahavo dharmā udgṛhītā bhavanti |
Thus many dharmas of this kind have been grasped by him,
sbh1.3:52.4
dhṛtā, vacasā parijitā, manasā cānvīkṣitā ḥ |
retained, well mastered by voice, examined by mind,
sbh1.3:52.5
dṛṣṭyā suprativiṣṭāḥ viddhāḥ |
well penetrated by view.
sbh1.3:52.6
evaṁ bahuśruto bhavati |
Thus he becomes much-learned.
sbh1.3:52.7
kathamadhigantā bhavati |
How is he an attainer [of meditative states]?
The four-fold characterization of the good friend — much-learned (bahuśruta), attainer (samāpatti-lābhin), compassionate (kṛpālu), and of unwearying mind (akhinna-mānasa) — followed by patience, confidence, and speech-faculty at 51.25–52.11, yields the eight causes/aspects named at 52.12 and again at 53.51. This same eight-fold list is canonical for kalyāṇa-mitra across Yogācāra sources. The 'three wonders' at 51.12–13 (prātihārya: psychic power, mind-reading, admonition) match the standard Pāli list of iddhi/ādesanā/anuśāsanī-pāṭihāriya from DN 11 and elsewhere.
sbh1.3:52.8
lābhī bhavatyanityasaṁjñāyāḥ, anitye duḥkhasaṁjñāyā, duḥkhe ’nātmasaṁjñāyāḥ, āhāre pratikūlasaṁjñāyā, vilohitakasaṁjñāyā, vikṣiptakasaṁjñāyā, asthisaṁjñāyāḥ, śūnyatāpratyavekṣaṇasaṁjñāyā |
He becomes a gainer of the perception of impermanence, of the perception of suffering in the impermanent, of the perception of non-self in suffering, of the perception of food as repugnant, of the perception of the bloody, of the perception of the scattered, of the perception of the bone, [and] of the perception that reviews emptiness.
sbh1.3:52.9
lābhī bhavati |
He becomes a gainer
sbh1.3:52.10
prathamasya dhyānasya, dvitīyasya, tṛtīyasya, caturthasyākāśānantyā yatanavijñānānantyāyatanākiṁcanyāyatananaivasaṁjñānāsaṁjñāyatanasya, maitryāḥ, karuṇāyā, upekṣāyā, muditāyāḥ, srota āpattiphalasya, sakṛdāgāmiphalasyānāgāmiphalasya ca, dviviṣayasya, pūrvenivāsasya, divyasya śrotrasya, cyutyupapādasya, cetaḥparyāyasyārhattvasyāṣṭavimokṣadhyāyitvasya |
of the first dhyāna, [the] second, third, [and] fourth; of the sphere of infinity of space, infinity of consciousness, nothingness, neither-perception-nor-non-perception; of loving-kindness, compassion, [Damaged/Conjectural: sympathetic joy, and equanimity]; of the fruit of stream-entry, once-returning, non-returning, [and] of the two-sphere [super-]knowledge; of past-life knowledge, divine ear, decease-and-rebirth knowledge, mind-reading, arhatship, [and] the eight liberations of meditation.
The printed/root order of the brahmavihāras is maitrī, karuṇā, upekṣā, muditā. The English follows the standard meditative order maitrī, karuṇā, muditā, upekṣā but now marks the reordered pair conjectural/corrupt so the repair is not silent.
sbh1.3:52.11
śakto bhavati |
He is capable
sbh1.3:52.12
pratibalaśca pareṣāṁ tribhiḥ prātihāryairavavadituṁ |
and competent to admonish others by the three wonders:
sbh1.3:52.13
ṛddhiprātihāryeṇa, ādeśanāpratihāryeṇa, anuśāsanāprātihāryeṇa |
the wonder of psychic power, the wonder of mind-reading, the wonder of admonition.
sbh1.3:52.14
evamadhigantā bhavati |
Thus he is an attainer.
sbh1.3:52.15
kathamanukampako bhavati |
How is he compassionate?
sbh1.3:52.16
pareṣāmantike kāruṇiko bhavati |
Toward others he is full of compassion,
sbh1.3:52.17
dayāpannaḥ, arthakāmo bhavati |
full of pity, desirous of their welfare,
sbh1.3:52.18
hitakāmaḥ, sukhakāmaḥ, sparśakāmaḥ, yogakṣemakāmaḥ |
desirous of their benefit, [their] ease, [their] ease-of-contact, [their] security from bondage.
sbh1.3:52.19
evamanukampako bhavati |
Thus he is compassionate.
sbh1.3:52.20
kathamapratikhinnamānaso bhavati |
How is he of unwearying mind?
sbh1.3:52.21
va ra tisaṁdarśako bhavati |
He demonstrates [the teaching],
This is the standard teaching sequence sandarśaka, samādāpaka, samuttejaka, saṁpraharṣaka: one who demonstrates, exhorts, arouses, and gladdens. The prior 'shower of delight' misread saṁdarśaka as if it were connected with delight rather than showing/demonstrating.
sbh1.3:52.22
samādāpakaḥ, samuttejakaḥ saṁpraharṣakaḥ, ājñāsī catasṛṇāmparṣadāṁ, dharmadeśanāyai dakṣo bhavatyanalasa, utthānasampannaḥ |
an exhorter, a gladdener, an arouser, a knower of the four assemblies, skilled in Dharma-teaching, non-lazy, endowed with [the capacity for] rising,
sbh1.3:52.23
ārabdhavīrya, ārabdhavīrya jātīyaḥ |
of aroused energy, of aroused-energy nature.
sbh1.3:52.24
evamaparikhinnamānaso bhavati |
Thus he is of unwearying mind.
sbh1.3:52.25
kathaṁ kṣamāvān bhavati |
How is he patient?
sbh1.3:52.26
ākruṣṭo na pratyākrośati |
Abused, he does not abuse in return;
sbh1.3:52.27
roṣito na pratiroṣayati |
angered [against], he does not anger in return;
sbh1.3:52.28
vādito na prativādayati |
disputed against, he does not dispute [back];
sbh1.3:52.29
maṇḍito na pratimaṇḍayati |
disparaged, he does not disparage [in return].
sbh1.3:52.30
anena dānapratyarthādāna pratyādāna samo bhavati pragāḍheṣvapi bandhaneṣu, rodhaneṣu, tāḍaneṣu, tarjaneṣu, che cche daneṣu, ātmāparādhī bhavati |
Through such [reciprocal] giving-and-receiving-of-blows, he becomes equanimous; even in deep bondage, imprisonment, beating, threatening, [or] cutting, he [counts] himself at fault,
sbh1.3:52.31
karmavipākañca pratisarati |
and follows up the result of [his prior] action;
sbh1.3:52.32
na pareṣāmantike kupyati |
he is not angry with others,
sbh1.3:52.33
nāpyanuśayaṁ vahati |
nor does he bear a latent tendency [of anger].
sbh1.3:52.34
iti vimānito a pi, vivarṇito a pi, vijugupsito a pi, na vikṛmimāpadyate |
Thus disregarded, deprecated, [or] despised, he does not falter,
sbh1.3:52.35
nānyatrārthāyaiva cetayate kṣamaśca bhavati |
has no [other] intention than [for] the goal — and he is patient
sbh1.3:52.36
śīta syo ṣṇasya, jighatsāyā, pipāsāyā, daṁśamaśakavātātapasarīsṛpasampannānāṁ, parato duruktānāṁ, durāgatānāṁ nāṁ , patanapakṣāṇāṁ, śārīrikāṇāṁ vedanānāṁ, duḥkhānāṁ, tīvrāṇāṁ, kharāṇāṁ, kaṭukānāṁ, a mana āpānāṁ, prāṇahāriṇīnāṁ kṣamo bhavatyadhivāsanajātīyaḥ |
of cold, heat, hunger, thirst, gnats, mosquitoes, wind, sun, creeping creatures, [patient] of ill-spoken [words] from others, of ill-arrived [hardships], of falling-out [strife]; of bodily painful feelings — intense, harsh, sharp, disagreeable, life-threatening — he is patient, of accommodating nature.
sbh1.3:52.37
evaṁkṣamāvān bhavatya sā viti |
Thus is he patient.
sbh1.3:53.1
kathaṁ viśārado bhavati |
How is he confident?
sbh1.3:53.2
asaṁlīnacittaḥ |
Of unshrinking mind,
sbh1.3:53.3
parṣadi dharmaṁ deśayati |
in an assembly he teaches Dharma
sbh1.3:53.4
agadgadasvaraḥ, asaṁpramuṣitasmṛtipratibhānaḥ |
with unfaltering voice, without lapse of mindfulness or [of] inspiration;
sbh1.3:53.5
na cāsya śāradyahetoḥ, śāradyanidānaṁ bhayamvā ājapati, samāviśati, nāpi kampābhyāṁ svedo mucyate, rogaklamebhyo vā |
and fear arising from timidity does not arise in him, does not come upon him; nor does sweat from trembling pour from him, except from illness or fatigue.
sbh1.3:53.6
evaṁ viśārado bhavati |
Thus he is confident.
sbh1.3:53.7
kathaṁ vākkaraṇenopeto bhavati |
How is he endowed with the speech-faculty?
sbh1.3:53.8
pauryā ryayā vācā samanvāgato bhavati |
He is endowed with noble speech:
sbh1.3:53.9
valgūnyavispa valgunyā, vispa ṣṭayā, vijñeyayā, śravaṇīyayā, apratikūlayā, aniśritayā, a paryāptayā |
sweet, articulate, clear, pleasing to hear, agreeable, non-clinging, sufficient.
sbh1.3:53.10
evamvākkaraṇenopeto bhavati |
Thus he is endowed with the speech-faculty,
sbh1.3:53.11
kalyāṇavādī |
speaking well.
sbh1.3:53.12
sa ebhiraṣṭābhiḥ kāraṇaiḥ samanvāgataścodako bhavati |
He, endowed with these eight causes, becomes an admonisher,
sbh1.3:53.13
smārakaḥ, avavādakaḥ, anuśāsako, dharma deśakaḥ |
a reminder, an advisor, an instructor, [and] a Dharma-teacher.
sbh1.3:54.1
kathaṁ codako bhavati |
How is he an admonisher?
sbh1.3:54.2
yadutādhiśīle ca śīlavipattyā, adhyācāre ācāravipattyā, dṛṣṭena, śrutena, pariśaṁkayā codayati |
Namely, [for failure] in higher virtue by [pointing out] virtue-failure, in misconduct by [pointing out] conduct-failure, he admonishes by what is seen, by what is heard, by what is suspected,
sbh1.3:54.3
bhūtena, nābhūtena, kālena nākālenārthopasaṁhitena nānā na rthopasaṁhitena, ślakṣṇena, na paruṣeṇa, mitravattayā, na dveṣāntareṇa |
by what is real, not by what is unreal; in [proper] time, not out of time; connected with meaning, not unconnected with meaning; gently, not harshly; with friendliness, not with hostility.
sbh1.3:54.4
evaṁ codako bhavati |
Thus he is an admonisher.
sbh1.3:54.5
kathaṁ smārako bhavati |
How is he a reminder?
sbh1.3:54.6
āpattimvā smārayati |
He reminds [the other] of a transgression,
sbh1.3:54.7
dharmaṁ cā vā rthamvā |
or of the Dharma, or of its meaning.
sbh1.3:54.8
kathamāpattiṁ smārayati |
How does he remind of a transgression?
sbh1.3:54.9
yathāpi tadāpatti madhyāpadyamānaḥ smarati |
When someone who has fallen into a transgression [no longer] remembers [it],
sbh1.3:54.10
tamenaṁ smārayati |
he reminds him:
sbh1.3:54.11
āyuṣmannamuṣmindeśe, amuṣmin, vastuni, amuṣmin kāle, evaṁ rūpaṁ, caivaṁ kālamāpanna ityevamāpattiṁ smārayati |
‘Friend, in such a place, in such a matter, in such a manner, at such a time, you fell [into transgression].’ Thus he reminds of the transgression.
sbh1.3:54.12
kathaṁ dharmaṁ smārayati |
How does he remind of Dharma?
sbh1.3:54.13
yathāpi tacchrutānudgṛhītān dharmānekākī smarati |
When someone, alone, [tries to] recall the dharmas [previously] heard, grasped —
sbh1.3:54.14
smartumicchati |
wishes to recall —
sbh1.3:54.15
tadyathā sūtraṁ, geyaṁ, vyākaraṇamiti vistareṇa pūrvavanna smarati |
namely sūtra, geya, vyākaraṇa, [as elaborated] above — [but] does not recall [them],
sbh1.3:54.16
tamena naṁ smārayati |
he reminds him,
sbh1.3:54.17
utsmāraṇikāmvāsyānuprayacchati |
gives him the prompting,
sbh1.3:54.18
āpṛcchanaparipṛcchanikāṁ vā, evaṁ dharmāna smārayati |
or the asking-and-inquiring [that draws it out]. Thus he reminds of dharmas.
sbh1.3:54.19
kathaṁ dharma arthaṁ smārayati |
How does he remind of the meaning of Dharma?
sbh1.3:54.20
yathāpi nathāṁ cārthān vismārayati vismarati |
When someone forgets the meanings,
sbh1.3:54.21
tamenaṁ smārayati |
he reminds him,
sbh1.3:54.22
punarapi pratinavīkaroti |
refreshes [his memory],
sbh1.3:54.23
uttānī karoti |
makes [the meaning] manifest,
sbh1.3:54.24
deśayati, saṁprakāśayati |
teaches, proclaims;
sbh1.3:54.25
yaccāpi kuśalamarthopasaṁhitaṁ, brahmacaryopasaṁhitaṁ |
and what is wholesome, connected with meaning, connected with the holy life,
sbh1.3:54.26
ciraka kṛ taṁ, cirabhāṣitamapyanusmārayitā bhavati |
long since done, long since spoken — of that too he reminds.
sbh1.3:54.27
evaṁ smārako bhavati |
Thus he is a reminder.
sbh1.3:54.28
kathamavavādako bhavati |
How is he an advisor?
sbh1.3:54.29
prāvivekye pratisaṁlayane, yoge, manasikāra re , śamathavipaśyanāyāṁ kālena kālamānulomikaṁ avavādaṁ pravarttayati |
In [matters of] seclusion, retreat, yoga, mental engagement, [and] calm-and-insight, from time to time he sets in motion appropriate admonition,
sbh1.3:54.30
kālena ca kālaṁ tatpratisaṁyuktāṁ kathāṁ karoti |
and from time to time engages in connected talk —
sbh1.3:54.31
tadyathā cetovinivaraṇasāṁpreyagāminīṁ śīlakathāmvā, prajñākathāmvā, vimuktikathāmvā, alpecchakathāmvā, vimuktijñānadarśanakathāmvā, saṁtuṣṭikathāmvā, prahāṇakathāṁ, virāgakathāṁ, nirodhakathāṁ, apacayakathāṁ, asaṁsargakathāṁ, idaṁpratyaya tā pra tītyasamutpādānulomān māṁ kathāṁ karoti |
namely, talk that leads to the dispelling of hindrances of the mind: virtue-talk, wisdom-talk, liberation-talk, talk of few-desires, talk of the knowing-and-seeing of liberation, contentment-talk, abandonment-talk, dispassion-talk, cessation-talk, decrease-talk, non-mixing-talk, [and] this-conditioned-arising-conforming talk.
The list of twelve kathā-vastu (topics of discourse) — virtue-talk, wisdom-talk, liberation-talk, few-desires-talk, knowing-and-seeing-of-liberation-talk, contentment-talk, abandonment-talk, dispassion-talk, cessation-talk, decrease-talk [apacaya], non-mixing-talk [asaṁsarga], pratītyasamutpāda-conforming-talk — is the canonical dvādaśa-kathā-vastu of Yogācāra abhidharma. Cf. the ten-fold kathā-vatthu list in the Pāli Anguttara (AN 10.69).
sbh1.3:54.32
evamavavādako bhavati |
Thus he is an advisor.
sbh1.3:54.33
kathamanuśāsako bhavati |
How is he an instructor?
sbh1.3:54.34
dharmeṇa, vinayena, samanuśāstuḥ śāsane ācāryo vā bhavati |
Being the teacher in the dispensation of the well-instructed Teacher [in matters of] Dharma and Vinaya,
sbh1.3:54.35
upādhyāyo vā, sahadhārmiko vā, gururvā, gurusthānīyo vā, anyatamānyatamasminnadhikaraṇe atisṛtaṁ, vyatikrāntaṁ viditvā |
or [being] a preceptor, or a co-religionist, or a guru, or a person occupying the place of a guru — in any matter of [the disciple’s] overstepping or transgression, knowing of it,
sbh1.3:54.36
kālena kālamavasādayati |
from time to time he chastens [him],
sbh1.3:54.37
daṇḍakarmama rmā nuprayacchati |
[and] prescribes a punishment,
sbh1.3:54.38
praṇāmayati cainaṁ |
and reproaches him;
sbh1.3:54.39
punarapi ca dharmeṇa samayepratisaṁstarasāmīcīsaṁjñaptiṁ pratigṛhaṇāti |
and again, by Dharma, at the [proper] time he accepts [his] conciliation, propriety, [and] resolution;
sbh1.3:54.40
saṁrohakaśca bhavati |
and he is a healer
sbh1.3:54.41
karaṇīye cākaraṇīye bhāvyācārānadhyācārān adhyācīrṇe carite anadhyācīrṇe carite ca śāstyanuśāstyevamanuśāsako bhavati |
in what should be done and not done, in good conduct and misconduct, in conducted and unconducted [matters] — he instructs and disciplines. Thus he is an instructor.
sbh1.3:54.42
kathaṁ ca dharmadeśako bhavati |
How is he a Dharma-teacher?
sbh1.3:54.43
kālena kālaṁ pūrvakālakaraṇīyāṁ kathāṁ karoti |
From time to time he gives the preliminary-action talk —
sbh1.3:54.44
tadyathā dānakathāṁ, śīlakathāṁ, svargakathāṁ, kāmeṣvādīnava niḥsaraṇaṁ, vyavadānapakṣāndharmān vistareṇa saṁprakāśayati |
namely, [the talks on] generosity, virtue, heaven, the danger in sense-pleasures, the escape, [and on] the dharmas on the side of purification, [all] of which he proclaims in detail.
sbh1.3:54.45
kālena kālaṁ caturāryasatyapratisaṁyuktāṁ kathāṁ kathayati |
From time to time he gives talk connected with the four noble truths,
sbh1.3:54.46
duḥkhaṁ vā ārabhya, samudayamvā, nirodhamvā, phalamvā, saparipākāya sattvasaṁkleśāya vā, satva ttva vyavadānāya vā, saddharmasya vā cirasthitaye, yuktaiḥ padavyaṁjanaiḥ, sahitairānulomikairānucchavikairaupāyikaiḥ, pratirūpaiḥ, pradakṣiṇairnipakasyāṁgasaṁbhāraistāṁ ca punaḥ kathāṁ kālena karoti |
[talk] taking up suffering, origin, cessation, [and Damaged/Conjectural: the path] — for the maturation of beings, for their de-defilement, for their purification, for the long endurance of the true Dharma — with suitable words and letters, accompanied [by the right context], conforming [to the audience], conforming to the occasion, suitable, befitting, auspicious, equipped with the limbs and requisites of alert mindfulness; and he gives that talk at the [proper] time,
The line introduces talk connected with the four noble truths but the extracted root reads phalam vā after suffering, origin, and cessation. Context requires the fourth truth, mārga ('path'); English marks the repair as conjectural/corrupt rather than silently translating the impossible 'fruit' as the fourth truth.
sbh1.3:54.47
satkṛtyānusandhimanupatitāṁ |
respectfully, in due sequence,
sbh1.3:54.48
harṣayan, rocayannanu yannu tsāhayannanavasādayaṁśca yuktāṁ, sahitāṁ, avyavakīrṇāṁ, yathādhārmikīṁ, yathāparṣanmaitracitto, hitacittaḥ, anukampācittaḥ |
gladdening, pleasing, encouraging, not depressing — suitable, accompanied [appropriately], unscattered, in accord with Dharma, in accord with the assembly, of friendly mind, beneficial mind, compassionate mind,
sbh1.3:54.49
aniśrito lābhasatkāraślokena cātmānamutkarṣayati |
not depending on gain, honor, [or] praise; he does not exalt himself,
sbh1.3:54.50
na parānpaṁsa paṁsa yatyevaṁ dharmadeśako bhavati |
[and] does not deprecate others. Thus he is a Dharma-teacher.
sbh1.3:54.51
yaścaibhiraṣṭābhi ḥ samanvāgato raṁgaiḥ samanvāgato bhavati |
He who is endowed with these eight factors
sbh1.3:54.52
evaṁ ca kālena kālaṁ codako bhavati, smārakaḥ |
and is thus from time to time an admonisher, reminder,
sbh1.3:54.53
avavādānuśāsakastasmāt kalyāṇamitra m ityucyate |
advisor, [and] instructor — therefore he is called a good friend [kalyāṇa-mitra].
sbh1.3:54.54
ayaṁ tāvat kalyāṇamitratāyā vistaravibhāgaḥ |
This much is the detailed division of association with good friends [kalyāṇa-mitratā].
sbh1.3:54.55
samāsārthaḥ punaḥ katamaḥ |
What is the summary meaning?
sbh1.3:54.56
sa cedayaṁ mitrasuhṛdanukampakaḥ ādita eva hitakāmo bhavati |
If this friend, helper, compassionate one is, from the start, desirous of [the disciple’s] welfare
sbh1.3:54.57
sukhakāmaśca |
and desirous of [his] ease,
sbh1.3:54.58
tacca punarhitasukhaṁ yathābhūtaṁ prajānāti |
and knows that very welfare-and-ease as it truly is,
sbh1.3:54.59
aviparyasto bhavatyaviparītadṛṣṭiḥ, pratibalaśca bhavatyupāyakuśalaḥ |
[being] unperverted, of unperverted view, capable and skilled in means —
sbh1.3:54.60
yadutāsyaiva hitasukhasya samudāgamāyosaṁhārāya dakṣaśca bhavatyanalasa, utthānasampanna, ārabdhavīryajātīyaḥ |
namely, for the accomplishment of that welfare-and-ease, for its conferral — and is skilled, not lazy, endowed with [the capacity for] rising, of aroused-energy nature,
sbh1.3:54.61
yaduta tameva hitasukhopasaṁhāramārabhya |
namely, taking up that very conferral of welfare-and-ease,
sbh1.3:54.62
ebhiścaturbhiḥ kāraṇaiḥ sarvākāraparipūrṇaḥ ṁ |
[then,] wholly perfect by these four causes,
sbh1.3:54.63
samāsataḥ kalyāṇamitro traṁ veditavyaḥ vyam |
he is in brief to be understood as the good friend.
sbh1.3:54.64
ayaṁ ca punaḥ kalyāṇamitratāyāḥ samāsārthaḥ |
And this is the summary meaning of association with good friends.
sbh1.3:55.1
yaśca pūrvo vistaravibhāgaḥ, yaścāyaṁ samāsārtha iyamucyate kalyāṇamitratā |
What was previously [given as] the detailed division, and what [is here] the summary meaning — this is called association with good friends.
sbh1.3:56.1
saddharmaśravaṇacintanā katamā |
What is hearing-and-reflection on the true dharma?
sbh1.3:56.2
saddharma ucyate buddhaiśca buddhaśrāvakaiśca sadbhiḥ samyaggataiḥ satpuruṣairākhyātaḥ |
By ‘true dharma’ is meant: that which has been declared by the Buddhas and the disciples of Buddhas — the rightly-gone good persons —
sbh1.3:56.3
deśita uttāno vivṛtaḥ |
taught, set forth, opened up,
sbh1.3:56.4
saṁprakāśitaḥ |
and proclaimed.
sbh1.3:56.5
sa punaḥ katamaḥ |
What, again, is that?
sbh1.3:56.6
tadyathā sūtraṁ, geyaṁ, vyākaraṇamiti vistareṇa pūrvavat |
Namely sūtra, geya, vyākaraṇa, [and the rest of the twelve, as elaborated above].
sbh1.3:56.7
dvādaśāṁgavacogataṁ saddharma ityucyate |
The twelvefold word-genre is called true dharma.
sbh1.3:56.8
tatra sūtraṁ katamat |
What is sūtra?
sbh1.3:56.9
yattatra tatra bhagavatā tāṁstān tāni tāni vineyācaritāni cārabhya skandhapratisaṁyuktā vā kathā kṛtā, dhātupratisaṁyuktāvā kathā kṛtā, dhātusaṁgaṇasaṁyuktā vā, āyatanapratisaṁyuktā vā |
Whatever talks were given by the Blessed One, here and there, taking up the conduct of various trainable persons — talks connected with the aggregates, with the elements, with combinations of elements, with the sense-bases,
sbh1.3:56.10
pratītyasamutpādapratisaṁyuktā vā, āhārasatyasthiti prati saṁyuktā vā, śrāvakapratyekabuddhatathāgatapratisaṁyuktā vā |
with dependent origination, with food and truth, or with disciples, solitary buddhas, and tathāgatas;
sbh1.3:56.11
smṛtyupasthānasamyakprahāṇadhi rdhi pādendriyabalabodhyaṁgamārgāṁgapratisaṁyuktā |
or connected with the foundations of mindfulness, the right exertions, the bases of psychic power, the faculties, the powers, the awakening-factors, the factors of the path;
sbh1.3:56.12
aśubhā, ānāpānasmṛtiśikṣāvetyaprasādapratisaṁyuktā kathā kṛtā |
or talks given on unattractiveness, on mindfulness of breathing, on the training, on serene clear faith —
sbh1.3:56.13
sā ca kathā saṁgītikāraiḥ parigṛhya śāsanacirasthitaye, yathā yogamanupūrveṇa racitā, anupūrveṇa samāyuktā |
those very talks, taken up by the council-makers for the long endurance of the teaching and arranged in due order, joined in due order,
sbh1.3:56.14
pratirūpairnāmakāyapadavyaṁjanakāyairyaduta teṣāṁ teṣāmarthānāṁ sūcanāyai kuśalānāmarthopasaṁhitānāṁ buddhacaryopasaṁhitānāmidamucyate sūtraṁ |
with appropriate sequences of names, words, and letters — that is, in such a way as to indicate the relevant meanings, which are wholesome, meaningful, and joined with the practice of the Buddha — these are called sūtra.
sbh1.3:56.15
geyaṁ katamat |
What is geya?
sbh1.3:56.16
yasyānte paryavasāne gāthā abhigītā, yacca sūtraṁ neyārthamidamucyate geyaṁ |
[A discourse] at whose conclusion verses are sung [in summary], together with sūtras whose meaning still requires interpretation (neyārtha) — this is called geya.
geya (sung-verse) and vyākaraṇa (predictive teaching) are paired in 56.16–20 with two contrasting kinds of sūtra: neyārtha (meaning-to-be-interpreted) sūtras paired with geya, and nītārtha (definitive-meaning) sūtras paired with vyākaraṇa. The pairing is structural and tradition-recognized in the twelve-aṅga schema; preserved here in brackets for visibility.
sbh1.3:56.17
vyākaraṇaṁ katamat |
What is vyākaraṇa?
sbh1.3:56.18
yasmiṁchrāvake yasmiñchrāvake bhyo a bhyatītakālagato tau upapattau vyākriyate |
[A discourse] in which, regarding disciples who have passed away, their [next] rebirth is predicted,
sbh1.3:56.19
yacca sūtraṁ nītārthamidamucyate |
together with sūtras whose meaning is final (nītārtha) — this is called
sbh1.3:56.20
vyākaraṇaṁ |
vyākaraṇa.
sbh1.3:56.21
gāthā katamā |
What is gāthā?
sbh1.3:56.22
yā na gadyena bhāṣitā |
What is not spoken in prose,
sbh1.3:56.23
apitu pādopanibandhena dvipadā vā, tripadā vā, catuṣpadā vā, paṁcapadā vā, ṣaṭpadā vā iyamucyate gāthā |
but composed in metrical lines — of two, three, four, five, or six feet — this is called gāthā.
sbh1.3:57.1
udānā katamā |
What is udāna?
sbh1.3:57.2
yatpudgalasya nāma gotramaparikīrtayitvā kīrttya, uddiśya bhāṣitamāyatyāmvā saddharmasthitaye, śāsanasthitaye ca |
